# Tenant-derived runtimes

Status: design for owner review, revision 78, 2026-11-16. Revision 78 applies the kind and field names of [`pod-lifecycle-controller-design.md`](pod-lifecycle-controller-design.md) revision 3, which the owner accepted on 2026-11-16 as its O3, including the merge of the template and warm pool resources into one `PodSet`. It changes no rule. Revision 77 applies the owner's answer of 2026-11-15, "yes", to removing the tool-capability fields under [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md) revision 3: a tenant-derived runtime write that names `capabilityInferenceMode` or `toolCapabilityOverrides` is refused with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field. Revision 76 applies the instruction of [`rest-only-client-api-design.md`](rest-only-client-api-design.md) for the agent card: Lenny no longer generates a card from a derived runtime's `agentInterface`, which stays as an override that discovery publishes and the A2A adapter reads. Revision 75 records the owner's later answers of 2026-11-14, "Ok on the rest", as decision 44, which confirm every choice revision 73 left for the owner's review: the upload lifecycle, with garbage collection of an upload unreferenced for 7 days, deletion with the derived runtime and with the tenant, and the per-tenant upload limit as a grant field; a single stored copy of each upload; the `inline` caps; an `artifact` entry of `digest` and `destPath` alone, placed whole and never extracted; the write `Warning`s on an operator runtime's default clones at a floating ref; and the store group as the adapter's primary group. They also confirm revision 74's handling of a lowered cap under DR-6, and settle that no deployment ceiling bounds a grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes`. No rule changes. Revision 74 applies the owner's answers of 2026-11-14 to the confirmation list of the question 17 write-up, recorded as decision 43, and changes these rules: a tenant's `PUT` may carry a `platform`-declared `gitRepository` asset unchanged, and only a change to it is refused; a tenant-derived runtime may set `prestageTimeoutSeconds`, the timeout of its warm-up checkouts, within the grant entry's new `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`; the grant gains `maxAssetUploadBytes`, the tenant's upload limit; and each grant entry gains `maxDefaultGitClones`, which the new deployment ceiling `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling` bounds. The owner confirmed the rules that a `platform`-declared asset's store feeds the tenant-owned chunk set, that a tenant's `gitRepository` asset needs `allowGitClones` and DR-3, that a warm-up checkout that fails its tree-size check or times out skips the entry, with the write `Warning` on large staged trees, and that `inline` and `artifact` assets need the grant flag `allowAssets`. Revision 73 applies the independent review of revision 72's write-up of question 17, and changes these rules: a `gitRepository` asset that a `platform-admin` writes on a tenant-derived runtime is `platform`-declared and keeps decision 35's protections, and a tenant's `PUT` cannot remove or change it; a tenant's `gitRepository` asset needs `allowGitClones` and DR-3; a tenant's `inline` and `artifact` assets need the new grant flag `allowAssets` and DR-3; an upload is stored once, as packs of the tenant-owned assets set, under a per-runtime digest, a per-tenant quota, and garbage collection of unreferenced uploads; `inline` content has its own cap; an `artifact` entry carries `digest` and `destPath` alone and is placed whole; an operator runtime's default clones draw `Warning`s and append on platform-derived runtimes; a warm-up checkout that fails its tree-size check or times out skips the entry; and a store shared by an operator entry and a tenant entry feeds the tenant-owned chunk set. Several of these were new choices, and "Revision notes" marks each as confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-14 under decision 43 or 44. Revision 72 applies the owner's model of 2026-11-14 for shared assets and warm stores, settled as decision 42 in the owner's words "sharedAssets should define any git stores to warm up. workspaceDefaults shouldn't also do that through `warm: true`", and changes these rules. A `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets` is the only way to declare a warm store, with optional `prestage: true` in place of the operator entry's target `path`, and a staged tree is adopted by the first `gitClone` source of its repository in the session's workspace, at that source's `path`. A tenant-derived runtime may carry `sharedAssets` on its owned pool: `inline` and `artifact` files under the operator's `allowAssets`, and `gitRepository` assets, which replace warm default clones, cache-only entries with `pool.warmCaches`, and the platform admin's `warmRepositories`. `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` loses its `warm` field and is allowed on operator runtimes. "Warm clones" under DR-9 is renamed "Warm stores", "Tenant shared assets" is new, and DR-11 is renamed "Shared assets and warm stores". Revision 71 follows [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) revision 8: the store group is the primary group of both the adapter and `lenny-store-fill`, and the disk watch's threshold is a chart value. Revision 70 applies the owner's answers of 2026-11-13 to the consolidated list of open items across the designs: decision 41 is settled as the refusal with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE`, `lenny-store-fill` writes with a dedicated store group, each resource class adds its own ephemeral-storage request to the volume sum, and a write that names a removed field is refused by name. Revision 69 applies the check pass of the cross-design review of 2026-11-13: one HTTP status for the placement codes, every pod's storage request, the pristine-tree modes and the adapter's deletion, the contribution of a URL two declarers name, and decision 41, which it recorded as open. Revision 68 applies the fix pass that followed the cross-design review of 2026-11-13: the store-fill permissions that PL-5 of [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) states, the wording of a never-launched pod, and the removal of LLM credential pools and direct delivery mode under [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md). Revision 67 is this design's part of the cross-design review of 2026-11-13, which applies the owner's decisions of that day listed after "Decisions": the removal of `type: mcp` runtimes and service mode under [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) (prefix AR-), with its O8 `registered` check, which DR-4 now owns; the platform chunk sets of [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md) decision D1, for which the session row now stores `coveredStores`; the `lenny-store-fill` container of the egress design's decision 61; per-slot `netagent` scopes under the egress design's decision 60; and the optional target `path` on an operator's `gitRepository` entry. Revision 66 was this design's part of the consistency pass of 2026-11-12. It reads against [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md), [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md), [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md), [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md), [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md), [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md), [`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md), and [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) as the review of 2026-11-13 leaves them. Nothing here is built or staged, so no test of the design has run. One spike has run: the warm clone spike of 2026-11-05, which used a stand-in for `netagent` and is recorded under "Not verified" in DR-9. Statements about the tree were checked by reading the code, the chart, the migrations, and the specification. It reads against [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) revision 93, [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) revision 42, [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) revision 59, [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) revision 40, [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) revision 45, [`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md) revision 19, [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) revision 22, and [`rest-only-client-api-design.md`](rest-only-client-api-design.md) revision 6. The owner has settled every decision under "Decisions". Proposals carry the label DR-n, and defects found here carry the label DF-n. The DF-n defects are filed in `BUILD-GAPS.md` at the repository root, and the table under "Defects found while verifying" names each F-id. "Review history" at the end records how the design reached this form.

## Problem

§15.1 **REST API** reserves `POST /v1/admin/runtimes` and `POST /v1/admin/pools` to `platform-admin`. A `tenant-admin` can only update records its tenant holds an access row on. The owner wants a tenant admin able to create derived runtimes.

The immediate need comes from the rule [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`, "Pods with no per-session policy"](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#effective-policy), and from [that design's decision 15](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions). A pod with several slots refuses a session whose environment has anything of its own wherever `netagent` cannot attribute a connection to its slot, which is the fallback of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions) decision 60, so an environment that needs such a runtime there uses a derived runtime with `oneSessionPerPod: true`. A tenant whose security team keeps its sessions off shared pods (`placement.allowSharedPod: false` under [TSS-6](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-6-placement-settings)) needs the same. Today each such environment needs an operator's action, and that way does not work even for an operator (see "Current state").

The capability is general. A tenant admin can also add default workspace files, setup commands, an agent interface, labels, and narrower limits to a runtime the platform has made available.

## Scope

This design covers tenant-created derived runtimes, the pools they need, the grant that turns the capability on, the fields a tenant can set, default git clones, capacity reservation, the base runtime lifecycle, and the interactions with environments, runtime routes and revisions, delegation, and security oversight.

These are out of scope:

- A tenant registering its own image or a standalone runtime.
- Sharing a tenant-derived runtime with another tenant, or deriving from one.
- Egress ceilings, overlays, and per-class gates, which [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) owns, security roles and the tenant security record, which [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) owns, and environment scoping, which [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) owns.

## Terms

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| Tenant-derived runtime | A derived runtime that a `tenant-admin` creates under a derivation grant. It is stored in a tenant-scoped table, named under the tenant's name prefix, served by its own owned pool, and visible to its tenant alone. See DR-4. |
| Platform-derived runtime | A derived runtime that a `platform-admin` registers through `POST /v1/admin/runtimes`, stored with the platform's runtime records as today. |
| Base runtime, or base | The runtime a derived runtime names in `baseRuntime`. For a tenant-derived runtime it is a platform-written standalone runtime that a grant entry lists. |
| Derivation grant, or grant | The per-tenant record a `platform-admin` writes through `PUT /v1/admin/tenants/{id}/derivation-grant`. It holds the tenant's name prefix, its budgets `maxRuntimes` and `maxOwnedPods`, its upload limit `maxAssetUploadBytes`, and its grant entries. See "Who turns it on". |
| Grant entry, or base entry | One item of a grant's `bases` list. It names a base the tenant may derive from, the template pool, the resource classes, the grant flags, the warm store and reservation allowances, the limit on default clones and `gitRepository` assets, the bound on the warm-up checkout timeout, `ownedOverlays`, and an optional `retireAt`. |
| Grant flags | `allowWorkspaceFiles`, `allowSetupCommands`, `allowGitClones`, `allowGitCloneWrites`, and `allowAssets` on a grant entry. Each is off by default, and each admits one kind of tenant default. |
| Template pool | A platform pool backed by the base, which a grant entry names. An owned pool copies from it every setting that needs a platform decision. See "Template copy". |
| Owned pool | The pool created with a tenant-derived runtime, under the same name. Its row carries `ownerTenantId`, and its pods serve the owning tenant alone. See DR-5. |
| Name prefix (`namePrefix`) | A string the platform assigns to each granted tenant. A tenant-derived runtime is named `<namePrefix>--<suffix>`, such as `acme--chat-payments`, and `--` is reserved for this use. See "Names". |
| Tenant view | The read interface on `runtimestore` that takes a tenant: `Resolve(ctx, tenantID, name)` and `List(ctx, tenantID)`. It sees the platform runtimes the tenant holds `runtime_tenant_access` on and the tenant's own runtimes. See "Reading". |
| Effective runtime | The result of merging a tenant-derived runtime with its live base and its current grant entry, which the tenant view computes on each resolve. See DR-6. |
| `baseStatus` | What `GET /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}` reports about the base. `retiring` carries a retirement date. `retired` means the date has passed. `incompatible` means the live base or grant entry no longer admits the runtime's settings. Under `retired` and `incompatible`, new sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`. |
| Tenant defaults | Default files, default setup commands, and default git clones that a tenant writes in a tenant-derived runtime's `workspaceDefaults`. Every session on the runtime receives them, and each kind is treated as code execution at the slot UID. See "Tenant defaults". |
| Default clone | An entry of `workspaceDefaults.gitClones`: a git repository that every session on the runtime clones. Its `write` field decides whether a session can push to it: `none`, the default, adds no push access, `clientOptIn` lets a session push when its plan sets `writeDefaultClones: true`, and `always` lets every session on the runtime push within the clone's `allowedRefPatterns`. See DR-9 and "Write scope". |
| Store, fill, and `gitRepository` asset | A `gitRepository` entry of a runtime's `sharedAssets` is the only way to declare a warm store. Before a pod reports ready, the `lenny-store-fill` container fills a store for each entry on the pool's warm list: a read-only bare repository holding the objects of one commit. A session's `gitClone` source of that repository then fetches only the commits the store lacks. An operator declares public entries on a Runtime, and a tenant admin declares entries on a tenant-derived runtime, which this document calls `gitRepository` assets. See "Warm stores". In this document a bare "store" means a warm store. The runtime store interface (`runtimestore`), a pool's row in `pools`, and the git mirror's per-tenant caches are always named as such. |
| Warm list | The one list of repository stores that a pool's pods fill before they report ready, kept per pool and, once [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) lands, per pool and runtime revision, because a revision's `sharedAssets` contributes entries ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)). Every pre-filled store on a pod comes from its pod set's list. Each entry records the repository URL, the ref (a branch, a tag, or a full commit), the declarer, and whether it carries `prestage: true`. One rule set governs every entry, whoever declared it. The PoolScalingController renders the list on `PodSet.spec.warmClones` for every pool, and the decision service and the Token Service read it from the pool row. See "Warm list" under "Warm stores". |
| Declarer | Who put an entry on a warm list: `platform` for a public `gitRepository` entry on a base Runtime, and for a `gitRepository` asset of a tenant-derived runtime the role that wrote it, `tenant` for a tenant admin's asset and `platform` for a `platform-admin`'s asset, which keeps the protections decision 35 gives an operator's entry. |
| Tenant shared assets | The `sharedAssets` entries of a tenant-derived runtime, which render on its owned pool alone: `inline` and `artifact` files under `/lenny/assets`, appended to the base's, and `gitRepository` assets. See "Tenant shared assets" under DR-9. |
| Prestaged tree | The working tree that the warm-up checkout builds in each idle slot for a warm-list entry with `prestage: true`. At bind the first `gitClone` source of the session's workspace that the entry's store serves adopts it by rename to that source's `path`. See "Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild" under "Warm stores". |
| `coveredStores` and `withheldStores` | Fields of a claim request, which the gateway also stores on the session row. `coveredStores` lists the stores of the tenant's entries whose read checks the session passed, plus the stores of public platform entries. `withheldStores` lists the stores whose read checks the session failed and that the adapter deletes for that session. A pod holding a store outside both lists is refused. The stored `coveredStores` also decides which platform chunk sets the session's checkpoints and exports may reference. See "Who reaches a store" and "Withheld stores". |
| Withheld store | A store whose read checks a session failed on a pod that serves that session alone. The adapter deletes it from the pod before anything runs for the session, the session proceeds without it, and the pod drains when the session ends. See "Withheld stores" under "Warm stores". |
| `oneSessionPerPod` | A tenant-derived runtime field. `true` gives the owned pool one session per pod and no recycling, whatever the template pool sets, and the field cannot return to `false`. See "`oneSessionPerPod` and `sessionPolicy`". |
| Served pod (`lenny.dev/served`) | A pod of an owned pool that has received a session. The gateway stamps the label at first assignment. See "Pods". |
| T, the tenant's charge | The pod figure checked against the grant's `maxOwnedPods`: the sum across the tenant's owned pools of `max(inUse, reservedPods) + warmCount + unusable`, where `inUse` counts a pool's pods in the `claimed` and `reserved` phases and `unusable` counts those in `draining` and `failed`, apart from a pod whose recorded fill failure is on a platform-declared warm-list entry. See "Bounds". The letter T means this charge alone in this document. |
| Reservation, floor, and allowance | A reservation keeps a minimum number of an owned pool's pods in existence before any session. The pool's `reservedPods` is its floor. A grant entry's `maxReservedPods` is the tenant's allowance per resource class. See DR-10. The word "reserved" also names a pod phase, which "Terms from other designs and the platform" defines, and that phase is unrelated to a reservation. |
| Marked pod | An `AgentPod` the WarmPoolController creates with `spec.reserved` set, whose pod takes the `lenny-reserved` priority class and counts against the reserved quota. See "Which pods are reserved". |
| `warm_stop_reasons` | A column on an owned pool row that lists why the pool must stop warming, such as a tenant suspension, a retirement, an emergency, or a security forbid. A non-empty value drains the pool. See "Who drains". |
| Retirement and emergency retirement | A retirement ends a base's use for tenant-derived runtimes after a notice period, 30 days by default. An emergency retirement takes effect at once, for a compromised image. See "Retirement" under DR-6. |
| `runtime.base_changed` | The audit event a platform write emits to each tenant whose tenant-derived runtime or stored overlay it changes. See "Notice" under DR-6. |
| DF-n and F-n.n.n | A DF-n is a defect in today's tree that verifying this design found. Each is filed in `BUILD-GAPS.md` at the repository root under an F-n.n.n identifier, the form every finding filed there takes. See "Defects found while verifying". |
| Draft 0111 | Draft proposal 0111, on the branch `draft/0111-warm-workspace`, which warms platform-written `gitRepository` entries of a runtime's `sharedAssets` into read-only stores on warm pods. Its rewrite defines the warm list for every pool to the requirements under "Relationship to draft proposal 0111", and this document cites these parts of its §14 rules for cache-served `gitClone` sources. Rule 2 validates a commit's tree and charges each blob at its size rounded up to 4,096 bytes plus 128 bytes per tree entry against `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`. Rule 3 builds the session's slot repository: `git init` in a new directory, the store's objects directory written to `objects/info/alternates`, a fetch of the commit, a detached checkout, and the config keys `lenny.gitStoreUrl`, the repository's normalized URL, and `lenny.baseCommit`, the commit it was built at. Rule 5, the capture copy, copies into a repository's own object store, before each checkpoint, every object it holds only through the store and needs. Rule 6, restore completion, runs after a checkpoint restore: it points alternates at the new pod's store, or removes them, and fetches `lenny.baseCommit` when that store differs. Rule 7, the lineage rule, lets a derived session's restore fetch through the pool of the session it was derived from, and the rewrite drops it if derive's copy of the stored plan leaves it no case. Rules 2, 3, 5, and 6 run in `lenny-materialize` as the slot UID ([WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)). Warm stores build on it. See "Relationship to draft proposal 0111". |
| Spike | A hands-on test run to check a mechanism this design relies on. This design records one, the warm clone spike of 2026-11-05, under "Not verified" in DR-9. |
| Stop check | A narrow review of the changes in one revision, run to confirm that no Critical, High, or Major issue remains. "Review history" records each one. |

### Terms from other designs and the platform

This design builds on mechanisms other designs and the specification define. The table gives each one the meaning this document relies on.

| Term | Meaning |
| :-- | :-- |
| Runtime and runtime record | A registered agent runtime: its image, process, isolation profile, and session settings. Every runtime runs sessions on agent pods, one session per slot of a claimed pod, because [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) removes `type: mcp` runtimes, service mode, and the `type` and `executionMode` fields. Its record is a row in `runtime_definitions`, and a runtime the chart deploys also has a `Runtime` custom resource ([§5.1](../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#51-runtime)). The row's `registered` column records whether that resource passed the RuntimeReconciler's validation, and DR-4 refuses a session whose final runtime row is not `registered`. |
| Derived runtime | A runtime record that names another runtime in `baseRuntime` and changes some of its fields under §5.1's merge rules ([§5.1 **Derived Runtime**](../spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#derived-runtime)). Today only a `platform-admin` creates one. |
| `Runtime` resource | The Kubernetes custom resource for a runtime. The AgentPod controller reads it to build each pod. |
| Pool | A set of pre-started agent pods for one runtime. Its record, the pool row, is a row in `pools`, which the PoolScalingController renders as a Kubernetes custom resource (CRD), a `PodSet`, for each pod set of the pool, and each pod has an `AgentPod` resource. Pool settings include `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, the number of sessions one pod serves at once, which also fixes the number of slot containers rendered per pod, the `recycle` block, which lets a pod serve another session after one ends, and `warmCount`, the number of idle pods kept ready. A CRD-only pool has the custom resources and no pool row, such as the embedded stack's `echo-pool-embedded`. `scalePolicy.scaleToZero` sets a cron window during which the pool keeps no warm pods. |
| Platform controllers | The PoolScalingController renders pool rows into `PodSet` resources and computes warm targets. The WarmPoolController creates and drains a pool's `AgentPod` resources. The AgentPod controller creates each pod. The RuntimeReconciler mirrors `Runtime` resources into runtime rows. |
| Pod phases | `AgentPod.status.phase`: `warming`, `idle`, `reserved`, `claimed`, `draining`, `failed`, and `terminated` ([§6.2](../spec/06_warm-pod-model.md#62-pod-state-machine)). `reserved` is a recycled pod held for its pinned tenant for a short time after its session ends, which this document calls the reserved hold. |
| Concurrent pod and reused pod | A concurrent pod serves several sessions at once, one per slot, under `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` above 1. A reused pod is a concurrent pod or a pod on a recycling pool. A pool reuses pods when `poolstore.ReusesPods` reads true for it. |
| Acknowledgment | A pool field, such as `sessionPolicy.acknowledgeSharedPod`, by which a deployer accepts the risk a pool setting carries, such as slots sharing a pod's network and IPC namespaces, its adapter, and its `netagent` ([`session-isolation-design.md`, "SI-10"](session-isolation-design.md#si-10-what-stays-shared)). |
| Pool recycle scrub | The cleanup §5.2 **Lenny scrub procedure** runs on a recycling pod between sessions. It is best effort. |
| `runtime_tenant_access` and `pool_tenant_access` | Rows by which a `platform-admin` gives a tenant access to a platform runtime or pool. This document calls each one an access row, and a bare "grant" always means a derivation grant. |
| Roles | `platform-admin` operates the deployment. `tenant-admin` administers one tenant. `tenant-viewer` reads one tenant's configuration. An environment `admin` is a member role inside one environment, below `tenant-admin` ([`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md)). `security-admin` writes the tenant security record ([SO-1](security-oversight-design.md#so-1-security-roles)). |
| Row-level security | The Postgres trigger `lenny_tenant_guard` and policy `lenny_tenant_isolation`, which confine a tenant-scoped table to the transaction's tenant. `pgtenant.InTx` runs a transaction in one tenant's context, and `pgtenant.InAllTenants` in the platform's all-tenants context. |
| Adapter, slot, and slot UID | The adapter is Lenny's process in its own container of each agent pod. It runs at its own UID, holds the pod's projected service account token, deletes a withheld repository store, and reaches a slot's files only through that slot's supervisor ([SI-5](session-isolation-design.md#si-5-the-supervisor-file-descriptor-broker)). A slot is one of the places on a pod where a session runs, served by its own container, `runtime-<n>`, under its own unprivileged slot UID ([SI-1](session-isolation-design.md#si-1-pod-layout)). Each slot holds one session at a time. |
| Runtime supervisor | The platform program that is the first process of every slot container of every agent pod ([PL-1](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)). It launches the runtime's `command` for each session and runs setup commands and `lenny-materialize` at the slot UID on the adapter's request, over the channel `CH-SUPERVISE` ([§4.7.10](../spec/04_system-components.md#4710-deployment-model)). The embedded deployment model, under which a runtime ran in the adapter's process, is removed ([PL-2](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model)). |
| Warm pod | A pod whose infrastructure is ready before any session: its containers are running, its repository stores are filled, and its warm-up checkouts are staged. Every runtime process launches at bind, after materialization and setup. |
| `lenny-materialize`, helper run | The platform's Go helper that a slot's supervisor runs as the slot UID to write a session's plan into `/workspace`, including every git step on a slot repository. One execution is a helper run, and a failed step reaches the adapter as a reason in the helper's slot-reported result ([`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md)). |
| Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild | At warm-up and after each slot release, `lenny-materialize` checks each warm-list entry with `prestage: true` out of its store into `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/<entry>`, with the index refreshed, and at bind it adopts the staged tree by rename for the first `gitClone` source of the session's workspace that the store serves. Under the reflink rebuild, one pristine checked-out tree per store sits in the store volume, and the helper creates the slot's files and clones their content with `FICLONE` where the filesystem supports it. The workspace materialization design owns both. |
| `lenny-store-fill` | An init container of every agent pod that renders a warm list. It runs after the `netagent` native sidecar, under its own UID, with no credentials and with only `lenny-git-stores` mounted read-write, and it fills each store and writes each pristine tree before the adapter and the slot containers start. The owner settled it on 2026-11-13 as decision 61 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), so no platform-privileged process parses fetched git objects. |
| Platform chunk set, contribution, and eligible source | A platform chunk set holds the content chunks of one platform source: a warm repository store, from the working trees of its warm-up checkouts, or a runtime's shared assets. A pod that has never launched a session, meaning that no session has bound to any of its slots, contributes the content it staged. A tree staged for a warm-list entry feeds the set of the entry that staged it, so a tenant entry never feeds a platform-owned set. A session's checkpoints and exports may reference the chunks of its eligible sources instead of uploading them, and the gateway computes those sources from the session's stored `coveredStores` and its runtime revision at every checkpoint and export. A session of a tenant-derived runtime is eligible for its base's assets source, to which its owned pool's pods contribute the base's files, and for the tenant-owned assets set of the runtime's own `inline` and `artifact` assets, which those pods contribute as well ([`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`, "Platform chunk sets"](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#platform-chunk-sets)). |
| Workspace plan and stored plan | A session's workspace plan lists its sources, such as `inlineFile` and `gitClone`, and its setup commands. The gateway composes the runtime's `workspaceDefaults` into it and stores the result on the session row, and every later materialization reads that stored plan ([§14](../spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md#14-workspace-plan-schema)). |
| `gitClone` source and `resolvedCommitSha` | A plan source that clones a git repository at a ref into a path. The gateway resolves the ref to a commit at bind and stores it as `resolvedCommitSha`. |
| Setup commands and `setupCommandPolicy` | Commands run in the pod before the agent starts. A runtime's `setupCommandPolicy` checks a client's commands against an `allowlist` or a `blocklist` of prefixes, and `shell` decides whether a command runs under a shell. A runtime's own default commands are exempt today ([§7.5](../spec/07_session-lifecycle.md#75-setup-commands)). |
| Workspace | The slot's workspace volume, mounted directly at `/workspace` in the slot container. It is the session's working tree and the runtime's working directory, and no path carries the session's identifier ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). |
| Claim, bind, materialization, and finalize | A claim reserves a warm pod for a session, and bind attaches the session and its plan to it. Materialization writes the plan's sources into the pod's workspace. Finalize is the call that completes a session created without a plan. |
| Tenant pin, pool pin, pinned idle pod, and commit pin | The tenant pin is the `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label that ties a pod to one tenant, which `AdmitTenantPin` checks at claim, and the pin readers are the code that reads that label. A pool pin is a session request's `pool` field naming one pool, and a resolution with no pool pin picks a pool by runtime. A pinned idle pod is an idle pod that carries a tenant pin and has no claim, which §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** counts apart from the pool's other warm pods and bounds by the pinned idle bound. A commit pin resolves a `gitClone` ref to a full commit at bind (`PinCommitSHAs`), and a pinned commit is a full commit SHA. |
| Environment, E, and E = ∅ | An environment is a group of a tenant's users with its own policy, which admits runtimes through a label selector. Its policy can carry denies, hosts its sessions may not reach, and routes, which send matching hosts' traffic to a target such as a tenant's upstream proxy: an egress route carries API traffic, and a git route carries git traffic to a git endpoint, the mirror, or the host directly. A tenant has routes of its own as well. Elsewhere in this document a "way" or "path" means a means to an end, and "route" means an API route or one of these routes. E is the environment a session names, and E = ∅ marks a session that names none ([TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping)). |
| Transparent client | A client that creates sessions without naming an environment and sends no workspace plan, so its sessions take the runtime's defaults. |
| Pods with no per-session policy | The rule of [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`, "Effective policy"](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#effective-policy) under which a pod with several slots evaluates every session with E = ∅ where `netagent` cannot attribute a connection to its slot, so a session whose environment has a deny, a route, or another object of its own is refused there with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE`. Where attribution holds, each slot's connections use its own session's scopes and environment, under decision 60 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), which the owner settled on 2026-11-13. |
| `PreRoute` chain and post-chain point | The `PreRoute` chain is the single run of the gateway's `PreRoute` interceptors on every session creation path, which may change the runtime and the pool a session requested. The post-chain point is the step after that chain and after revision selection, and before the pod claim, at which every check that depends on the runtime, the revision, or the pool runs on the final runtime and pool and the chosen revision ([`runtime-routing-design.md`, "Post-chain point"](runtime-routing-design.md#post-chain-point)). The final row is the session row as the post-chain point sees it. |
| Runtime route | A tenant's admin resource that sends the sessions requesting one runtime to target runtimes by weight, each target with an optional pool, applied by a built-in `PreRoute` interceptor ([`runtime-routing-design.md`, "RT-4 Runtime routes"](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes)). Routes replace the experiments that [`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md) removes, and they write no pool configuration. |
| Runtime revision, traffic, and pod set | A revision is an immutable, numbered copy of the runtime fields that define what runs in its pods, such as `image`, `requireSoPeercred`, `workspaceDefaults`, and `sharedAssets`. A runtime's traffic is its active revisions and their weights, and the gateway places each new session on one of them. A pod set is the warm pods of one pool built from one revision, and a pool keeps a pod set per active revision during a rollout ([`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md), RV-1 to RV-3). A derived runtime has no revisions of its own, and a session on it runs a revision of its base, chosen by the base's traffic, with the derived runtime's fields merged over it ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). A retired revision is unrelated to this design's base retirement. |
| Rollout | The procedure that moves a runtime's traffic from one revision to another and deletes the old revision's pod sets ([`runtime-revisions-design.md`, "RV-4 Rollout"](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-4-rollout)). Its `automatic` policy warms the new revision's pod sets before it moves traffic, and waits for an owned pool's new pod set for at most `rollout.ownedPoolWarmTimeoutSeconds`. It replaces the pool-level `RuntimeUpgrade` of §10.5. |
| Delegation | A session can start child sessions, each on the runtime the delegation names, and a parent can export files to its child. A client's `delegationLease` narrows what the session's children may receive, such as their depth (`maxDepth`), and a `DelegationPolicy` lists the targets a parent may delegate to, by label or by an `ids` list of names. Isolation monotonicity requires a child's isolation profile to be at least as restrictive as its parent's ([§8.3](../spec/08_recursive-delegation.md#83-delegation-policy-and-lease)). |
| Derive | A route that creates a new session from an existing one, defined in [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md). It replaces the former replay route. `from: latest` starts from the source's latest snapshot, and `from: start` starts from the source's stored plan at the same git commits. |
| `netagent` | A container in every agent pod that acts as the pod's egress proxy. It terminates TLS for hosts that carry a credential, attaches credentials, and enforces git paths and ref patterns. |
| `lenny-egress` and the decision service | `lenny-egress` is the central egress proxy through which agent traffic leaves the cluster. The decision service, `lenny-egress-authz`, decides whether each connection is allowed and which route it takes. |
| Token Service | The Lenny service that stores provider credentials and mints short-lived tokens from them ([§4.3](../spec/04_system-components.md#43-token-service)). |
| Git mirror | `lenny-git-mirror`, the egress design's service that serves git fetches from per-tenant mirror caches, which the egress design calls stores, with a prefetch resource that fetches a repository ahead of any session. Draft 0111 placed a mirror in the gateway, which the egress design removes, and "the gateway's mirror" in this document means that one. |
| Pod credential API | The gRPC API on which `netagent`, and the git mirror, ask the Token Service for a credential. [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Interfaces"](egress-and-git-design.md#interfaces) defines it as I8. |
| VCS pool | A tenant object, named for a version control system (VCS), that names repository prefixes and a provider credential ([`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md)). Under the [VCS layer](user-git-identity-design.md), `userAuthorization: required` makes each repository need the session user's own access at the provider, `prAuthoring: user` has pull requests authored as the user, and `allowWrite` admits pushes. |
| Git path and covered host | A covered host is a git host that a VCS pool or a git route of the tenant names, or an alias host of that provider, as [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Git on covered hosts"](egress-and-git-design.md#git-on-covered-hosts-netagent) defines it. `netagent` terminates TLS for it and runs the pod's git through itself. A pool has a git path to such a host, except a pool with `egressProfile: restricted` and no `gitAccess`, which has none. |
| Repository authorization check | The check [UG-2](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-2-repository-authorization) runs on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`: the session user must hold a link to the provider and must be able to read the repository there. |
| Stops and `planOriginRuntimes` | [`session-derive-design.md`, "Stops"](session-derive-design.md#stops) records each tenant-derived runtime whose defaults a stored plan holds in `planOriginRuntimes`, with stop counters kept in `content_stop_epochs`, so a derive is refused when one of those runtimes has since had its content stopped by a grant flag set false or a forbid. Each stored setup command also carries an `origin`: `operator`, `tenant`, `client`, or `derive`. The `origin` decides which policy checks the command, and it decides no folder, because DR-12 has the gateway write each command's folder on it as `cwd` when the plan is composed. |
| Repository allowlist, and readable and writable sets | A tenant's repository allowlist names the repositories its VCS pools may serve. A session's readable set holds the repositories it may fetch, and its writable set, from `writableRepositories`, those it may push to. `netagent` enforces both. |
| Writable entry states and the `prAuthoring: user` attach | Each writable repository entry has a `state`. `granted` marks one the session may push to, and `pendingApproval` one that waits on a repository write approval. On a VCS pool with `prAuthoring: user`, a writable entry attaches the session user's link as a binding with `delivery: inject` ([UG-3](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-3-pull-request-authoring)), so pull requests are authored as that user. |
| Service identity | A non-human caller listed in a VCS pool's `serviceIdentities`, with its own repository allowlist, whose sessions use the App token and skip the user's check ([`user-git-identity-design.md`, "Service identities"](user-git-identity-design.md#service-identities)). An unlisted service caller is refused on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`. |
| Link and App token | A link is a user's stored authorization at a provider. An App token comes from the tenant's GitHub App installation and acts as a bot account. |
| Provider capability level | How a VCS pool's provider issues tokens, which the [egress design](egress-and-git-design.md) classes as `mint-scoped`, a token limited to named repositories, `mint-coarse`, a token with the identity's full rights, or `stored`, a token the tenant stored. |
| Gates | Per-tenant grants a platform admin gives, one per risk class, before a tenant can opt in to a capability. [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) defines them, and this design adds the class `tenantDerivedRuntimes`. |
| Egress ceiling | The largest set of hosts any pod of a pool can reach through the egress proxy, whichever tenant or session it serves ([`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Configuration by scope"](egress-and-git-design.md#configuration-by-scope)). |
| Credential delivery (`inject` and `file`) | A credential binding's `delivery`. Under `inject`, `netagent` attaches the token to requests and the runtime holds only a placeholder. Under `file`, `netagent` writes the token to a file the runtime reads. |
| LLM host reservation and proxy token | The egress design reserves the endpoint hosts of the LLM upstream records of [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md), so no tenant route or host set can claim one. The check runs at each egress decision, so raw sockets on a pool with `egressProfile: internet` escape it. That design removes LLM credential leases and direct delivery mode, so a session's runtime holds only the session's proxy token, which the gateway's LLM proxy alone accepts, and the proxy attaches the upstream's credential. |
| Tenant pool overlay | A tenant's record over a pool's egress and git settings, which applies to that tenant's pods alone. The pool's `tenantOverride` flags decide, per setting, whether an overlay may narrow, extend, or neither ([`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Tenant pool overlays"](egress-and-git-design.md#tenant-pool-overlays)). |
| `egressProfile` | A pool setting: `restricted`, `proxied`, or `internet`. Under `internet`, the pod also has raw sockets that bypass `netagent` and `lenny-egress`. |
| Guardrail, posture, and `suspendedBy` | A guardrail is a setting a `security-admin` writes in the tenant security record ([SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)) that limits what a tenant admin can configure. A change that weakens one takes effect after a delay. Posture is that record's read-only report of a tenant's security-relevant settings. `suspendedBy` marks an object a guardrail suspended, and every component then treats the object as absent. |
| Audit chain, SO-4a mirror, and SIEM destination | Each tenant has an audit chain of events. [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain) mirrors a platform admin's write to a tenant's configuration into that tenant's chain, and [SO-3](security-oversight-design.md#so-3-per-tenant-siem-destination) delivers a tenant's events to its own SIEM (security information and event management) destination. |
| Bootstrap seed | The `lenny-bootstrap` Job, a Helm hook that runs after install and after each upgrade, creates runtimes, pools, and tenants from the chart's `bootstrap` values, such as `bootstrap.tenants`. It leaves an existing resource unchanged unless `--force-update` is passed, which §17.6 item 5 governs. A seeded runtime or pool can carry `tenantAccess`, the tenants that receive access rows. |
| Label webhook | `lenny-label-immutability`, the admission webhook that keeps Lenny's pod labels from changing after creation. The egress design adds an `unset → owner` rule, which lets the controller set `lenny.dev/tenant-id` to the owning tenant when it creates a pod, and states which of its proposals stages it in the "Tenant-owned pool" row of [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Pod models"](egress-and-git-design.md#pod-models). |
| Resource class | A named pod size, such as `small` or `medium`, that sets a pod's CPU and memory requests. A runtime's `allowedResourceClasses` lists the classes its pools may use. |
| `ResourceQuota`, `PriorityClass`, and `LimitRange` | Kubernetes objects. A `ResourceQuota` caps what the pods of a namespace request, a `PriorityClass` sets a pod's scheduling priority, and a `LimitRange` bounds each pod's requests. |
| Kata | The microVM isolation runtime that a `microvm` isolation profile selects. Its pods run on the nodes of the node class whose members include `microvm` ([PM-1](placement-design.md#pm-1-node-classes-and-presets)). |
| Placement offering and reserved overlay | A placement offering is a platform-admin-defined option that carries a `nodePlacement` and a node tenancy, and every pool references one. A reserved overlay is a `nodePlacement` that a pool applies to its reserved pods alone, bounded by the pool's node class ([PM-3](placement-design.md#pm-3-placement-offerings-and-per-pool-nodeplacement)). |
| Tenant security settings | The operator-set settings on a tenant that replace `workspaceTier`, such as `placement.nodeTenancy` and `placement.minIsolationProfile` ([`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-1-settings-model)). |

### Labels from other designs

Proposals in other designs carry a label whose prefix names the design. This document cites them by label and links each citation to its definition. The table names what each cited label stands for. A decision number of another design, such as decision 15 of the tenant scoping design, is always named with its design, and a bare "decision N" means a row of this document's "Decisions".

| Labels | Design | What they name |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| EG-2, EG-3, and EG-9 | [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Proposal split"](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) | Proposals of the egress design: EG-2 the pod network agent and proxied egress, EG-3 the pod git path, and EG-9 egress policy objects, which adds pool ownership (`ownerTenantId`), tenant pool overlays, the per-class gates, and the single write path for tenant opt-ins. |
| TS-1 and TS-3 | [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) | [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping) environment scoping, and [TS-3](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-3-session-environment-context-and-cross-environment-sharing) session environment context and cross-environment sharing, which includes the fail-closed gate on `runtimeAuthorizedForCaller`. |
| SO-1, SO-2, SO-3, and SO-4a | [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md) | [SO-1](security-oversight-design.md#so-1-security-roles) security roles, [SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture) the tenant security record and posture, [SO-3](security-oversight-design.md#so-3-per-tenant-siem-destination) the per-tenant SIEM destination, and [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain) the mirror of a platform admin's writes to the tenant's audit chain. |
| SD-1 | [`session-derive-design.md`, "SD-1: One route"](session-derive-design.md#sd-1-one-route) | The single derive route and its modes. |
| UG-2 | [`user-git-identity-design.md`, "UG-2: Repository authorization"](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-2-repository-authorization) | Repository authorization on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`. |
| RT-1, RT-2, and RT-4 | [`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md) | Proposals of the runtime routing design: [RT-1](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-1-removal) the removal of experiments, [RT-2](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-2-the-interceptor-contract) the `PreRoute` payload, the single chain, and the post-chain point, and [RT-4](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes) runtime routes. |
| SI-1, SI-3, SI-5, SI-8, SI-10, SI D9, and SI O9 to O11 | [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md) | The pod layout with one container per slot, the slot release, the supervisor's file-descriptor broker, shared repository stores and assets, what stays shared between slots, the decision that every workspace entry belongs to the slot UID, and the decisions on `netagent`'s per-slot attribution (O9, answered in the egress design's "Slot attribution and fetch grants"), withheld stores on pods with several slots (O10), and git on slot repositories (O11, which the owner settled on 2026-11-12 as the slot UID). |
| PL-1 to PL-3 | [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md) | The pod blueprint, the removal of the embedded deployment model, and the runtime-visible layout: `/workspace`, `/session`, `/lenny/assets`, `/lenny/git-stores`, `/lenny/bin`, and `/run/lenny` in a slot, and `/adapter/...` in the adapter. |
| WM-9 and WM-10 | [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) | Archive extraction in the helper with the rule that no platform-privileged process parses untrusted content, and every git step on a slot repository in the helper, with the pinned git and the fetch grant. |
| PM-1, PM-3, PM-7, PM-8, and PM-9 | [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md) | Node classes, placement offerings with the reserved overlay, region placement, system components with their priority classes, and namespaces. |
| TSS-6 and TSS-9 | [`tenant-security-settings-design.md`](tenant-security-settings-design.md) | The placement settings on the tenant, and the removal of `workspaceTier`. |
| RP-2 and RP D3 | [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies) | Declared capabilities with dependency rules, which replace integration levels. |

The owner settled AR O1 to O8 on 2026-11-13, and DR-4 owns the O8 check. |
| IC-4 and IC decision D1 | [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#platform-chunk-sets) | The checkpoint catalog with its platform chunk sets, which the owner settled on 2026-11-13. |

| RV-2 to RV-5, and runtime revisions decision D8 | [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) | Proposals of the runtime revisions design: [RV-2](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-2-selection) revision selection, [RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods) pod sets per pool and revision, [RV-4](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-4-rollout) the runtime rollout, and [RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes) derived runtimes on base revisions. [D8](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions) exempts a base rollout's extra pod set on an owned pool from the tenant's bounds. |

## High-level design

Lenny runs agent sessions on runtimes, and each runtime has pools of pre-started pods that sessions claim. Today only the platform operator can create a runtime or a pool. A tenant that needs a variant of a runtime the operator shares has to ask the operator each time, for example when one team's environment needs pods that serve a single session so that the team's network policy can apply, or when every session should start with the tenant's own files, tools, and repositories. The current code also lets a tenant administrator edit a shared runtime or pool in ways that reach other tenants, and several of the checks that should keep tenants apart are missing.

This design first closes those gaps. It then lets a tenant administrator create a derived runtime: a copy of a runtime the operator has made available to that tenant, whose settings the tenant can only narrow, with optional files, setup commands, and git repositories that every session on it receives. Each such runtime gets its own pool, copied from a pool the operator chose, whose pods serve that tenant alone. The operator turns the capability on per tenant, sets how many pods each tenant may hold, and can let a tenant reserve pods so that they exist before any session needs them. The main limit is that anything a tenant administrator adds to a runtime runs as code in every session on it, so a stolen tenant administrator account reaches the credentials of that tenant's sessions. Each kind of addition therefore needs its own grant from the operator, and the tenant's security team can forbid it.

The rest of this section states each part in the design's terms, which "Terms" above defines.

**Prerequisites (DR-1 to DR-3).** DR-1 removes every `tenant-admin` write on platform-global runtimes and pools, makes session create, discovery, and delegation read `runtime_tenant_access`, implements the bootstrap seed's `tenantAccess`, and reserves `--` in names. DR-2 lets a pool on a derived runtime create pods, through `podRuntimeRef`, which points the AgentPod controller at the base's `Runtime` resource. DR-3 runs setup commands in the slot container at the slot UID through the runtime supervisor, away from the adapter's service account token, as [SI decision D6](session-isolation-design.md#decisions) states for every slot. Each fixes today's tree whether or not the rest lands.

**Records, names, and the grant (DR-4).** Tenant-derived runtimes live in `tenant_runtime_definitions`, a table under row-level security, so a query with no tenant context sees none of them. Every name carries the tenant's name prefix, so names stay unique across the deployment. Every session path reads runtimes through the tenant view, whose type replaces the plain runtime store interface so that a missed site does not compile. A `platform-admin` writes a derivation grant per tenant, which lists the bases and template pools the tenant may use, the grant flags, and the budgets. New routes under `/v1/admin/derived-runtimes` carry the tenant's writes, and "Fields" states what a tenant can set on each field.

**Owned pools (DR-5).** Each tenant-derived runtime has one owned pool. It copies every setting that needs a platform decision from the template pool, and a template write updates the copies in the same transaction. A tenant's one choice over concurrency and recycling is `oneSessionPerPod`. The controller stamps the owner on each pod at creation, and `lenny.dev/served` tells the code that reads the tenant pin which pods have served a session. The tenant's charge T bounds the pods a tenant holds, and a deployment total bounds the sum of the grants.

**Base changes (DR-6).** A platform write to a base or a template succeeds whatever tenant-derived runtimes exist, apart from the deletions that DP9 under "Security properties" names. The labels R-n and DP-n name this design's requirements and security properties, which those sections list. The tenant view merges each tenant-derived runtime with the live base and grant entry on every resolve, so a change that the runtime no longer meets reads as `incompatible`. An outbox delivers `runtime.base_changed` to each affected tenant, and a retirement gives tenants notice, apart from an emergency path.

**Default git clones, shared assets, and warm stores (DR-9 and DR-11).** A tenant can name repositories that every session clones. A default clone holds no credential and passes the same checks as a client's `gitClone` source on every path that composes it. Its `write` field decides whether a session can push to it without the client naming it. Where the operator allows it on the deployment and in the tenant's grant, a tenant can also add read-only files under `/lenny/assets` and `gitRepository` assets to its runtime's `sharedAssets`. A `gitRepository` asset that a platform admin writes on the runtime is the platform's, and the tenant cannot change it. A `gitRepository` asset fills a store on each warm pod of the owned pool, and any `gitClone` source of that repository, from the defaults or the plan, fetches only the commits the store lacks. With `prestage: true`, the warm-up checkout of the materialization design also stages the repository's working tree in each idle slot before bind, and the first such source adopts it. Every store on a pod comes from one warm list per pool, which holds the tenant's entries and the operator's public entries on the base under one rule set. A session reaches a tenant's store only after passing its read checks, and on a pod that serves one session a store whose check fails is deleted for that session. DR-11 has no section of its own: "Tenant shared assets" and "Warm stores" under DR-9 state it.

**Repository folders (DR-12).** Every repository a plan clones goes in its own subfolder of `/workspace`, and an optional `workingDirectory` names the folder the runtime starts in. DR-12 applies to sessions on every runtime.

**Capacity reservation (DR-10).** Where the operator and a platform admin allow it, a tenant sets a floor of pods on an owned pool. The floor holds against other pools through a split namespace quota and, optionally, the reserved overlay of the placement design, which places reserved pods on nodes within the pool's node class. No reservation preempts or evicts another pod.

**Interactions, oversight, and records (DR-8 and DR-7).** Environments see only runtimes the tenant can reach, a runtime route reaches only runtimes and pools the tenant can use, and delegation resolves through the tenant view. Every check on the runtime and pool runs at the post-chain point, on the final row and the chosen base revision. The tenant security record gains `derivedRuntimes.forbid`, posture reports each tenant-derived runtime, and audit events carry each default command's full text.

```
 platform-admin ──(derivation grant: bases, template pools, flags, budgets)──> Gateway
 tenant-admin ───(POST /v1/admin/derived-runtimes)──────────────────────────> Gateway
                                                                                │ one transaction
                                     tenant_runtime_definitions + owned pool row (copied from the template pool)
                                                                                │
                                              PoolScalingController: PodSet (podRuntimeRef, warmClones)
                                                                                │
                     WarmPoolController and AgentPod controller: pods built from the base's Runtime resource,
                                          labelled for the owner, stores filled and checkouts staged before ready
                                                                                ▲
 Session create ──> Gateway: tenant view resolves the name, composes defaults, runs clone checks ──> claim
```

## Owner's choice

On 2026-11-04 the owner chose this design over a tenant-owned pool on an unchanged base, in these words: "I want derived runtimes for the two reasons you state. I want tenants to define their own default files and git clones." The two reasons are that transparent clients of a shared runtime keep working when an environment adds a deny, and that a tenant admin creates runtimes. The alternative stays in git history as `designs/tenant-owned-pools-design.md`, revision 5, at commit `7a72e0b65`.

The owner then answered "Default clones: yes" and "Default setup commands: yes", and added: "I think we need to add settings to control the min number of pods per tenant to reserve capacity. This should only be allowed if the platform admin/operator allow it". DR-9 and DR-10 state the clones and the reservation, and "Tenant defaults" states the commands.

On warming, the owner asked for "warming up the clone ahead of time", and then confirmed that draft proposal 0111 is what that meant: "This design should build on top of 0111, or replace 0111 where it makes sense. Add a note to the design doc for the record". "Warm stores" states the design and "Relationship to draft proposal 0111" holds that note. On the write scope of a default clone, the owner observed that "the client may not know what the repo is called, since it's not declared in the session but the pool", and then accepted the recommended `write` field. On derive, the owner wrote: "ideally we pick up the exact state in which the source session was", and "Whether the repo is read-only or write-only should be irrelevant. I don't think we should provide `defaultCloneCommits` as an option in the derive or replay APIs." The owner then merged derive and replay into one route with two modes, which [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) states.

## Requirements

- R1. A `tenant-admin` holding the platform's grant can create, update, and delete a derived runtime of a base the grant names, with no `platform-admin` action per runtime.
- R2. A tenant admin can give an environment one-session pods of a runtime whose shared pool serves concurrent sessions.
- R3. Another tenant cannot see, name, use, collide with, or be affected by a tenant-derived runtime.
- R4. Nothing a tenant sets widens what the base and the platform allow: image, process, isolation, egress ceiling, credential delivery, or command policy.
- R5. The platform bounds the pods each tenant's derived runtimes hold, warm and active, and those bounds sum to no more than a deployment total the operator sets. Nodes and the namespace `ResourceQuota` stay shared with every other pool, so R5 limits what owned pools take and reserves nothing. R10 states reservation.
- R6. A tenant-derived runtime blocks no platform write, apart from the exceptions DP9 states, and a platform change does not strand a tenant without notice, apart from a stated emergency path.
- R7. A tenant's security team can forbid the capability and can see what it produced.
- R8. A deployment that issues no grant behaves as it does today, apart from the defect fixes in DR-1 to DR-3.
- R9. A tenant admin can define default workspace files, setup commands, and git clones on a derived runtime, each under its own grant flag, and a default clone can give a session push access to its repository with no repository name from the client. Where the operator allows it on the deployment and the grant entry sets `allowAssets`, a tenant admin can add read-only shared assets once DR-3 has landed. A tenant admin can warm a repository ahead of time on warm pods through a `gitRepository` asset, under `allowGitClones` and DR-3, and any session whose workspace clones that repository uses the store and, when the entry asks for it, a tree staged before bind.
- R10. Where the operator and the platform admin allow it, a tenant admin can reserve a minimum number of pods for a derived runtime. The pods exist before any session, other pools cannot take their share of the namespace quota, and with the placement design's reserved overlay on nodes the operator reserves, other pools cannot take their nodes. No reservation evicts another pod.

## Security properties

- DP1. A tenant-derived runtime's owner comes from the authenticated principal at create and never changes. No request field sets it.
- DP2. A tenant-derived runtime's row is readable only in a transaction carrying its tenant's context or the platform's all-tenants context. A query with no tenant context cannot see it.
- DP3. Every session path resolves a runtime name within the session's tenant, through an interface that cannot read another tenant's row. A tenant-view miss on the final row refuses the request, on any name, with one answer for an absent name and another tenant's name.
- DP4. A pod of an owned pool serves only the owner, from its creation to its end.
- DP5. The image, process, isolation profile, credential delivery mode, and egress ceiling of an owned pool's pods come from platform-written records.
- DP6. A tenant default, file or command, and a tenant's `inline`, `artifact`, or `gitRepository` asset are treated as code execution at the slot UID. Each is accepted only under its grant flag, `allowAssets` for `inline` and `artifact` assets and `allowGitClones` for a `gitRepository` asset, and only on a base whose setup runs at that UID, which DR-3 provides, so neither a tenant command nor a base command reading a tenant file runs with the adapter's identity. A command also passes the base's `setupCommandPolicy`, with `shell: false`.
- DP7. Budgets are platform-held. A tenant has no write path to its own grant.
- DP8. The capability adds no Kubernetes permission to the gateway, and no tenant input reaches a cluster-scoped resource.
- DP9. A platform write to a base or a template pool succeeds regardless of what tenant-derived runtimes exist. The exceptions are `DELETE .../tenant-access/{tenantId}` and a template's `DELETE`, which the operator's own live grant entry refuses, a base's `DELETE` before its retirement date, which an emergency retirement gets past, and a write that raises the setup bound past a pool's window. A base write that raises `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds`, or a template write that raises a pool's fetch retry window, is refused when any pool, platform or tenant-owned, would no longer fit its window, and the refusal names those pools and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` at which they fit (decision 40).
- DP10. Each write passes through the single write path for tenant opt-ins that [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) (egress policy objects) adds, applies any guardrail of the tenant security record ([SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)), and records the content it changes.
- DP12. A default clone stores no credential. Its token comes from the tenant's VCS pool or the session user's link, under the checks a client's `gitClone` source gets, and those checks run on every path that composes it.
- DP13. A reservation never evicts a pod, and the total reservable capacity is set only through Helm.
- DP14. A warm store holds only a repository on its pool's warm list. On an owned pool that list holds the runtime's `gitRepository` assets and the base's public operator entries. A session reaches a tenant's or a private store only after passing that repository's read checks, and on a pod that serves one session a store whose checks it fails is deleted before anything runs for it. On a pool that reuses pods, a repository later removed from the runtime stays readable on occupied pods until they retire, as "Who reaches a store" under DR-9 states.
- DP15. A default clone adds a repository to a session's writable set only under a stated `write` value. A value that needs no client statement also needs a platform grant and confines pushes to a named ref namespace, and a delegated child never gets write access its parent lacks.

### Shared records today

A `tenant-admin` updating a platform-global record its tenant holds an access row on affects every other tenant holding an access row on it.

`PUT /v1/admin/runtimes/{name}` checks only that the caller's tenant holds an access row. `applyRuntimeUpdate` then writes `workspaceDefaults`, `labels`, `setupCommandPolicy`, `delegationPolicyRef`, `publishedMetadata`, `sessionPolicy`, `limits`, `isolationProfile`, and `capabilities`. `runtimes.go` contains no role check. §14.1 **Runtime workspace defaults.** composes the runtime's `workspaceDefaults` into every session's plan, and §7.5 **Command policy:** exempts those commands from `setupCommandPolicy`. An administrator of tenant `acme` can therefore place a command that runs in a session of tenant `globex`. The RuntimeReconciler rewrites the CRD-owned fields at its next reconcile, and its field list omits `workspaceDefaults`, `publishedMetadata`, `sessionPolicy`, and `sharedAssets`, so those writes persist on a CRD-registered runtime too.

The pool routes have the same gate. F-5.2.67 records the egress case. The same gate also lets one tenant re-point `runtimeRef`, change `isolationProfile`, set the warm count, and drain a shared pool, which refuses new sessions for every tenant on it.

### Not verified

- How a §10.5 upgrade's new image reaches pods was not established, because `PodSetSpec` has `runtimeRef` and no image and the runtime upgrade record holds `NewImage`. [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) replaces that upgrade with a runtime rollout ([RV-4](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-4-rollout)), under which an owned pool keeps a pod set per distinct pod hash among the active base revisions and follows the base's rollout with no run of its own ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). Whether the controller can render pod sets and warm lists per pool and revision is listed under that design's "Validation owed". Until it lands, an owned pod takes the base's `Runtime` resource at creation.
- DF-3 rests on a search for any path that creates a `Runtime` resource for an admin-registered runtime. The search found none, and no pod was started to confirm it.
- Billing attributes no idle pod to a tenant today. Idle warm pods appear only in `lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes`, a per-pool metric with no tenant label (`pkg/controller/warmpool/idle_meter.go`). [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md) adds `pool.idle_usage`, a periodic §11.2.1 event that reports an owned pool's warming, idle, held, draining, and reserved pod minutes with the pods' resources, attributed to `ownerTenantId`. It depends on DR-5 for the owner and reads DR-10's `spec.reserved` mark.
- What the projected service account token can do at the gateway was not traced, so DF-4's rating is a floor.
- What `messages.go` does on a not-found answer from its runtime read was not traced. The type change under "Reading" covers it either way.
- The tree was read at commit `a575d5718`, and the design documents as they stand in the working tree.

## Costs of the capability

The owner has decided to have this capability. These are its costs.

- **Tenant code in every session.** Any tenant default is code execution at the slot UID, in every session on that runtime, and the user creating the session has no opt-out. A default file is code as well as a command: a base command installs from it, and a runtime loads a hook or configuration file from the workspace. A stolen `tenant-admin` therefore reaches what [`user-credential-delivery-design.md`](user-credential-delivery-design.md) lists for this attacker: it reads a `file` token and uses an `inject` token, including a host's mint operation. It also reads what later sessions place in the workspace. A grant with `allowWorkspaceFiles`, `allowSetupCommands`, and `allowGitClones` off carries none of this cost and still serves R2. DF-1 is worse than this today, because it crosses tenants, and DR-1 closes it. Within a tenant the risk is new.
- **Warm pods per runtime.** Each tenant-derived runtime has its own pool and at least one warm pod, because a pool has no working cold path today (DF-11). An environment moved from a concurrent pod to one-session pods also uses more pods per session.
- **Capacity planned by customers.** §17.8.2's sizing assumes an operator's pool list. Tenant writes now add pools within the budgets, and the namespace `ResourceQuota` is shared.
- **Slower base retirement.** A base with tenant-derived runtimes takes a notice period to retire, apart from the emergency path, and a platform change reaches runtimes the operator did not write.
- **A wider read path.** Every session-path read of a runtime changes to take a tenant, across `pkg/gateway/sessionserver`, `pkg/gateway/mcpfabric`, `pkg/gateway/session`, and `cmd/lenny-gateway`, and each holder of the plain runtime store interface changes type. The compiler finds those sites. Many of them continue on a not-found answer today, so the change is needed for correctness as well as isolation (see "Reading").
- **Operating load.** Tenant-chosen runtime and pool names appear in operator logs and in metric labels, which raises label cardinality, and an operator debugging a failed pod now reads tenant configuration.
- **Prerequisite work.** DR-1, DR-2, and DR-3 close defects this capability cannot rest on. DF-3 and DF-5 mean no derived runtime runs a session today, so the way [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 15](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions) gives needs DR-2 whoever creates the runtime.

## DR-1 to DR-3: Prerequisites

**DR-1, the shared-record boundary.** R3 and R4 need a base that no tenant can change. A `tenant-admin` loses every write on a platform-global runtime or pool, including the pool's `warm-count`, `drain`, `circuit-breaker`, `bootstrap-override`, and `resume-reconciliation` routes. `PermManageRuntimes` and `PermManagePools` leave `RolePermissions(RoleTenantAdmin)`, which is what `TenantAdminPermissions()` returns and what the custom-role ceiling reads, so a custom role cannot hold them either. `AllPermissions()` keeps them for `platform-admin`. Reads stay. [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s tenant pool overlay remains the path for egress and git settings, and a derived runtime is the path for the rest. DR-1 also moves session create, discovery, and delegation to read `runtime_tenant_access`, and reserves the `--` name form. On a resolution with no pool pin, a pool with a pool row in `pools` and no owner needs a `pool_tenant_access` row, an owned pool checks its owner, and a CRD-only pool such as `echo-pool-embedded` is exempt, because the foreign key on `pool_tenant_access` refuses it. A soft-deleted row counts as a pool row, and a read error refuses. A miss answers as no matching pool. The check runs at the post-chain point of [RT-2](runtime-routing-design.md#post-chain-point), on the final pool, only where a create, derive, or child placement claims a pod, so a revocation does not fail `/start` for a session already holding a claim or lose slot accounting in `startOnPod`, `launchOnPod`, `resumeOnPod`, and `accountFailedSlot`.

Those reads refuse every session on a fresh install unless the bootstrap seed writes access rows, and it does not (DF-14). DR-1 therefore implements §17.6's `tenantAccess` on seeded runtimes and pools. An absent `tenantAccess` gives access to `default` when the seed names no tenant, which is the chart's default `bootstrap.tenants: []`, the seed's tenant when it names exactly one, and none when it names several (this design's decision 15). The chart and the handler take a `tenants` list, so several is a normal value, and §17.6's singular `tenant` is stale (DF-19). The `lenny-bootstrap` Job also runs post-upgrade, so a list or default applies only when the seed creates the row, as §17.6's "left unchanged" states, which keeps `helm upgrade` from undoing a revocation. `--force-update` adds the tenants of a listed `tenantAccess`, removes no access row, logs a `WARN` naming each existing access row the list omits, and never applies the default, which narrows §17.6 item 5's full replacement for this field. A seed naming several tenants that leaves a runtime or pool with no access logs a `WARN` naming it, because a session there otherwise reports only that no pool matches.

[`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md) removes experiments and their pool effects ([RT-1](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-1-removal)), which closes the experiment path of DF-9. Runtime routes replace them. A route writes no pool configuration, and its admission refuses a target outside the tenant view and a pool the tenant cannot select ([RT-4](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes)), so a route reaches no pool another tenant uses. DR-8's "Runtime routes" states how routes meet tenant-derived runtimes.

**DR-2, pods for derived runtimes.** `podRuntimeRef` is under "Pods". §5.1 changes to state that a derived runtime needs its own pool, removes the fallback sentence, and states that `sessionPolicy` on a derived runtime supplies the values of its pool. A platform-created derived runtime follows the same rule, so this fixes the way [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 15](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions) gives for an operator on its own.

**DR-3, setup command identity.** The adapter asks the slot's runtime supervisor over `CH-SUPERVISE` to run each setup command in a forked worker at the slot UID, through the `setup` request of [SI-3](session-isolation-design.md#si-3-runtime-processes-slot-release-and-setup-commands), with output on log pipes as the materialization design states. Commands then run from the runtime image, with the agent's access. A client's commands get the same change. Every slot container of every agent pod runs the supervisor ([PL-1](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)), so no base is refused a tenant default for want of one. DR-3's claim is limited to the adapter's identity: the token and the writable volume go out of reach. Under session isolation each slot has its own UID and its own run volume, so a slot reads only its own credential file at `/run/lenny/credentials.json` ([SI-7](session-isolation-design.md#si-7-credentials-manifests-and-other-shared-files)).

## DR-4: Tenant runtime records

### Options

| Option | Result |
| :-- | :-- |
| A nullable `owner_tenant_id` on `runtime_definitions` | Existing reads keep working unchanged, and each one would return another tenant's row unless it adds a check. DF-2 shows how application-layer filtering on this record has fared. It also fails `lenny_tenant_guard` and §12.3's R-01, which requires `tenant_id` to lead the primary index, or every composite index on a high-frequency query path, of a tenant-scoped table. |
| A composite key `(tenant_id, name)` on one table | It rewrites the table, its foreign keys, and the platform rows, and it leaves two tenants with one name, which conflicts with the pod label, the CRD names, and pool lookup by name. |
| **A separate tenant-scoped table, with a reserved name prefix per tenant (recommended)** | A query with no tenant context cannot see a tenant row, so a missed read leaks nothing.
