- `submodules` is absent, because a submodule names repositories the write did not check. `resolvedCommitSha` is absent, because the gateway writes it.
- `path` is present and is a relative subdirectory. It is neither `.` nor equal to, a parent of, or a child of another default clone's path or a default file's path, on the runtime or its live base.
- No component of a default clone's `path` or a default file's `path` is `.git`, compared case-insensitively. `workspaceplan.validatePath` does not refuse this today, and "Not verified" below states why it matters.
- On a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, the owned pool is one [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) accepts, so a recycling or concurrent owned pool is refused at write. Otherwise every session on the runtime would answer `USER_AUTHORIZATION_UNSUPPORTED_POD`.
- The owned pool has a git path. A template with `egressProfile: restricted` and no `gitAccess` has none.
- With `write` other than `none`, the "Write scope" rules below hold.

**One session per pod.** A clone with a `write` other than `none`, or under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, needs an owned pool on which `poolstore.ReusesPods` is false, so that each pod serves one session in its life. `oneSessionPerPod: true` gives that on any template. Under decision 60 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), settled on 2026-11-13, `netagent` scopes each slot of a concurrent pod to its own session where it can attribute a connection to its slot, and it keeps the union of the pod's sessions' readable and writable sets where it cannot. A writable entry on a pod with several slots would then reach another slot under that fallback, and on every reused pod it would share `netagent`, the adapter, and the sandbox kernel with a later or concurrent user's session, which is the ground on which [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md#pod-models) refuses reused pods under its decision 52. `userAuthorization: required` refuses such pods already. Two statements keep this true after the write:

- The transaction that writes the first such clone sets `oneSessionPerPod: true` on the owned row, when the effective policy reuses no pods or the request sets the field, and is refused otherwise. The field is never cleared. While it is set, the effective policy reuses no pods, whatever the template states. A template write still succeeds, under DP9, and the event names each pool it does not reach. Removing the clone does not clear it, so a pool that has held such a clone never widens. A tenant that needs a wider policy creates a new runtime.
- The field is one-way because a pod created for one session can hold a stored writable plan or the tree of a session under `userAuthorization: required`, and `rebindReserved`, `ClaimSlot`'s two passes, and the idle scan all read the pool's current policy. No check on a pod could cover them all, and a widened policy would reach each of them.

A `gitRepository` asset needs no such pool, as "Who reaches a store" states, apart from one under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, which takes the same rule as a clone there, because its read checks are per user.

### Binding

`ComposeInto` adds the clones after the default files and ahead of the client's sources, and they enter the stored plan as `gitClone` sources carrying `runtimeDefault: true`. The gateway writes that field and refuses it from a client, as it does `resolvedCommitSha`. The rest of the session path then treats them as declared sources: VCS pools with `userAuthorization: required`, readable sets, the pod's clone, and the stored `resolvedCommitSha` all apply with no second mechanism. The host and prefix checks under "Checks at write" run again at bind, because a VCS pool or git route can change after the write, and neither `checkGitCloneAuthBindings` nor the bind-time ref resolver covers an `auth`-less clone or a full commit.

The order in today's code needs changing. `resolveSubmittedPlan` runs `checkGitCloneAuthBindings` and `PinCommitSHAs` on the client's plan, and `composeRuntimeDefaults` runs later, once the `PreRoute` chain has fixed the runtime, so a composed clone would skip both. Composition reads the `workspaceDefaults` of the chosen base revision, and it and the checks below run at the post-chain point of [RT-2](runtime-routing-design.md#post-chain-point). These rules follow:

| Path | Rule |
| :-- | :-- |
| `POST /v1/sessions` with a plan, and `POST /v1/sessions/start` | After `composeRuntimeDefaults` and before `claimAtCreate`, the gateway runs the auth binding check, the environment and allowlist checks, the repository authorization check, and the commit pin on the composed default clones. It also runs the read checks on each tenant entry of the final pool's warm list for the chosen revision whose repository the composed plan does not name, as "Who reaches a store" states. The client's sources keep their earlier checks, so a bad client plan still fails before routing. |
| `POST /v1/sessions` with no plan | The plan binds at finalize, after the create-time claim. The gateway runs every check except the commit pin on the final runtime's default clones, and the read checks on the final pool's tenant entries that those clones do not name, before `claimForNewRow`, so a user with no access holds no pod and the claim carries the right `withheldStores`. |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/finalize` | The same full run follows `composeRuntimeDefaults`, with the read checks on the pool's tenant entries that the plan does not name. A refusal binds nothing and leaves the session in `created`, as §14.1 states for a rejected plan. |
| A delegated child | `composeChildWorkspaceDefaults` makes the same full run, with the child's user and environment and the read checks on the target pool's tenant entries that the child's plan does not name, and a refusal fails the delegation before the child row exists. `Service` gains the resolver and credential dependencies it lacks today. |
| Derive | [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) binds a copy of the source's stored plan at create and composes nothing. The full run covers the plan's clones, and the read checks cover each tenant entry on the target pool's warm list whose repository the plan does not name, as the next row does, ahead of `reserveDerivedSession` and any copy, so a refusal has nothing to undo. |
| Resume, retry, and rebuild | The stored plan holds the commit, so these read it, and the repository authorization check runs as [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) states for resume. The next pod can hold a warm store whose repository the stored plan does not name, because a tenant admin added the `gitRepository` asset after the session bound, so the gateway also runs the read checks for each tenant entry of the pool's warm list whose repository the plan does not name, ahead of the claim on both branches of `resumeOnPod`. A failure withholds the store as "Withheld stores" states, and the resume proceeds. On a pod with several slots a failure refuses the resume, as "Withheld stores" states, and the resume fails under §6.2's failure path, and the recorded reason has the fields the refusal below has, because the resume driver has no response to write to. |

All of these read the final runtime and its chosen revision, so a runtime route or a `PreRoute` interceptor that moves a session brings the final runtime's clones with it, under the same checks.

### Whose credential

| Case | Identity on the clone | When it cannot read |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| No `auth`, a repository under no VCS pool prefix | Anonymous, on a covered host or a git route | A floating ref refuses the session at bind, under the refusal rule below. A full commit skips `ls-refs`, so a missing commit fails at materialization with `SESSION_CREATION_FAILED`, after the claim. |
| VCS pool with `userAuthorization: off` | The tenant's App or stored token | As above. Any user who can start a session on the runtime gets the repository's content, which the tenant's allowlist already gives a session on that pool. |
| VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, human root session | The App or link token that the Token Service mints over the pod credential API, after the session user's link passes the repository authorization check | `LINKS_REQUIRED`, the combined missing-links response with the link under `details.userLinks`, or `USER_REPOSITORY_ACCESS_DENIED`. Nothing falls through to the App, and no session runs without the clone. |
| The same, listed service identity | The App, under the subject's own allowlist | Refused when the repository lies outside that allowlist. |
| The same, unlisted service caller | None | Refused, as [`user-git-identity-design.md`, "Service identities"](user-git-identity-design.md#service-identities) states. |
| Delegated child | The parent's user, on the child's row | As for that user, and the delegation fails. |

A refusal caused by a default clone goes through its own error writer, which carries `details.source: runtimeDefault`, the clone's `path`, and a reason, and omits `url`, `ref`, `host`, `matchingPools`, and the underlying error text. `writeRefResolveError` and `writeVCSResolveError` return those fields today, and a repository later moved or made private would otherwise show its name to any user of the runtime. [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) names the repository because the user declared it, and here a tenant admin named it. The combined missing-links response, `LINKS_REQUIRED`, keeps the `linkUrl` under `details.userLinks`, which names the provider alone. A user who passes sees the URL and commit in `GET /v1/sessions/{id}`.

A runtime can therefore become unusable for some users. A write answers with a `Warning` when a default clone is under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, naming that access now decides who can use the runtime.

### Write scope

The client of a default clone may not know the repository's name, because a tenant admin declared it on the runtime. A client-side `writableRepositories` entry therefore cannot be the only way to push access. `auth.leaseScope` stays a read scope in every case, and `write` is a separate field.

| `write` | Which sessions can push | What the client states | What it needs |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `none`, the default | None through this field. A client that knows the name can still declare it in `writableRepositories`. | Nothing | `allowGitClones` |
| `clientOptIn` | A session whose plan sets `writeDefaultClones: true` | One flag, which names no repository and covers every clone with `write: clientOptIn` on the runtime | `allowGitClones` |
| `always` | Every session on the runtime | Nothing. A transparent client sends no plan, so this is its only way to push access | `allowGitCloneWrites` in the grant, off by default, `allowedRefPatterns` on the clone, and a pool with no `prAuthoring: user` |

**At write.** A `write` value other than `none` carries these conditions, each answering `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`:

- The clone carries `auth`, and its repository lies under a VCS pool prefix whose pool sets `allowWrite`.
- "One session per pod" above applies.
- `write: always` carries `allowedRefPatterns`, each a literal prefix under `refs/heads/` with at least one named segment, such as `refs/heads/lenny/*`. With no client statement, a prompt-injected agent in any session would otherwise reach the default branch.
- `write: always` is refused under a pool with `prAuthoring: user`. A writable entry there attaches the user's binding, which acts with a token derived from the user's own authority, so that attach needs a statement from the client. `clientOptIn` is that statement, and its write answers with a `Warning` naming the attach.
- `derivedRuntimes.forbid`, the guardrail DR-7 adds, allows it: `writableDefaultClones` refuses both values, and `alwaysWritableClones` refuses `always` alone.

**At bind.** Composition first settles whether the value applies to this session, under the rules below, and only then adds the repository to the stored plan's `writableRepositories`, with the clone's patterns. A clone that reads as read-only therefore triggers no attach. An added entry is an ordinary writable entry: the allowlist and `allowWrite` checks, the repository authorization check, the attach rule of `prAuthoring`, `netagent`'s enforcement, and `git.operation` records all apply, and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` shows it. A user with no link on a clone with `write: clientOptIn` under `prAuthoring: user` gets `USER_LINK_REQUIRED`, because the client asked for the write.

- **A user who can read and not write.** The session continues read-only, and its response lists `readOnlyDefaultClones` with each clone's `path` and a reason, omitting the repository's name. The client did not name the repository, and the read is what the runtime needs. A repository the client itself names in `writableRepositories` keeps the refusal [`user-git-identity-design.md`](user-git-identity-design.md) states. The same rule covers an `allowWrite` lowered after the write, a `pendingApproval` entry, and a clone with `write: always` whose pool later turns on `prAuthoring: user`.
- **A delegated child.** A child gets write access under `clientOptIn` or `always` only when its parent's writable set holds the same repository and any `allowedRefPatterns` on the clone lie within the parent's entry, and it is read-only otherwise, in any environment. A clone with no patterns takes the parent's entry's, so a parent limited to `refs/heads/alice/*` cannot delegate a push to `main`. A read-only parent could otherwise delegate to a runtime with `write: always` and push through it.
- **A derive.** A derive composes nothing, so it copies the source's writable entries, under [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md). An entry a default clone added follows this rule on the new session, so it reads as read-only on a target that reuses pods, and when its pool has since turned on `prAuthoring: user`. The derive response (`POST /v1/sessions/{id}/derive`) lists such an entry in `readOnlyDefaultClones`, with the clone's `path` and a reason and without the repository's name, and lists every other read-only entry in `readOnlyRepositories`, as [`session-derive-design.md`, "Writes"](session-derive-design.md#writes) states.
- **A runtime route.** A route target brings its `write` values, so a route can add push access to the sessions it moves. A route write naming a target runtime whose default clones carry `write` values answers with a `Warning` naming those runtimes, as [RT-4](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes) states.

### Derive

[`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) states derive for every session, and decision 22 below records the owner's choice. These parts concern default clones:

- A derive copies the source's stored plan, so a default clone travels as a `gitClone` source marked `runtimeDefault`, and the target's clones do not compose. A tenant's later change to the runtime reaches no derived session.
- `from: latest` restores the snapshot and clones nothing. A snapshot archives the whole workspace, and `ArchiveTree` excludes no `.git`, so it holds a clone's commit, local commits, index, and uncommitted changes, whether the source could write or not. A store-backed repository's snapshot leaves out the objects its store holds, and restore fetches them, so a commit since removed from the host fails that derive.
- `from: start` clones each repository at the source's `resolvedCommitSha`, and a store on the target serves it where the URL matches, as it serves any `gitClone` source under "Session" in "Warm stores".
- Every check under "Binding" runs on the new session's user and environment, so a kept tree does not skip the read check.
- A derive runs the check under ["Stops" in that document](session-derive-design.md#stops) on `planOriginRuntimes`, and it is refused onto a runtime whose row is not `registered`, under "Registered rows" in DR-4.

### Environments

A default clone's VCS pool must be visible in the session's environment, and its host must survive that environment's policy. Otherwise the session is refused in that environment. The write's `Warning` lists each admitting environment where the check would refuse. Where the rule under "Pods with no per-session policy" applies, it reads the composed plan, so a concurrent owned pool whose default clone names a scoped repository refuses the session as it would for a client's plan. A warm-list entry, whoever declared it, must resolve with no environment, as "Tenant shared assets" and "Scoped repositories" under "Warm stores" state, because its store fills on a pod that has no session and therefore no environment.

### A clone is code

A default clone reaches an exempt base command and the runtime's workspace configuration as a default file does. `allowGitClones` therefore has the conditions of `allowWorkspaceFiles`: the grant flag, and DR-3 landed.

A clone adds one risk a file does not have. Whoever can push to the named ref runs code at the slot UID in every new session on that runtime, and Lenny records no write by that person. A default file changes only through a tenant admin's audited write. A full commit removes this risk, and `derivedRuntimes.forbid: floatingCloneRefs` requires one. Each session's stored `resolvedCommitSha` records what it ran.

### Materialization and cost

A default clone whose URL no store on the pod holds is fetched at materialization, after the pod is claimed. One whose URL a store holds fetches the difference from it and, when the entry carries `prestage: true`, adopts the tree staged before bind, as "Warm stores" states. A tenant admin warms a repository through a `gitRepository` asset in the runtime's `sharedAssets`, and also avoids a slow first fetch through the mirror prefetch resource that [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Scopes and who manages them"](egress-and-git-design.md#scopes-and-who-manages-them), defines.

- A floating ref adds an `ls-refs` call per session create and makes create depend on the egress path. A full commit avoids both.
- The composed plan counts toward §7.1 **Bind bounds.**, and the pool's fetch retry window, how long `netagent` retries the git mirror for a repository whose fetch is pending, covers every clone of a plan together, so defaults use up a client's share of each.
- [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) refuses a runtime write that raises the setup bound, the runtime's `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds`, past what a pool's window admits, and a tenant's `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds` is such a write. The refusal goes to the tenant, on its own write. A platform write meets the same refusal under the exception DP9 states (decision 40): a base write that raises the setup bound, or a template write that raises the window, is refused when any pool, platform or tenant-owned, would no longer fit, and the refusal names those pools and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` at which they fit.

### Tenant shared assets

Proposal DR-11 carries this part of the design. The owner settled it on 2026-11-14 as decision 42. A tenant-derived runtime's `sharedAssets` takes the three entry types an operator's Runtime takes, appended to the base's, and they render only on the runtime's owned pool:

```yaml
sharedAssets:
  - type: inline
    path: acme/style-guide.md
    content: "..."
  - type: artifact
    destPath: acme/models/classifier.onnx
    digest: sha256:4f1c...
  - type: gitRepository
    name: monorepo
    url: https://github.com/acme/monorepo
    ref: main
    prestage: true
```

**Owned pool only.** Tenant assets reach only pods that serve the owner alone (DP4). Every tenant-derived runtime has its owned pool under DR-5, and the PoolScalingController renders the runtime's assets onto that pool's pod sets and onto no other pool, the template pool included. A write that would place a tenant asset on a pool whose `ownerTenantId` is not the writing tenant answers `422 TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL`. DR-5 leaves no such pool for a tenant-derived runtime, so the code is an internal guard that no request reaches today, and it keeps the rule explicit if a later change gives such a runtime another pool. Its test is a unit test of the render, and the error catalog lists it as an internal guard.

**`inline` and `artifact` entries.** These place read-only files under `/lenny/assets` in every slot of the owned pool's pods, which the adapter writes before READY as it does a base's ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). A `POST` or `PUT` refuses an entry with `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` and `details.field` unless all of these hold:

- The Helm value `runtimes.tenantDerivation.allowAssets` is true, the grant entry sets `allowAssets`, and DR-3 has landed. Both values are false by default, so a tenant places files under `/lenny/assets` only when the operator offers them on the deployment and grants them to that tenant, and a file there is code, as "Tenant defaults" states for a default file.
- The path, an `inline` entry's `path` or an `artifact` entry's `destPath`, is relative, contains no `..` component and no `.` component, does not begin with `.lenny`, and names no `.git` component, compared case-insensitively.
- The entry appends. A path equal to, above, or below a path one of the base's assets holds, in any revision in the base's traffic, is refused, so a tenant file never replaces or shadows an operator's file, against §5.1's merge rule that lets a derived runtime replace a conflicting `destPath`. Two of the runtime's own entries cannot overlap either.
- An `inline` entry's content is at most `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxInlineAssetBytes`, 1 MiB by default and at most 4 MiB, and the runtime's `inline` entries together are at most 4 MiB, because their content lives in the derived runtime's row. Larger content goes through an `artifact` entry. An `artifact` entry's size is at most `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetEntryBytes`, and the runtime's `inline` and `artifact` entries together are at most `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetsBytes`.
- An `artifact` entry carries `digest` and `destPath` alone, and the digest is that of an artifact uploaded for this runtime. `POST /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}/artifacts` takes the bytes, chunks them under IC-2 into the packs of the runtime's tenant-owned assets set under the tenant's key, and answers with the digest and size. A digest is scoped to the derived runtime the upload route names, so an entry naming a digest uploaded for another runtime is refused. An entry that carries `ref`, the field with which an operator's `artifact` entry names an object, answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `details.field`. The adapter places the file whole at `destPath`, reassembled from the packs, and never extracts it, whatever its content. The entry is pinned to that digest, so a later upload changes no pod set until a write names the new digest, and the size the registry records for the pod set's `assetsBytes` comes from the upload.

**Uploads.** An uploaded artifact is stored once: the packs it was chunked into at upload are its only copy, and a pod's contribution of the file to the tenant-owned assets set finds its chunks already there. A tenant's stored upload bytes are at most the grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes`, which the operator sets per tenant and which reads `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetUploadBytes`, 1 GiB by default, when the grant omits it (decision 43), and an upload past it answers `429 STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` with `details.currentBytes` and `details.limitBytes`, as an upload past the tenant's storage quota does. An upload that no version of its runtime still in use references, neither the runtime's current `sharedAssets` nor the append of a pod set of its owned pool, is deleted by reference-based garbage collection after a grace period of 7 days, which keeps an upload that a tenant has made and not yet named. Uploads are deleted with their derived runtime and with the tenant. A deletion removes the upload's reference, and the set's garbage collection reclaims each chunk that no other reference holds. `inline` content lives in the derived runtime's row, under the bounds above, and is not billed apart from that row. The packs of the tenant-owned assets set, which hold the uploads and the pods' contributions of `inline` files, are billed to the tenant once per pack ([`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`, "Checkpoint packs and platform chunk sets"](billing-resource-attribution-design.md#storageusage)).

The files are data class Confidential under [TSS-8](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-8-data-classes). Uploaded artifacts are stored only as packs of the runtime's tenant-owned assets set under the tenant's key, deleted with the runtime and with the tenant by §12.8 erasure, and kept while a legal hold covers the tenant. A tenant-derived runtime's `inline` and `artifact` entries form a tenant-owned assets set of the platform chunk sets, which its uploads seed and to which the owned pool's never-launched pods contribute, which only the tenant's sessions of that runtime reference, and which is billed to the tenant ([`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`, "Platform chunk sets"](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#platform-chunk-sets)). Each file is readable from every slot of every pod of the owned pool, and every session on the runtime can read and copy it, so an asset must not hold a secret, which the tenant documentation states. A runtime can load a file under `/lenny/assets` as configuration, so a tenant asset is treated as code, as "Tenant defaults" states for a default file: `derivedRuntimes.forbid: workspaceFiles` also refuses `inline` and `artifact` assets, and `verbatimClientFiles: forbid` therefore refuses them as well.

**`gitRepository` entries.** A tenant's `gitRepository` asset declares a store on the owned pool's warm list, and it is the tenant's only way to warm a repository. It replaces the warm default clone and the cache-only entry of earlier revisions, and it carries the checks those had. Each entry carries `name`, `url`, `ref`, and optional `prestage`, and no `auth`, `path`, `write`, `depth`, or `submodules`. A write naming `path` answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, because `prestage: true` replaced it (decision 42). Its fill uses the VCS pool that covers the repository. A `platform-admin` who wants a private repository warmed on one tenant's owned pool writes the entry on that tenant-derived runtime through `PUT /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}`, which [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain) mirrors. The stored entry records its declarer from the role of the writer: `platform` for an entry a `platform-admin` writes, and `tenant` for one a tenant admin writes. An entry that a write leaves unchanged keeps the declarer recorded when it was written. A `POST` or `PUT` refuses an entry with `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` and `details.field` naming it unless all of these hold:

- The grant entry's `maxWarmCloneBytes` is above zero. For a `tenant`-declared entry, the grant entry also sets `allowGitClones`, DR-3 has landed, as "A clone is code" requires of a default clone, and the runtime's default clones and `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets together are within the grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones`.
- `name` is unique among the runtime's `gitRepository` assets and is a valid label value, and no two of them name one normalized URL.
- `url` passes §14's restrictions and the shared canonicalizer, and it carries no userinfo.
- The host is the host of one of the tenant's VCS pools or git routes, and it lies within the owned pool's ceiling.
- A repository under a VCS pool's prefix is on the tenant's repository allowlist.
- `ref` is a well-formed ref name or a full commit, and it cannot begin with `-`, because `lenny-store-fill` passes it to git.
- The owned pool has a git path.
- The repository lies under no VCS pool prefix of the tenant, or credential selection with no environment (E = ∅) resolves it to a VCS pool with no `environments` list whose allowlist names it, as "Scoped repositories" under "Warm stores" states. A pod with no session has no environment, so both the mirror route and the direct route ask the Token Service with E = ∅, and the Token Service refuses a pool that E = ∅ cannot see. Without this check the fill would fail on every pod and the runtime would serve no session.
- A repository under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` needs an owned pool that does not reuse pods, as "One session per pod" states.

`derivedRuntimes.forbid: gitClones` also refuses `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets. A `gitRepository` asset adds no source to any session's plan: its store serves only a `gitClone` source that a default clone or the client's plan already holds, and a prestaged tree that no such source adopts is discarded. A `tenant`-declared asset still needs `allowGitClones` and DR-3, as the cache-only entry it replaces did, because its tree is staged in each idle slot's workspace and a session adopts it as the checkout of its repository.

**Platform-declared assets.** A `gitRepository` asset that a `platform-admin` writes keeps the protections that decision 35 gives an operator's entry, because the tenant cannot fix its cause. A pod whose fill it failed counts in no term of the tenant's charge T ("Bounds"), its failure alerts the operator, `GET` reports it to the tenant admin as `blockedBy: platformEntry`, and a host the tenant denies suspends it with the reason `tenantDenied` in place of failing the tenant's pods ("Reach before binding"). It does not count toward `maxDefaultGitClones`, the tenant's `derivedRuntimes.forbid: gitClones` does not refuse it, and it needs neither `allowGitClones` nor DR-3, because each of those bounds the tenant's own input. Every other check above applies to it, and the read checks of "Who reaches a store" run on it as on a `tenant`-declared asset, because its store is private to the owner's pool, as decision 39 read-checked a private platform entry. A tenant's `PUT` cannot remove or change it. `GET` lists such an asset with `declarer: platform`, and a body that carries it unchanged, every field equal to the stored entry's and `declarer` absent or `platform`, is accepted, so a tenant can write back what it read (decision 43). A body that names one, by its `name` or its URL, with any field changed answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`, and a body that leaves it out keeps it as stored, because the tenant cannot remove it. Only a `platform-admin`'s write changes or removes it.

### Warm stores

A `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets` fills a store on each of the pool's pods before any session, so any `gitClone` source of that repository later fetches only the difference between the store's commit and the commit it binds. The store is fetched once per pod and shared read-only by every slot on that pod. It holds objects, and under the reflink rebuild one pristine checked-out tree. Each session's working tree is its own: with `prestage: true` the warm-up checkout stages the repository's tree in each idle slot's workspace at warm-up and after each slot release, and at bind `lenny-materialize` adopts that tree for the first source that the store serves, so setup commands still run per session. A store changes only where the bytes come from: every check under "Binding" runs as it does without it, and the session's commit is still resolved at bind.

**Warm list.** Every pre-filled repository store on a pod comes from one warm list that the PoolScalingController renders for the pool, and, once [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) lands, for each pool and pod set, onto that pod set's `PodSet` ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)). The base's public entries come from the revision's `sharedAssets`, and the derived runtime's `gitRepository` assets are part of its contribution to the pod-set key. A pod warming for one revision therefore reaches only that revision's entries. One rule set, the one this section states, governs every entry whoever declared it (decision 31). Each rendered entry records the normalized repository URL, the ref, which is a branch, a tag, or a full commit, the store directory, named by a hash of that URL, the declarer, `platform` or `tenant`, and `prestage`, which is true when the warm-up checkout stages the repository in each idle slot. Entries come from these declarers (decisions 32 and 42):

- **The operator, on a base.** A public `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime's `sharedAssets` goes onto the warm list of every pool of that runtime, the owned pools of runtimes derived from it included. `sharedAssets` is versioned, so the entry reaches the pod sets of the revisions that carry it. Operator entries on a Runtime are public only, so a Runtime write whose entry carries `auth` is refused. No entry adds a source to a session's plan. An entry may carry `prestage: true`, and the warm-up checkout then stages the repository in each idle slot, as "Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild" states. The owner settled staging on platform pools on 2026-11-13 as option (a) of the question of what a platform pool stages, with a target `path`, and replaced the `path` with `prestage: true` on 2026-11-14 (decision 42). An entry carries no path field, so `prestage` is unrelated to the `destPath` that draft 0111 gave an entry for the store's location, which stays dropped.
- **The tenant-derived runtime.** Its `gitRepository` assets, under "Tenant shared assets", each with the declarer recorded from the role that wrote it: `tenant` for a tenant admin's asset, and `platform` for a `platform-admin`'s private entry for that one tenant, which keeps decision 35's protections under "Platform-declared assets". The `warmRepositories` field on an owned pool's row and the `pool.warmCaches` field of earlier revisions are gone, because a store comes only from `sharedAssets` (decision 42). A pool write naming `warmRepositories`, a derived-runtime write naming `pool.warmCaches`, a runtime write naming `workspaceDefaults.gitClones[].warm`, and a write naming a `gitRepository` entry's `path` each answer `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, under the rule for removed fields of [`runtime-revisions-design.md` D27](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions).

Entries naming one normalized URL share one store on the pod, which is filled once. The runtime's own entry's ref wins over the base's entry's, and a runtime holds at most one entry per URL among its `gitRepository` assets. The store's declarer is `tenant` when either entry is `tenant`-declared and `platform` otherwise, and the store is staged when either entry carries `prestage: true`. A store serves a source at another commit through a larger delta, because git is content-addressed. One URL that a base's public entry and a `gitRepository` asset of the runtime both declare is still two sources of the platform chunk sets of [IC-4](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#platform-chunk-sets), and the trees the warm-up checkout stages from their shared store always feed the tenant-owned set of that URL, whichever entry carries `prestage: true`. A `platform`-declared asset's store feeds the tenant-owned set as well, because it serves the owner alone, its fill runs on the tenant's identity, and its content is a repository private to that tenant, which [TSS-8](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-8-data-classes) keeps under the tenant key. Only a store that a base's public entry alone declares feeds the platform-owned set. Decisions 23 to 27 apply to an operator's store as they do to a tenant's, and the age limit stays unset by default on platform pools, as "Age" states.

**Carrier.** The PoolScalingController renders the warm list on `PodSet.spec.warmClones` for every pool, from the public `gitRepository` entries of the revision's `sharedAssets`, which for an owned pool are the base's, and, on an owned pool, from the derived runtime's `gitRepository` assets. That one carrier serves platform pools and owned pools, so the base's entries no longer reach the adapter through `encodeSharedAssets`. The owned pool row copies `maxWarmCloneBytes` from the grant entry, and the `PodSet` carries the pool's store bytes as `warmCloneBytes`, as "Size" states. `createPod` encodes the list onto the flags of `lenny-store-fill` and the adapter, and it records the store directories on the `AgentPod` as `warmStores`. The WarmPoolController also stamps a new `lenny.dev/owner-tenant` label on the `AgentPod`, which is what the creation rule under "Bounds" lists by, because `agentPodLabels` holds no owner today and the tenant pin label also appears on a shared pool's pods. A derived runtime has no `Runtime` resource, and `encodeSharedAssets` reads that resource, so 0111's carrier cannot serve here. A CRD field pattern refuses userinfo in the URL as a second gate, as 0111 states for its own field.

**Fill.** Before the pod reports ready, the `lenny-store-fill` init container fills a new bare repository for each store on the list, whoever declared its entry, on a dedicated `emptyDir`, `lenny-git-stores`, which it mounts read-write and every slot container mounts read-only at `/lenny/git-stores` ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). The owner settled the container on 2026-11-13 as decision 61 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), in the words "if the separate container works from a permissions standpoint, then yes", so the fill keeps [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway)'s rule that no platform-privileged process parses untrusted content, with no exception. The container runs after the `netagent` native sidecar has started, under its own UID, which maps inside the pod's UID range under user namespaces, with no service account token and no credential volume, and with the pinned git from `lenny-bin` mounted read-only and exec-only. It fills the stores and writes each pristine tree of the reflink rebuild, then exits before the adapter and the slot containers start. `netagent` attributes the container's connections to the platform fill by its UID and applies the fill's authorization, so the fill fetches through `netagent` on the session git path with the identity stated below. The adapter mounts the same volume read-write at `/adapter/git-stores` for one purpose, the deletion of a withheld store under "Withheld stores", and it never parses repository data. The fill works on stores, which no slot can write, so it is not git on a slot repository, which [SI O11](session-isolation-design.md#open-decisions) keeps out of the adapter. `lenny-store-fill` writes with the store group, the dedicated store GID of [PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction), which is the primary group of both `lenny-store-fill` and the adapter and of no other container, as the file group and a umask of `0002`, so the stores' directories are mode `0775` and their object files `0444`, and after each pristine tree's checkout it runs an explicit mode pass that sets the tree's directories to `0775`, its files to `0444`, and its executables to `0555`, whatever modes git's checkout wrote, and clears every setuid and setgid bit. Nothing grants write to the other class, every slot reads them through the other class ([SI-8](session-isolation-design.md#si-8-shared-repository-stores)), no slot container holds the store group, and the adapter, whose primary group it is, deletes a withheld store and its pristine tree through the group class. Every agent-pod container runs with `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, so a setuid or setgid bit in a repository has no effect even before the mode pass clears it. On a Kata `block-plain` pool, whose volume root carries the setgid bit and the dedicated volume GID, `lenny-store-fill` sets each store's directory and its pristine tree's directory to the store group before it writes into them, as PL-5 states. The owner accepted this hardening on 2026-11-13 under decision 61 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), in the words "ok but add an owed item to stress test this thoroughly (with actual tests)". A bare store records no absolute path, so it serves alternates at `/lenny/git-stores` in the slots whatever path the fill wrote it at. The fill ignores hooks and repository configuration. On an owned pool the owner's label is on the pod from creation, so the fill runs as the tenant. On a shared pool the pod has no tenant, and a public entry fills anonymously through the mirror's public store or `direct`, as the egress design states for a warm fill. The store records its commit, resolved from a floating ref or fetched by SHA for a full one, as its only ref, because git offers the ref tips of an alternate, an object store a repository borrows objects from through `objects/info/alternates`, as haves. The store is immutable from then on. Whether these permissions hold on every runtime class, and whether the container's 1 GiB memory limit holds for the largest store and pristine tree a pool's `warmCloneBytes` admits, is owed to validation, as the egress design's decision 61 lists, together with the hostile-repository stress suite the owner asked for, run as tests on runc, gVisor, and Kata.

- **Size.** The volume's `sizeLimit` is `warmCloneBytes`, and the pod's ephemeral-storage request includes that value, because the controller renders the request as the sum of the `sizeLimit`s of the pod's disk-backed volumes plus the resource class's own request ([PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction)). The scheduler and the quota then count it. `lenny-store-fill` ends a fill that passes the limit, `sizeLimit` is the second line, and a breach evicts this pod alone. Every store is sized, whoever declared it. On a platform pool a platform admin sets `warmCloneBytes` on the pool row. On an owned pool it is the template's value, which covers the base's entries, plus the grant entry's `maxWarmCloneBytes`, which covers the runtime's `gitRepository` assets. Both figures count each store together with its pristine tree under the reflink rebuild, which `lenny-store-fill` writes on the same volume. Neither a tenant's input nor an operator's can leave node storage unsized.
- **Share of the quota.** Every agent pod requests ephemeral storage equal to the sum of its disk-backed volumes' `sizeLimit` values ([PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction), decision D3 of [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#decisions)) plus its resource class's own `requests.ephemeral-storage`, 1 GiB in the shipped classes and set per class ([decision 18 of `billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md#decisions), settled on 2026-11-13 as option (a) of BR-Q1), so every pod counts against `lenny-agent-storage-quota`, and a warm list adds its `warmCloneBytes` to that request. The warm stores are the part a grant controls, and one grant could use the quota and leave other tenants' pods refused. A grant write that raises the sum across grants of `maxOwnedPods` times the entry's largest `maxWarmCloneBytes`, plus that entry's template `warmCloneBytes`, is refused above `warmClones.totalBytes`, under the grant lock, and preflight compares that total, together with the other disk-backed volumes every pool's pods request, with the storage quota. Any other grant write passes, including an emergency `retireAt`, so a lowered Helm value blocks only raises, and `GET` reports the excess. A template write that raises `warmCloneBytes` succeeds under DP9, and `GET` reports that excess as well. Pods created before a lowering hold their requests until they end. A reservation does not hold against storage, as DR-10 states.
- **Checkout.** A commit the store holds causes no fetch, so the fetch cap does not bound what a checkout writes, and a small pack can expand far past its size, because a tree can reuse subtrees. Before every checkout, `lenny-materialize` streams the tree's listing under a git directory of its own, as the slot UID, and it stops at the first of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries`, or the run's deadline with the slot-reported reason `git_tree_limit`, which the adapter maps to `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` ([WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)). Each entry is charged as 0111's rule 2 charges it, each blob rounded up to 4,096 bytes plus 128 bytes per entry, and the total runs across the plan's clones, so empty files and repeated subtrees count, and several clones cannot sum past the limit. A clone without a store runs this after its fetch, which the fetch cap bounds. This takes the place of 0111's rule 2 bound, which ran in the gateway's mirror. The helper's result is slot-reported, so the adapter's per-slot disk watch, which fails the slot's session at `adapter.diskWatchThresholdPercent` of the workspace volume's `sizeLimit`, 90% by default, is the backstop, and the volume's `sizeLimit` is the last line ([SI-10](session-isolation-design.md#si-10-what-stays-shared)). The warm-up checkout runs the same check against the empty slot's bytes. A `prestage` run whose check stops an entry, or that reaches the pod's prestage timeout, the runtime's `prestageTimeoutSeconds` or, without one, the pool's value or `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`, skips that entry in that slot, and the slot still reports ready: a session that clones the repository there builds it from the store, as [WM-15](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-15-warm-up-checkout-and-reflink-rebuild) states, and the skip is counted in `lenny_prestage_skipped_total` and reported as a `PrestageSkipped` event on the `AgentPod`. A runtime write, a tenant's or an operator's, answers with a `Warning` when the staged trees of its entries with `prestage: true`, as the latest `prestage` runs on its pools measured them, together exceed half of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, because each staged tree counts against every idle slot's volume, and a session that does not adopt it keeps its bytes until the background deletion ends.
- **Reach before binding.** The decision service and the Token Service read the warm list from the pool row, and, once revisions land, for the pod's pool and revision. While a pod has no session, its readable set is the warm list of its pool and revision plus the repositories of the warm-up policy that applies to it, which is the pool's warm-up policy on an unbound pod and the tenant's warm-up additions on a tenant-bound pod. Its tokens are read tokens alone, whatever `userAuthorization` says, and a repository off the tenant's allowlist has already left the rendered list. The hosts of every entry join the pod's pre-session policy within the ceiling, and a tenant deny still wins. A tenant entry under a host the tenant denies fails its fill, which is reported. A platform-declared entry, a base's public entry or a `platform`-declared asset, whose host the owning tenant denies is left off that owned pool's rendered list and reported as suspended with the reason `tenantDenied`, so the tenant's own policy never fails its pods on an operator's entry. On a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, the fill uses the App, as [`user-git-identity-design.md`, "Mirror, warm fills, and the 6-hour promise"](user-git-identity-design.md#mirror-warm-fills-and-the-6-hour-promise) states. Repositories on providers whose capability level is `mint-coarse` or `stored` skip the mirror under the [egress design](egress-and-git-design.md)'s routing rule, so each pod fetches them from the host.
- **Failure.** A fill that fails takes the pod to `failed`, as 0111 states, with no fallback to a ready pod lacking its store. `lenny-store-fill` stops at the first store whose fill fails and names it in its termination message, and the AgentPod controller records that store's directory and its entry's declarer in `fillFailure`, so a pod has one cause. After `warmClones.failureThreshold` consecutive failures of one store, whoever declared its entry, the WarmPoolController backs off creating that pool's pods while that store is on the list, doubling up to 10 minutes, and sets `WarmCloneFillFailing` with the declarer as a label. The backoff applies on platform pools and owned pools alike. `GET` reports `warm.state` and `blockedBy` with a reason to the tenant admin: `gitRepositoryAsset` with the `name` of a `tenant`-declared asset, or `platformEntry` with the URL of the base's public entry or the `name` of a `platform`-declared asset. An alert for a platform-declared entry goes to the operator, and a `TenantReservationUnmet` that such an entry causes names that cause. A pod that a platform-declared entry failed counts in no term of T, as "Bounds" states (decision 35). The backoff is keyed by store directory, so removing the failing `gitRepository` asset is the immediate way out, and a loop of writes cannot reset it.
- **Shared capacity.** The WarmPoolController creates no `AgentPod` with a warm list while the owner has `warmClones.maxConcurrentFillsPerTenant` pods running a fill, which leaves out a Pending pod, so that another tenant's burst at the quota cannot hold this limit, so one tenant's fills do not take over `lenny-egress`. Under decision 38, a pod on an owned pool that fills only platform-declared entries counts toward this limit, because its fill runs on the tenant's egress identity. A shared pool has no tenant to key the limit on, and the rewrite of 0111 states its fill concurrency. `controller.tuning.gitStoreFillTimeout` bounds each fill.

**Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild.** The owner approved both on 2026-11-12, and [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) owns their mechanics. This section states how they meet the warm list **[design]**:

- **What is staged.** Every rendered entry that carries `prestage: true`, from the revision the pod's template names, whoever declared it: on every pool the operator's public entries, and on an owned pool the runtime's `gitRepository` assets as well (decision 42). An entry without `prestage` fills a store and stages nothing. A staged tree adds no source to a session's plan: a session adopts it only when its workspace clones that repository.
- **When and how.** At warm-up and after every slot release, the adapter asks the supervisor of each idle slot to run `lenny-materialize` as the slot UID, which checks each such entry out of its store into `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/<entry>` with the steps a session's materialization runs, including the tree-size check, and refreshes the index more than 1 s after the checkout. No runtime process runs in an idle slot, because every runtime launches at bind.
- **At bind.** The first `gitClone` source of the session's workspace, in the composed order of the defaults and then the plan, whose URL the entry's store serves and that a store may serve, which leaves out a source with `depth` or `submodules: true`, adopts the staged tree by renaming it to that source's `path`. A source in the plan has passed its read checks or refused the session, so a session adopts only a tree of a repository it may read, and a tree staged from a store withheld for the session is removed before anything runs, as "Withheld stores" states. When the session's resolved commit differs from the staged one, the helper checks the commit out in place. When the target already holds files, such as a default file written earlier, the existing per-path collision rule applies, as it does for a clone. A second source of the same URL in one workspace clones from the store. A staged tree that no source adopts, such as one for a session that does not clone the repository, is renamed aside and deleted in the background, and checkpoints skip the reserved names. [WM-15](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-15-warm-up-checkout-and-reflink-rebuild) states the adoption steps.
- **Why the entry names no path.** The owner asked, on 2026-11-14: "does prestage need a path? Can't the path be determined at the time of copying (based on workspaceDefaults and the session plan)?" It does not. The adoption is a rename within the slot's workspace volume, so the files keep their inode numbers and modification times and the index the warm-up checkout refreshed stays valid at the new path, and nothing in a staged tree records its own location: its alternates name the store by its absolute path under `/lenny/git-stores`. The target folder is therefore read from the composed workspace at bind.
- **Reflink rebuild.** The store volume also holds one pristine checked-out tree per store that serves a staged entry, which `lenny-store-fill` writes with the store. Where the filesystem supports reflinks (runc on XFS or btrfs), the helper creates the slot's files as the slot UID and clones their content from the pristine tree with `FICLONE`, so each slot's copy is copy-on-write and independent. On gVisor and Kata it falls back to a normal checkout.
- **Reach.** Staging reads only stores already on the pod, so it adds no fetch and nothing a slot could not already read. The read checks of "Who reaches a store" still run at bind, and a staged tree from a store withheld for the session is removed before anything runs for the session, as "Withheld stores" states.

**Scoped repositories.** A warm-list entry of either declarer is refused at write unless its repository lies under no VCS pool prefix of the tenant, or credential selection with no environment (E = ∅) resolves it to a VCS pool with no `environments` list whose allowlist names it (decision 36). A write of a `gitRepository` asset on a tenant-derived runtime, by a tenant admin or a platform admin, answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`. A public entry on a Runtime reaches the owned pools of several tenants, and refusing the Runtime write over one tenant's VCS pool would let a tenant block a platform write, against R6. That write therefore passes with a `Warning` naming the owned pools on which the entry renders suspended. A later VCS pool write that makes an entry fail the predicate, such as one that adds `environments` to the covering pool or deletes or narrows a tenant-wide pool that covered it, is never refused for that reason. It passes with a `Warning` naming each runtime and entry it affects. The PoolScalingController applies the same predicate against the current pools at each render, so the entry leaves the rendered list, and `GET` reports it as suspended with the reason `environmentScoped`. A default clone of that repository stays on the runtime and materializes cold for the sessions that pass its checks. "Changing the list" then drains idle pods holding the store, and `coveredStores` refuses such pods at the next claim. The entry returns to the list once the predicate holds again, such as when the pool is unscoped. A repository removed from the allowlist has already left the rendered list.

**Session.** Materialization follows 0111's rule 3, as [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) rewrites its transport and [WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git) places it in `lenny-materialize`. The helper runs, as the slot UID with the pinned git, `git init` in a new directory, writes the store's objects directory under `/lenny/git-stores` to `objects/info/alternates` as an absolute path, fetches `resolvedCommitSha` through `netagent` unless it is already reachable, checks it out detached, and moves the result under "Not verified" below, or adopts a staged tree under "Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild". The adapter runs no git on the slot repository. Before a helper run that may fetch, the adapter registers a fetch grant with `netagent` naming the slot, the URLs, and a window ending at the run's deadline, so `netagent` treats the helper's fetch as materialization rather than as the session's own ([WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)), and [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Slot attribution and fetch grants"](egress-and-git-design.md#slot-attribution-and-fetch-grants) owns the grant and its carrier. A store serves any `gitClone` source in the plan whose normalized URL equals the store's, whoever wrote the source, whether a client, an operator's or a tenant's default clone, or a derive's copy, unless the source sets `depth` or `submodules: true` (decisions 33 and 42). The adapter decides this from the stores the pod holds and names the store in the helper plan. Serving a client's source reads nothing new, because every store on a pod is readable from every slot, and a session holds a pod only after passing the read checks of every tenant store on it or with that store withheld. A restore covers it as well: a derive copies the stored plan, so a client's source is in the new session's plan as a default clone is, and restore completion fetches the plan's repositories, as "Restore" states. A new session on a pod with no store for the URL fetches that commit shallow, as 0111's fallback does, with no alternates. A store changes where the bytes come from, and a tree from a shallow fetch has no history before its commit, which `depth` already allows a client to choose. A commit the store already holds needs no network, so `netagent` sees no request for it, and the bind checks are the only gate on that read. That is why the host, prefix, and repository authorization checks run at bind on every path, including for a full commit.

**Who reaches a store.** At create, and on every other path that claims a pod, the gateway runs the read checks on every tenant entry of the final pool's warm list for the chosen revision, whether or not the plan names the repository, as "Binding" states (decision 34). In this section and under "Binding", a tenant entry means every `gitRepository` asset of the tenant-derived runtime, a `platform`-declared one included, because each is private to the owner's pool. A `gitClone` source of the composed plan that names an entry's repository covers it, because that source's own checks are the repository's read checks. The gateway runs the read checks for each entry whose repository the plan does not name, which on a resume, retry, rebuild, or derive includes an entry added after the session bound. The checks are those a `gitClone` source of that repository gets: the environment's visibility and denies, the allowlist, and under `userAuthorization: required` the user's own access, which [`user-git-identity-design.md`, "Mirror, warm fills, and the 6-hour promise"](user-git-identity-design.md#mirror-warm-fills-and-the-6-hour-promise) already demands for a private warm fill. Each claim request carries `coveredStores`, the store directories of every such entry whose checks passed, plus the stores of the pool's public platform entries, which every session may read. It also carries `withheldStores`, the store directories of the entries whose checks failed and that "Withheld stores" withholds. On a path that claims in a later call, such as `MaterializeDelegatedChild`, the checks run against the target pool's list at that call. A pod whose `warmStores` lie outside the two lists together is refused, inside the row lock on the Postgres-backed fallback, so a change between the checks and the claim cannot put a session on a store it was not checked for. A plan source whose checks fail refuses the session, as "Whose credential" states for a default clone, because the session needs its files. A pod claimed at create stays with its session through finalize, because the create's checks covered what it holds. The store is readable from every slot on the pod with its whole history up to its commit, which the read scope already covers.

**Stored with the session.** The gateway writes the claim's `coveredStores` and `withheldStores` on the session row in the transaction that assigns the session to its pod, and a later claim of the same session, such as a resume onto another pod, replaces both with the sets that claim checked. A path that places a session with no claim check, `ClaimSlot`'s first pass or `rebindReserved`, still runs the read checks and stores the sets they produce, so every session row carries its own. The stored `coveredStores` decides which platform chunk sets the session's snapshots and output files may reference: at every checkpoint, export, and resume, the gateway computes the session's eligible sources from it and from the session's runtime revision, as [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`, "Platform chunk sets"](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#platform-chunk-sets) states under decision D1, which the owner settled on 2026-11-13. A store in `withheldStores` is never eligible, because its repository is one the session's user cannot read, and a store the pod holds outside the stored `coveredStores`, which a reused pod can hold under "Reused pods", is not eligible either. The adapter's word never decides eligibility. The row reaches no client: `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` reports `withheldWarmEntries` without URLs, as below.

**Withheld stores.** An entry whose repository the session's plan does not name offers objects that the session does not need, so a failed read check on one withholds its store for that session and does not refuse the session (decision 34). The gateway passes `withheldStores` on the call that assigns the session to the pod, `FinalizeWorkspace` or Resume. Every path makes that call, `ClaimSlot`'s first pass and `rebindReserved` included, so withholding reaches those paths where `coveredStores` does not. Before the adapter materializes anything, runs restore completion, runs a setup command, or starts the runtime, it deletes each listed store directory from `lenny-git-stores` through `/adapter/git-stores`, together with that store's pristine tree under the reflink rebuild, and the `AgentPod`'s `warmStores` drops it. The deletion uses `openat2` and `unlinkat` relative to directory descriptors under `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`, so it follows no symbolic link, and the adapter reads no repository data and never the pristine tree. Its first helper run for the session then removes any tree staged from that store under `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/` before it writes anything else. The slot container's read-only mount at `/lenny/git-stores` is the same `emptyDir`, so the deletion is visible there at once. Kubernetes cannot change a running pod's mounts, so deletion is the only way to withhold a store, and nothing refills it on that pod. The WarmPoolController therefore drains the pod when the session ends, in place of returning it to idle or to a reserved hold. The deletion runs before restore completion, so 0111's rule 6 finds no store for that URL and deletes the alternates file of any restored repository that names it.

The session response and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` list `withheldWarmEntries`, each with the entry's `name` and a reason: `environment`, `userAccess`, `userLinkRequired`, or `providerUnavailable`. The list omits the URL, for the reason "Whose credential" gives: a tenant admin named the repository, and the client did not. The tenant admin's `GET` on the runtime reports, for each entry, the count of sessions that withheld it. Each withholding writes a `warm_store.withheld` record carrying the session, the entry, and the reason. Posture does not change, because withholding narrows access.

These pods refuse the session instead of withholding:

- **Pods with concurrent sessions.** Deleting a store would break the slot repositories of the pod's other sessions, which borrow objects from it, and every slot container mounts the same store volume, so a store cannot be withheld from one slot, which [SI O10](session-isolation-design.md#open-decisions) keeps. A failed check on a pod with `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` above 1 therefore refuses the session, and the adapter refuses an assignment that carries a withhold list onto a pod that already has a session. Every warm-list entry passes the environment rule at E = ∅. A concurrent owned pool refuses a tenant entry under `userAuthorization: required` at write, and the render leaves off such a pool a platform entry under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, so no per-user, link, or provider check runs there. Where `netagent` cannot attribute a connection to its slot, such a pod evaluates every session at E = ∅ and refuses a session whose environment has denies, routes, or objects of its own ([`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`, "Effective policy"](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#effective-policy)), so no read check fails there. Where attribution holds, under decision 60 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), a session evaluates with its own environment, and an environment that denies an entry's host fails that entry's read check. That case is reachable, and the session is refused with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE` naming the entry by its `name`, without its URL, as decision 41 settles. [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) and the egress design's "Facts the pages state" list this case under that code. A tenant whose users meet it gives the environment a derived runtime with `oneSessionPerPod: true`, on which the store is withheld instead.

"Residual risks" states what a deletion leaves on the node's disk.

**Reused pods.** The owner allowed `warm` on a pool that reuses pods, by concurrency or by recycling, in the words "go with your recommendations". `ClaimSlot`'s first pass and `rebindReserved` place a session on a running or held pod with no claim check, so `coveredStores` does not reach them. A repository removed from the runtime or off the allowlist therefore stays readable to new sessions on such a pod until it retires, under `maxPodUptimeSeconds` or when its last session ends, and the age drain, which acts on idle pods alone, does not reach it either. Such a pool cannot serve a clone under `userAuthorization: required`, so its sessions already read through the tenant's App, and no per-user check is lost. A write of a `gitRepository` asset on such a pool answers with a `Warning` stating this and naming `oneSessionPerPod: true`, and the `Warning` states the missing bound when the template has no `maxPodUptimeSeconds`. A template write that makes a pool holding a `gitRepository` asset reuse pods states the same in `runtime.base_changed`. Refusing the pod instead would make a new session need a new pod and could hit the tenant's budget until the old pods empty. Every runtime process launches at bind, so no runtime process reads a store while its slot has no session.

**Changing the list.** The WarmPoolController drains an idle pod holding a store no longer on the rendered list, which follows a removed `gitRepository` asset, a repository off the allowlist, a grant set to 0, an entry removed from the base, and an entry suspended at render, under "Scoped repositories" or for a tenant deny. An entry leaving the rendered list for any reason also leaves `coveredStores`, so pods holding its store are refused at the next claim, at each caller of `AdmitTenantPin`, and that removal always passes. Every other pod stays claimable, because it holds a subset of what the session was checked for. A new entry or a ref change touches no existing pod: new pods fill from the new list, and older pods leave under the age limit. Running sessions keep their stores until they end, apart from a store withheld for that session. Once [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) lands, the runtime's `sharedAssets` append, its `gitRepository` assets included, enters the pod-set key of [RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods), under its decision D13. A change to them then creates a new pod set on the owned pool without creating a base revision, and the rules of this paragraph govern how the earlier pod set's pods leave: an idle pod holding a removed store drains, a pod outside `coveredStores` is refused at the claim, and every other pod stays claimable until it leaves under the age limit. A change to the base's public entries creates a base revision instead, and reaches the owned pool through that revision's rollout.

**Age.** A store never refreshes, so the WarmPoolController drains an idle pod whose store is older than the pool's `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds`, while fewer than `warmClones.maxConcurrentFillsPerTenant` of the pool's pods are running a fill, whether or not replacements succeed. A failed fill, a Pending pod held at the quota, or a pool at its bound therefore does not stop the drain, and a large pool does not take past the limit by draining in series, so this bounds how long an idle pod holds content a host has since removed or a tenant has since lost access to, and it bounds the delta's growth. It also means an outage at the host longer than the limit empties the pool's warm pods. The tenant admin, or a platform admin writing for the tenant, sets `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds` per owned pool, between `warmClones.minAgeSeconds` and `warmClones.maxAgeSeconds`, 3600 and 86400 by default, and an unset value reads `warmClones.defaultMaxAgeSeconds`. The controller reads the value at each drain decision, so a change applies without a pod roll, and a stored value outside lowered Helm bounds reads as the nearest bound. A longer limit keeps content a host has removed, or a repository whose owner has revoked the tenant's access, readable to new sessions on idle pods for longer, because a clone pinned to a full commit that a store holds contacts no host. That content can belong to another organization, so the tenant can only shorten the default, and an operator who raises `warmClones.maxAgeSeconds` accepts the longer window for every tenant. A shorter one costs refill traffic on `lenny-egress`, which `maxConcurrentFillsPerTenant` bounds. A platform admin can also set `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds` on a platform pool, with no Helm bound. Unset there, a store has no age limit, which decision 31 keeps as the default on platform pools. On an owned pool the limit applies to every store on the pod, a platform entry's included.

**Budget.** A warm pod counts as `warmCount` and a failed one as `unusable`, under "Bounds", with no new figure, apart from a pod a platform-declared entry failed, which counts in no term of T. A marked pod that has not finished its fill is not `held`, so `TenantReservationUnmet` reports a reservation a failing fill blocks. DR-10's "Reporting" defines `held` and the alert.

**Restore.** A resume onto another pod follows 0111's rules 5 and 6, which the "Draft 0111" row under "Terms" summarizes, and both run in `lenny-materialize` as the slot UID ([WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)): the capture copy, which the helper's `capture-copy` finishes before a checkpoint's pre-copy pass starts, and restore completion, the helper's `restore-complete`, which overwrites alternates without reading the restored file and fetches `lenny.baseCommit` when the pod's store differs, after the adapter restores the snapshot through the broker. That fetch is shallow first, because a warm repository's history is larger than the slot's `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` by design, and the store holds it outside the slot. Only when 0111's rule 5 listing reports a missing object does the helper fetch history, capped at the slot's remaining bytes. The capture copy leaves out `lenny.baseCommit` and its ancestors, so that case is an agent that amended or reset the pinned commit. Such a repository on a pod with no store, whose history does not fit, fails that resume, on a resume path that also holds a cold fetch to 30 s. Every store-backed repository a session has is a `gitClone` source in its stored plan, whoever wrote the source, so that fetch passes the session's readable set. A derive copies the stored plan, so a derived session's sources stay in its plan, and 0111's lineage rule has no case left here, which the rewrite of 0111 confirms. `lenny.gitStoreUrl` is agent-writable, so restore completion fetches only a URL the gateway passes for that bind, which is the plan's repositories, as [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) states.

### Relationship to draft proposal 0111

For the record, the owner directed that this design build on 0111 and replace it where that makes sense. Draft 0111 is on the branch `draft/0111-warm-workspace`, its status is Draft, and its review did not converge. [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "Relationship to proposal 0111 and open findings"](egress-and-git-design.md#relationship-to-proposal-0111-and-open-findings), already rewrites it around pod-side git and drops `FetchGitPack`, the gateway mirror, and pack serving. This section applies on top of that rewrite.

These parts of 0111 stand for every warm-list entry: a pod-wide read-only bare store filled before ready, one immutable commit, a per-slot repository over alternates that fetches a delta, no worktrees against a store, the capture copy, restore completion, the treatment of an agent-written repository as untrusted content, which [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway)'s rule now states, a failed fill taking the pod to `failed`, no setup pre-running, and `ownerTenantId`.

Earlier revisions held a table here of the places where warm clones departed from 0111: the declaration, the carrier, the full commit, the sized store, the age limit and drain, the backoff, the authorization, and the tree bound. Under decision 31 each of those departures becomes the rule for every entry on a pool's warm list, so the record of differences gives way to what the rewrite of 0111 must do (decision 37). This design does not stage the rewrite, because the owner has not started proposals. The rewrite must do the following:

- Define one warm list per pool and its rule set, as "Warm stores" states. That covers the entry fields (the URL, the ref as a branch, a tag, or a full commit, the declarer, and `prestage`), one store per normalized URL with a tenant entry's ref winning, the carrier `PodSet.spec.warmClones` for every pool, the volume `lenny-git-stores` sized by `warmCloneBytes` with the matching storage request on every pool that renders an entry, the age limit unset by default on platform pools, the drain when an entry leaves the list, claim refusal through `coveredStores`, withholding through `withheldStores`, backoff keyed by store for every declarer with the declarer recorded on the `AgentPod`, the per-tenant fill limit and a fill limit for shared pools, acceptance of a full commit, and the helper's tree bound before checkout. A Runtime or pool write that leaves a rendered entry unsized is refused.
- Make a Runtime's `gitRepository` entry public only, and drop private entries on a Runtime, the ownership key of owner and URL that rule 2 gives a Runtime, and the Runtime-wide ownership checks. A private repository is warmed for one tenant through a `gitRepository` asset on that tenant's derived runtime, under "Tenant shared assets".
- Drop `destPath`, because every store sits on the warm list's volume and a session reaches a store through alternates. The entry's only staging field is `prestage: true`, which names no path.
- Replace rule 1's eligibility with the rule under "Session": a store serves any `gitClone` source whose normalized URL matches it, unless the source sets `depth` or `submodules: true`.
- Confirm that derive's copy of the stored plan leaves the restore lineage no case, and if so drop rule 7 and `REG-GITLINEAGE`.
- State what restore completion does when an agent writes a `lenny.gitStoreUrl` outside the set of URLs the gateway passed for that bind. This design recommends that the adapter write no alternates for it and fetch nothing, so the restore fails when an object is missing, which harms only the agent's own session.
- State that a repository an agent wires to a store through alternates without `lenny.*` keys gets no store service. The capture copy and restore completion do not touch it, its snapshot keeps an absolute alternates path that breaks on a pod whose store differs, and it cannot reach a withheld store, because the deletion runs before anything runs for the session.
- Apply the environment rule under "Scoped repositories" to every entry, at write and at render.

### Not verified

[`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) moves the clone into the pod and does not state how materialization treats a target directory that already holds files. Under DR-12 every clone names its own subfolder, but default files and earlier sources materialize first and can write into that subfolder, so a non-empty target still has to work. A snapshot's content does not meet a clone on a derive, because `from: latest` clones nothing.

The answer is an integrity rule at the slot UID. `lenny-materialize` runs the clone as the slot UID with the pinned git, and it holds no service account token and no VCS token ([WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)). A `.git/config` in the target that sets `core.fsmonitor`, or a hook there, would run tenant-chosen code at the slot UID, which every session file can already reach through the agent, so it no longer reaches the adapter's identity, which DR-3 exists to protect. It could still change the plan's outcome. The `.git` refusal above closes the tenant's way in. The helper clones into a fresh directory under `/workspace/.lenny-build/` with `--template=`, passes `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false` on every command, runs all its git work there, including setting `origin`, and only then moves the result to the target. An existing `.git` at the target is replaced as a unit, because the path collision rule works per path and a planted `commondir` or hook would otherwise survive a merge. The helper runs no git in the target after the move apart from the index refresh, the capture copy, and restore completion, each under the configuration and environment the helper supplies, so nothing an agent wrote takes effect, and the adapter runs no git on the slot repository at all.

**Symbolic links.** The move and every other write the materializer makes run in `lenny-materialize` at the slot UID, into a tree an earlier session's agent may have written or a restore may have seeded. `extractSymlink` and `ValidateSymlinkTarget` check a link's target lexically, so a restored pair such as `a/b/l -> ../..` and `src -> a/b/l/../../outside/src` passes both and resolves outside the workspace. `overlayTree` and `writeInlineFile` then follow it. Every helper write, move, and removal therefore opens its path with `openat2` under `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV` beneath `/workspace`, and a path crossing a link fails the materialization, as [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md) states for every source. Today's writers in the adapter have this hole (DF-15, F-13.4.24), so the fix does not wait for this design.

**Warm clone spike, 2026-11-05.** Its evidence is under `evidence/tenant-derived-runtimes-design/spike-warm/`. No Go `netagent` exists, so a stand-in for its git path terminated TLS for every host under a per-run CA, re-originated over HTTP/1.1 against public roots, and logged each request's wants and haves. It ran git 2.49.1 against public repositories, anonymously, with the store mounted read-only and each move crossing filesystems.
