- GitHub, GitLab.com, Bitbucket Cloud, Codeberg (Forgejo), Gitea.com, sourcehut, kernel.org, and Azure DevOps each served a fetch by SHA of a tip, of a non-tip ancestor with full history, at depth 1, and at depth 12. Azure DevOps answers only protocol v0, and git fell back.
- Over alternates, git's first have was the store's commit on every host, and the fetch of a commit ten commits later moved 13.7 to 482.2 KiB against 87.7 to 12,680.9 KiB with no store. A store whose ref was deleted drew no haves and a full resend on every host, which shows that the store needs its commit as a ref, as "Fill" writes it.
- A commit the store holds was found and checked out with no fetch. After the move, the absolute alternates path held, `git status` was clean, `fsck --connectivity-only` passed, and a commit succeeded.

Still unverified: a real `netagent`, HTTP/2 between git and `netagent`, a minted credential or private repository, a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, git on GnuTLS, large repositories and checkout time, restore through the capture copy and restore completion, every git step run by `lenny-materialize` as the slot UID with the pinned, exec-only git against a store at `/lenny/git-stores` that `lenny-store-fill` filled, the fetch grant, and the warm-up checkout with the reflink rebuild on runc with XFS and its fallback on gVisor and Kata.

## DR-12: Repository folders and the working directory

The owner settled decision 19 with a rule for every session, which covers a client's clones as well as default clones: every repository goes in its own subfolder of `/workspace`, the session's working tree ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). A session can then start in a folder the plan names, because a coding agent expects to start inside its repository.

**Repository folders.** Every `gitClone` source, from a client, a runtime's defaults, or a tenant's defaults, names `path`, a relative subdirectory of `/workspace` that is not `.`. §14's default of `.` goes, and a plan whose clone omits `path` or names `.` answers `400 WORKSPACE_PLAN_INVALID`. Two clones in one plan cannot share a path or sit one inside the other, and composition refuses a client clone that meets a default clone, with `details.source: runtimeDefault` and no URL, as "Binding" states for other refusals a default causes. A file whose path lies inside a clone's folder keeps §14's per-path collision rule, so a plan can still write a file into its repository. `lenny-materialize` still clones into a fresh directory and moves the result, as DR-9's "Not verified" states, because an earlier source can have written into the target.

**`workingDirectory`.** The plan gains an optional `workingDirectory`, a relative path under `/workspace`. An empty string and `.` both name `/workspace` itself. A runtime's `workspaceDefaults` can set one too, which passes the same checks at the runtime write and follows §5.1's rule for a derived field, replacing the base's value. A plan that sets the field, including to an empty string or `.`, wins over the runtime's value, and a plan that omits it takes the runtime's value, or `/workspace` when the runtime sets none, so a client that does not know a default repository's folder still starts in it. Any other value passes the same path checks as a source `path`, including the `.git` refusal. The stored plan holds the composed value, always as a concrete path, so a derive, retry, or rebuild onto another runtime starts in the same folder.

- **Start.** After materialization, and before setup commands, the adapter opens the folder through the slot's broker, which resolves it beneath the workspace root with `openat2` under `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` ([SI-5](session-isolation-design.md#si-5-the-supervisor-file-descriptor-broker)), and it checks again after setup and before the runtime starts, because a command can remove or replace it. A missing folder, a file, or a path crossing a symbolic link fails the session with `SESSION_CREATION_FAILED` and the reason `working_directory_invalid`, as the [egress design](egress-and-git-design.md)'s finalize failures do. The folder reaches the runtime as a new `cwd` field on the session's `session_start` frame (§28.5.3) and on `StartSessionRequest`, which carry none today, and §6.4 changes from a runtime deriving `cwd` from the session identifier to a runtime using that field, which always lies under `/workspace`. The slot container's working directory is `/workspace` itself, so the supervisor starts every runtime process there, and the runtime moves into `cwd` ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)). No runtime is deployed, so the reference runtimes and the runtime author guide adopt it with no fallback. The session row's `cwd` and the resume driver's `expected_workspace_root` hold the constant `/workspace`, which no longer depends on the session identifier, so `resumeOnPod`'s root check compares constants.
- **Setup commands.** Each `setupCommands` entry gains an optional `cwd`, a relative path under `/workspace` with the same checks, and the adapter applies the start check to it before the command runs. When a session's plan is composed, the gateway resolves the folder of each command that sets no `cwd` and writes it on the stored command as an explicit `cwd`. A client's command takes the plan's working directory. An operator's or tenant's default command takes the working directory named by the `workspaceDefaults` that hold the command, or `/workspace` when they name none, so a client's choice does not move a command its author wrote for another folder. A command a derive adds through `additionalSetupCommands`, whose `origin` is `derive`, takes the plan's working directory when it sets no `cwd`, as a client's command does. After composition a command's `origin` decides only which policy checks it, through the `setupCommandPolicy` exemption, and never its folder. A derive copies each stored command with its `cwd`, so the relabel of an `operator` command to `client` under [`session-derive-design.md`, "Checks"](session-derive-design.md#checks) leaves the command in its folder. A command under `setupCommandPolicy.shell: false` cannot change folder itself, which is why the field exists.
- **Resume and derive.** The stored plan carries the value and each command's `cwd`, so a derive, retry, rebuild, and resume start in the same folder and run each setup command in the same folder. An agent can delete or replace the folder during a session, so on a resume, a restore, or a `from: latest` derive a folder that fails the check starts the runtime in `/workspace` and records `workspace.working_directory_fallback`, rather than failing a session the user did not misconfigure. A native SDK resume that keys on its directory can then start a fresh conversation, and the event says so.

**Compatibility.** §14 treats a newly required field as a breaking change that needs a `schemaVersion` bump. No deployment exists, so DR-12 changes `schemaVersion` 1 in place, and §14's rule applies from the first release.

DR-12 changes a wire contract and the stored plan, so it lands before DR-9, and it stands on its own for sessions on every runtime.

## DR-10: Capacity reservation

### What a reservation holds

`maxOwnedPods` is a ceiling. A tenant holding 20 cannot go above it, and nothing ensures that 20 can start. A reservation is a floor, and three things can take capacity away from one:

| What takes capacity | Control in this design | Holds against it |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| A pod ending, which frees its place until a replacement starts | The controller keeps the pool at its floor, counting warm and active pods | The pods that exist. A replacement can wait on the two rows below. |
| Other pools' pods reaching the namespace `ResourceQuota` | The quota is split, and the reserved share is unavailable to other pods | Pod count, CPU, and memory, at quota admission. Ephemeral storage stays shared, as "The quota" states. |
| Other pools' pods filling the nodes | The template's reserved overlay ([PM-3](placement-design.md#reserved-overlay)), when it names nodes the operator reserves | Fully when the overlay's nodes host nothing else. Without an overlay, only the pods that exist hold node capacity, and a replacement competes for what is free. |

### Who can turn it on

Every level is off until the level above it allows it.

| Setting | Set by | Effect |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.reservations.enabled` | Operator (Helm), false by default | While false, the chart renders neither the `lenny-reserved` `PriorityClass` nor the split quota, which "The quota" below states, and a grant cannot allow a reservation. The controller reads the same value, so stored floors stop applying, no pod is marked, `createPod` renders no class on a marked `AgentPod` with no pod, T stops charging `reservedPods`, and `GET` reports the reservation as `inactive`. Pods already running continue. |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.reservations.totalPods`, per resource class | Operator (Helm) | The deployment's reservable total, which sizes the reserved quota. |
| `bases[].maxReservedPods`, per resource class | Platform admin, in the grant | The tenant's allowance, none by default. Across all grants, the allowances for a class stay at or below the total, checked under the lock that grant writes take. Across one grant, all allowances together stay at or below `maxOwnedPods`. |
| `pool.reservedPods` | Tenant admin, or a platform admin writing for the tenant | The floor on one derived runtime's pool, at or below the allowance across the tenant's pools of that base and class. |

The allowance belongs to the tenant, and the tenant divides it among its runtimes, because each pod belongs to one pool and one resource class. A tenant using only shared pools has nothing to reserve, because warm pods on a shared pool have no tenant. A derived runtime with no changes to the base gives such a tenant an owned pool.

Reservations apply in the agent namespace the PoolScalingController manages (`--agent-namespace`), because the tree has no pool-to-namespace mapping. Under [PM-9](placement-design.md#pm-9-namespaces), `lenny-agents` holds every agent pod of every profile and `lenny-agents-kata` is dropped, so a template with a `microvm` profile carries an allowance as any other template does, and its reserved overlay keeps its reserved pods within the node class of the `microvm` member ([PM-3](placement-design.md#reserved-overlay)).

### Mechanism

**The floor.** The WarmPoolController computes the warm target of an owned pool on each reconcile, from `PodSet.spec.reservedPods` and its own `AgentPod` list, as `max(spec.minWarm, reservedPods - active)`, where `spec.minWarm` is the `warmCount` the PoolScalingController rendered, or 0 under a stop, and passes it to `plan.Compute` as both `MinWarm` and `MaxWarm`. `warmTarget` clamps to `MaxWarm`, so raising `MinWarm` alone would have no effect, and setting both keeps the pinned idle bound at zero, as DR-5 needs. `reconcilePDB` and the pool conditions read the same target, because they read `spec.minWarm` today and a reserved pod would get no disruption budget. `active` counts `claimed` and `reserved` pods, and leaves out `draining` and `failed`, which are unusable. It cannot be computed in the PoolScalingController, which syncs every 30 seconds while the WarmPoolController reacts to `AgentPod` events at once, because a stale target creates pods the floor does not need. A pool with `reservedPods: 5` and `warmCount: 1` runs 5 warm pods when idle, 2 warm pods with 3 sessions, and 1 warm pod with 5 or more sessions.

The floor applies after the scaling formula, so low demand or a `scaleToZero` window cannot take the pool below it. `resolveWarm` writes `MinWarm: 0` for both a force-zero and a `scaleToZero` window, so the controller cannot tell them apart from that value. The PoolScalingController therefore renders `spec.reservedPods` as 0 on `ForceZeroMinWarm` and when the pool row has `DrainingSince` set, because the `drain` route sets only that column, and as the stored value otherwise. `drainAndDeleteWarmPool` bypasses `syncWarmPool`, so it writes the field itself. A non-empty `warm_stop_reasons` maps to `ForceZeroMinWarm`, so a suspended tenant, a retired base, and a security forbid still drain the pool.

**The quota.** With reservations on, the chart renders the cluster-scoped `PriorityClass` `lenny-reserved`, named by the Helm value `placement.priorityClasses.reserved` in the priority-class scheme of [PM-8](placement-design.md#priority-classes), with value 0 and `preemptionPolicy: Never`, and splits `lenny-agent-quota` in the agent namespace through the `PriorityClass` quota scope, which selects that class. A new `lenny-agent-reserved-quota` matches that class and limits `pods` to the sum of `totalPods`. Limiting CPU and memory there would need the total requests of a rendered pod, and `applyResources` stamps a class's requests on every container, so a pod's total depends on its sidecars and RuntimeClass overhead. A limit computed from the class alone would run out at half the stated pods or sooner. The grants and the class sizes bound what those pods request, so a `pods` limit is enough for the reserved share. The reserved quota sits beside the per-class quotas of [PM-8](placement-design.md#priority-classes): a reserved pod carries `lenny-reserved` in place of its class's agent priority class, so a class quota that selects `lenny-agent-<class>` does not count it, and the reserved quota bounds it instead. `lenny-agent-quota` matches every other pod, and it holds the namespace's pod count less `totalPods`, and its CPU and memory less `reservations.reservedRequests`, in which the operator states what to set aside. Kubernetes refuses `requests.ephemeral-storage` under a `PriorityClass` scope (`IsResourceQuotaScopeValidForResource` accepts `pods` and the CPU and memory resources alone), so a third quota, `lenny-agent-storage-quota`, carries it with no scope, at the namespace's value. Other pools can therefore use up storage and refuse a reserved pod, and `TenantReservationUnmet` reports it. An operator sizes that value with the reserved pods included. Nothing in the tree can compute a rendered pod's total, so this figure is the operator's statement, and documentation gives the arithmetic. If it is wrong, the namespace total is misstated, and no reserved pod is refused.

**Which pods are reserved.** `priorityClassName` is immutable, so the WarmPoolController decides when it creates an `AgentPod`: while the pool has fewer than `reservedPods` non-terminal `AgentPod` objects marked `spec.reserved`, the new one is marked and its pod takes the class. Later pods use the general quota. Two more rules keep the floor:

- The controller drains unmarked idle pods before marked ones.
- `createPod` retries a rejected pod indefinitely, and `plan.Compute` counts that `AgentPod` as warm. While marked `AgentPod` objects are below `reservedPods`, the controller deletes an unmarked `AgentPod` in `warming` that has no pod, and creates a marked one. Deleting one that has just created its pod loses only a warming pod, because `claimer.go` claims only `idle` pods. Terminating `AgentPod` objects do not count as marked. Otherwise a full general quota would leave a stuck `AgentPod` standing in for a pod the reserved quota could start. The planner gains this action, and the rule converges because marks only rise until they reach `reservedPods`.

The controller reads `AgentPod` objects through its cached client, so a stale count can over-mark a pod. The reserved quota then refuses it, and the next reconcile corrects the count.

**Nodes.** A template pool can set `placement.reservedOverlay`, a `nodePlacement` of a `nodeSelector` and `tolerations` that the pool applies to its reserved pods alone, which replaces `reservedPlacement` ([PM-3](placement-design.md#reserved-overlay)). The operator labels and taints the nodes it names, so that no other pod lands there. The field is platform-written on the template and copied to owned pools, and the tenant cannot set it. It is validated as an offering's `nodePlacement` is, so it cannot leave the pool's node class, tolerate a class taint or a `lenny.dev/tenant` taint, or name another tenant's nodes, which replaces the refusals that earlier revisions stated for the T4 selector and the Kata toleration. A tenant whose security settings require dedicated nodes is served by a single-tenant pool with dedicated tenancy ([PM-2](placement-design.md#pm-2-node-tenancy)), whose reserved pods run on that tenant's nodes: the overlay adds to the pool's tenant node affinity and toleration and never replaces them. Without an overlay, `GET` and posture report `placementConfigured: false`; posture's `placementConfigured` reads the overlay. Preflight checks that a node carries the overlay's selector, and the size of that node pool remains the operator's responsibility.

**No preemption and no priority.** A reserved pod never evicts another pod, and its class has value 0, so it has the priority of every other pod. Ordinary agent pods carry the agent priority class of their node class, `lenny-agent-<class>` of [PM-8](placement-design.md#priority-classes), also value 0 with `preemptionPolicy: Never`. A preempting reserved pod's victims would include other tenants' running sessions, and §28.5.6 states that a preemption bypasses the eviction API and opens no `CH-ADMISSION` channel, the gateway's drain-readiness admission webhook on pod eviction, so the checks on that channel do not run. A raised value would also cause kubelet node-pressure eviction to prefer other tenants' pods and platform pods over one tenant's idle reserved pods. When nodes are full, a reserved pod waits for free capacity or the cluster autoscaler.

### Budget

`reservedPods` enters the one figure under "Bounds". A pool is charged `reservedPods + warmCount` from the moment the reservation is written, because the warm target keeps `warmCount` warm once sessions have used the reservation. A claim within the reservation leaves T unchanged, so it always passes, and a claim beyond it raises T like any other. With `warmCount` at least 1, a reservation stays below `maxOwnedPods`. The floor is subject to the creation rule under "Bounds", so the bound wins when the two conflict, because R5 protects other tenants. During a base rollout, the extra pod set that "A base rollout" under "Bounds" names is never marked `spec.reserved`, counts against neither the tenant's allowance nor `reservations.totalPods`, and runs under the general quota, under decision D8 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions). The floor applies to the pod set of the revision with the highest weight.

### Changes after a write

- A grant write lowering an allowance or `maxOwnedPods` lowers allowances and `reservedPods` in the same transaction, in name order, until the allowances and T fit, and `runtime.base_changed` goes out. Excess marked pods that are idle drain, and active ones end with their sessions.
- Those active pods still hold reserved quota. A grant write raising any allowance therefore counts live marked pods above each tenant's allowance, and it is refused while allowances plus that overrun would pass `totalPods`.
- A `totalPods` lowered below what grants allow does not reach the database. Marked pod creation then fails at quota admission, and `PoolScaleoutBlockedByQuota` fires. Preflight compares the two where it can read them.
- `DELETE` releases the runtime's reservation with its pool.

### Reporting

`GET /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}` reports `reservation`, with `requested`, `held`, `state`, `placementConfigured`, and `blockedBy`. A new metric reports requested and held pods per pool, under the cardinality statement in §16, and a `TenantReservationUnmet` alert fires when `held` stays below `requested` past a Helm-set period, whatever the cause, so a refusal at RuntimeClass admission or a missing class is reported as well as a quota refusal. Posture's `gates` section reports the allowance and its use. [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md`](billing-resource-attribution-design.md#poolidle_usage) attributes a tenant's reserved idle cost through `pool.idle_usage`, which reports an owned pool's idle pod minutes under its owning tenant and counts the minutes of reserved pods in `reserved_pod_minutes`. The idle minutes of a base rollout's extra pod set, which "A base rollout" under "Bounds" names, go to the platform rather than the owning tenant, under decision D8 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions), so the tenant is not billed for them. `lenny_warmpool_idle_pod_minutes` reports idle pods per pool and carries no tenant label.

### What it costs and what it leaves open

- The reserved share of the quota is unavailable to every other pool, including while a tenant has not used its allowance. This is the operator's cost at the Helm value.
- Nodes reserved through the overlay cost idle nodes for the whole reserved total.
- A tenant pays for reserved pods while they sit idle, whether billing reads that or the operator charges another way.
- A node failure reduces `held` until replacements start, and a replacement takes the pod start time. A reservation with no overlay can wait longer.
- The floor counts pods, and a one-session pool's `reservedPods` equals sessions. A concurrent pool's floor serves more sessions per pod.

## DR-8: Interactions

### Environments

A tenant view runs first, so an environment's `runtimeSelector` only ever sees what the tenant can reach. This also closes the case where `Selector.Matches` with an empty selector admits every registered runtime in the deployment.

`Merge` overlays labels, so a new derived runtime inherits the base's labels and joins every environment whose selector matches them, with its default commands. A write answers with a `Warning` listing the environments that admit it and any `dangerouslyShare` selector that matches it, an environment entry that opens a sharing path for delegated children into another environment, as [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) does for its writes. A tenant admin separates a runtime by overriding a label's value or with `exclude`, because the merge cannot remove a base key.

`ENVIRONMENT_REQUIRED` and `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE` read the final runtime and pool on the tenant view, under [that design](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md)'s rule for the final row. An owned pool at one session per pod with no recycling evaluates with E's full policy, and so does one with recycling, under the residual [that design's decision 5](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions) accepts: what the pool recycle scrub does not reach passes from one environment's session to the next in the tenant. An owned pool that keeps a concurrent template's value evaluates each session with its own E where `netagent` attributes each connection to its slot, under decision 60 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), and stays a pod with no per-session policy where it cannot, where the rule refuses as it does for any such pod. The `Warning` for an environment's first entry gains the name of the template-eligible bases, so a tenant admin learns the way.

### Runtime routes

A runtime route ([RT-4](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-4-runtime-routes)) is tenant-scoped. Its admission reads the tenant view and pool access for every active revision of the runtimes it names, so a route can name the tenant's own derived runtime as its requested runtime or as a target, and the runtime's owned pool as a target's pool, while another tenant's runtime or owned pool answers as unknown. A route writes no pool configuration, so it neither sizes nor drains an owned pool. A target pool that the client did not pin is resolved as for any create.

A route or an external `PreRoute` interceptor can move a session after admission. The post-chain point of [RT-2](runtime-routing-design.md#post-chain-point) re-runs, on the final row and the chosen base revision, the tenant-view check under "Reading", the pool check under "Pods", the environment checks that [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) places there, and the `registered` check under "Registered rows" in DR-4, and the tenant pin refuses another tenant's pod at claim. An access row revoked after the route's write therefore stops the sessions and delegated children it would move. A route write naming a runtime whose default clones carry `write` values answers with a `Warning`, as "Write scope" under DR-9 states.

### Delegation

A delegated child's exports are not refused for the target's runtime. Every slot container of every agent pod runs the runtime supervisor ([PL-1](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-1-pod-blueprint)), the embedded deployment model is removed ([PL-2](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model)), and `type: mcp` runtimes are removed ([`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md)), so no target lacks a supervisor, and the export refusal this section held leaves under AR O8, which the owner settled on 2026-11-13. A target whose runtime row is not `registered` is refused at the post-chain point under "Registered rows" in DR-4, on the final row, because the child's `PreRoute` chain can move it to another runtime after `materializeExport` has run, and the deferred `undoChildExport` cleans up. On every target, `lenny-materialize` writes the exported files at the child's slot UID, and an operator's setup command that reads one runs at that UID through the supervisor under DR-3, away from the adapter's service account token, so a prompt-injected parent's `requirements.txt` reaches only the child's own session. Before DR-3 and the materialization design, a command on the child ran at the adapter's UID with the service account token (F-8.7.16), and those two close it. A client's own plan file on such a pod is a separate path, which needs the client's authority and which F-7.5.17 records.

`resolveDelegationTarget` and `runtimeAuthorizedForCaller` read through the tenant view with the parent's tenant, so another tenant's name answers `TARGET_NOT_IN_SCOPE`. `runtimeAuthorizedForCaller` returns true on a read error today (F-10.6.23), and a tenant-view miss would reach that branch, so DR-8 depends on [TS-3](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-3-session-environment-context-and-cross-environment-sharing)'s fail-closed gate. `DelegationPolicy` allow lists are tenant-scoped, and a label or `ids` entry matches a derived runtime as it does any other. Isolation monotonicity applies on the owned pool's copied profile.

### Cross-environment sharing

A tenant-derived runtime can be the target of a sharing path, a pair of `dangerouslyShare` entries through which a delegated child runs in another environment, under [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 14](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions). The child runs under the target environment's policy on the owned pool, and its default commands are the tenant admin's. A label edit can add it to a declared path at once, which is the residual that design already states, and the `Warning` above reports it. Sharing never crosses a tenant, because environments are per tenant.

### Per-tenant capability overrides

`runtime-capability-overrides` applies on top of the effective runtime, once `ResolveForTenant` and the `PUT` handler read the tenant view. A tenant can override capabilities for its own sessions on any runtime, so the prohibition on a derived runtime's `capabilities` does not stop that surface. Under decision D17 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#per-tenant-capability-override), the override narrows and no longer replaces:

- It may only narrow `capabilities.interaction` and `capabilities.injection`, and a widening is refused, because these fields describe the binary (F-5.1.36). A narrowing that removes a capability another one depends on removes the dependent too, under the dependency rules of [RP-2](runtime-protocol-design.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies).
- The members it replaced today for pre-launched runtimes leave with them, because every runtime launches at bind (decision D21 of [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#decisions)). F-5.1.35 and F-6.1.29 close with that removal.

## DR-7: Security oversight and records

**Guardrail.** The tenant security record ([SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)) gains one constraint:

```json
"derivedRuntimes": { "forbid": ["all"] }
```

The values are `all`, `setupCommands`, `workspaceFiles`, `gitClones`, `floatingCloneRefs`, `writableDefaultClones`, `alwaysWritableClones`, `reservation`, and `reusedPods`. As with the other constraints, the platform grants the class and the tenant's security team can still forbid it. Stopping tenant code takes `setupCommands`, `workspaceFiles`, and `gitClones`, because a file and a clone are code. `workspaceFiles` also refuses the runtime's `inline` and `artifact` assets, and `gitClones` its `gitRepository` assets, under "Tenant shared assets". `verbatimClientFiles: forbid`, the guardrail that stops tenant client config files and overlay file templates, also forbids `workspaceFiles` and `gitClones`, because each puts arbitrary content in the pod, and a `security-admin` who set it expects that channel closed.

- A violating write answers `422 GUARDRAIL_VIOLATION`.
- `reusedPods` requires `oneSessionPerPod: true` on every derived runtime of the tenant. A runtime holding `false` is non-compliant whatever its template states today, so a create or a `PUT` without the field answers `422 GUARDRAIL_VIOLATION`, and a tightening suspends it until a compliant `PUT`. A row holding `true` takes no widening from a template, so a template write never needs to read this value. The field is one-way, so removing the value leaves it set.
- A tightening sets `suspendedBy` on each non-compliant derived runtime, apart from one it names as an exemption under **Named exemptions.** in [SO-2, "Enforcement"](security-oversight-design.md#enforcement), which posture then lists. A compliant `PUT` clears the mark, as [SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)'s table states for a restored preset, a named, versioned set of request rules that a guardrail can require on a credential binding. A suspended runtime leaves discovery, new sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`, and its pool stops warming and drains. Running sessions continue, under the residual "A stored plan outlives a stop".
- Removing a forbid is a weakening, with [SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)'s delay. Creating a derived runtime is not a weakening by itself (decision 9).

**Posture.** A new `derivedRuntimes` section reports each runtime's name, base, `baseStatus`, count and digest of default commands and files, each default clone's URL and ref, whether the ref is a full commit, its `write` value, each `inline` and `artifact` asset's path and digest, each `gitRepository` asset's `name`, URL, ref, `prestage`, and declarer, each warm-list entry suspended at render with its reason, `reservedPods`, `oneSessionPerPod`, the effective `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` and recycling, `placement.offering`, last writer, and last change. `gates` reports the grant, its limits, and current use. `pools` already reports `ownerTenant`.

**Records.** `admin.runtime.updated` carries `changedFields` alone today. The new events, `admin.derived_runtime.created`, `.updated`, and `.deleted`, carry the owner, the base, the version, the full text of each setup command, each file's path and digest, each default clone's URL, ref, path, and `write`, each `inline` and `artifact` asset's path, size, and digest, each `gitRepository` asset's `name`, URL, ref, `prestage`, and declarer, and before and after values for `oneSessionPerPod`, `sessionPolicy`, and the pool's `warmCount` and `reservedPods`. Command text belongs in the record, because it is what ran in every session. File content stays out, because a tenant can place sensitive data there against the documentation, and the composed plan or the artifact store already holds it. An artifact upload emits `admin.derived_runtime.artifact_uploaded` with the digest and size, and the deletion of an upload emits `admin.derived_runtime.artifact_deleted` with the digest and the reason: `unreferenced`, `runtimeDeleted`, or `tenantDeleted`. `derivation-grant` writes and retirement emit their own events. A store withheld for a session writes `warm_store.withheld` with the session, the entry, and the reason, as "Withheld stores" under DR-9 states. A `platform-admin`'s write to a tenant-derived runtime uses the same route and is mirrored under [SO-4a](security-oversight-design.md#so-4a-mirror-to-the-tenant-chain).

## Configuration by scope

| Setting | Description | Scope | Managed by |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.enabled` | Whether a new grant or a raise is accepted. False by default. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxOwnedPodsTotal` | The limit on the sum of every grant's `maxOwnedPods`. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.baseRetirementNoticeSeconds` | The minimum notice for a retirement. 2592000 by default, which is a judgment. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxWorkspaceDefaultsBytes` | The size bound on a derived runtime's default files. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClones` | The limit on a tenant-derived runtime's default clones and `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets together for a grant entry that sets no `maxDefaultGitClones`, 8 by default, which is a judgment. An operator runtime's default clones and a `platform`-declared asset do not count toward it. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling` | The ceiling on every grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones`, 32 by default, which is a judgment. The chart refuses a `maxDefaultGitClones` default above it. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.allowAssets` | Whether a tenant-derived runtime may carry `inline` and `artifact` assets on this deployment. False by default. A tenant also needs the grant entry's `allowAssets`. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxInlineAssetBytes` | The limit on one `inline` asset's content, 1 MiB by default and at most 4 MiB. A runtime's `inline` assets together are at most 4 MiB, because they live in its row. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetUploadBytes` | The limit on one tenant's stored artifact uploads for a grant that sets no `maxAssetUploadBytes`, 1 GiB by default, which is a judgment. It is a default rather than a ceiling: a grant may set a larger value (decision 44). | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetsBytes` and `maxAssetEntryBytes` | The limit on a tenant-derived runtime's `inline` and `artifact` assets together, 256 MiB by default, and on one entry, 64 MiB by default. These figures are judgments. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.warmClones.maxBytesPerPod` and `totalBytes` | The ceiling on any grant's `maxWarmCloneBytes`, and on the sum across grants of `maxOwnedPods` times that value. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.warmClones.defaultMaxAgeSeconds`, `minAgeSeconds`, and `maxAgeSeconds` | The age limit on an idle pod's store when a pool sets none, 86400 by default, and the bounds on a pool's `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds`, 3600 and 86400 by default. These figures are judgments. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds` | The age limit on one pool's idle stores, whoever declared them. Unset on a platform pool, a store has no age limit. | Pool | Tenant admin on an owned pool, within the Helm bounds. Platform admin on any pool. |
| `warmCloneBytes` | The size of the warm list's volume on one pool's pods, and the storage request that matches it. On an owned pool it is the template's value plus the grant entry's `maxWarmCloneBytes`. | Pool | Platform admin on a platform pool. Computed on an owned pool. |
| `sharedAssets` on a tenant-derived runtime | `inline` and `artifact` files under `/lenny/assets`, and `gitRepository` assets that declare the owned pool's stores, each with optional `prestage: true`. | Runtime in a tenant | Tenant admin, under the Helm and grant `allowAssets` for files and `allowGitClones` with `maxWarmCloneBytes` for repositories. A platform admin writes a `platform`-declared `gitRepository` asset through the same route, under `maxWarmCloneBytes` alone, and only a platform admin changes it. |
| `gitRepository` entries in a Runtime's `sharedAssets` | Public entries that reach the warm list of every pool of the runtime, owned pools of derived runtimes included. `prestage: true` stages the repository in each idle slot. | Runtime | Platform admin |
| `prestageTimeoutSeconds` on a tenant-derived runtime | The timeout of a warm-up checkout run on the owned pool's slots. Unset, the base revision's value, then the owned pool's, then `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds` applies. | Runtime in a tenant | Tenant admin, within the grant entry's `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds` |
| `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` on a platform runtime | Repositories every session on the runtime clones by default, with `write: none`. A platform-derived runtime's clones append to its base's. | Runtime | Platform admin |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.warmClones.failureThreshold` and `maxConcurrentFillsPerTenant` | The failures of one store before backoff, on every pool and for every declarer, and the fills one tenant can run at once. These figures are judgments. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries` | The most tree entries the adapter checks out from one plan's clones. The figure is a judgment. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.reservations.enabled` | Whether reservations exist at all. False by default. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `runtimes.tenantDerivation.reservations.totalPods` and `reservedRequests` | The reservable pods per resource class, which limits the reserved quota, and the requests set aside from the general quota. | Deployment | Platform admin (Helm) |
| `derivation-grant` (`namePrefix`, `maxRuntimes`, `maxOwnedPods`, `maxAssetUploadBytes`, and `bases[]` with `allowWorkspaceFiles`, `allowSetupCommands`, `allowGitClones`, `allowGitCloneWrites`, `allowAssets`, `maxWarmCloneBytes`, `maxDefaultGitClones`, `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`, `maxReservedPods`, `ownedOverlays`, and `retireAt`) | What the tenant can derive from, under which template, with which defaults and overlays, and up to what limits. | Tenant | Platform admin (admin API) |
| Template pool | The source of every copied pool setting, including `placement.offering` as the default, `placement.region`, and `placement.reservedOverlay`. | Pool | Platform admin |
| Derived runtime fields and `pool` block | Under "Fields", "Template copy", and DR-10. | Runtime in a tenant | Tenant admin, or a custom role with `manage_derived_runtimes` |
| `derivedRuntimes` in the security record | What the tenant's security team forbids. | Tenant | `security-admin` |
| Environment selector | Whether an environment admits the runtime. | Environment | Tenant admin |

Every setting in [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) and [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) stays as written there.

## Scenarios

**An environment's way to one-session pods.** The platform has granted `acme` the base `chat` with template pool `chat-shared`, which serves concurrent sessions on a runtime class where `netagent` cannot attribute a connection to its slot, so its pods keep the union scope under the fallback of the egress design's decision 60. Environment `payments` denies `huggingface.co`, so Bob's create on `chat` in `payments` fails with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE`, and the `Warning` from the policy write named `chat` as affected and as a base that offers this way. Alice, a `tenant-admin`, posts this:

```yaml
name: acme--chat-payments
baseRuntime: chat
labels: { environment: payments, approved: "false" }
oneSessionPerPod: true
pool: { warmCount: 2, resourceClass: small }
```

The response warns that `payments` admits the runtime. The controller creates two pods from `chat`'s `Runtime` resource, labelled for `acme`. Bob's create succeeds on a one-session pod with `payments`' full policy. The grant sets neither default flag, so no tenant code runs, and this way does not need DR-3.

**Another tenant.** An administrator of `globex` posts `name: acme--x` and gets `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`, and a post under `globex--x` with no grant gets `403 DERIVATION_NOT_GRANTED`. A `globex` user creating a session on `acme--chat-payments` gets the answer for an unknown runtime, and `GET /v1/runtimes` does not list it. A `globex` route naming `acme--chat-payments` as a target gets the answer for an unknown runtime, because route admission reads the tenant view.

**A concurrency value.** The template serves 20 concurrent sessions. Alice sends `sessionPolicy: { maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod: 5 }` and gets `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`, with `details.field: sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`. She can set `oneSessionPerPod: true`, and another value takes a template from a `platform-admin`.

**A budget reached.** `acme` holds `maxOwnedPods: 20`, with warm counts summing to 8 and 11 active pods, so T is 19. Bob's claim takes T to 20, which the bound allows. Carol's claim would take it to 21, so it answers `429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, though warm pods sit idle. With those 12 active pods, a write taking the warm sum from 8 to 9 answers `422 DERIVATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, with the current use. A write lowering the sum passes.

**A base retired.** An operator retires `chat` with 30 days' notice. `acme`'s audit chain and SIEM destination receive `runtime.base_changed`, and `GET` shows `baseStatus: retiring`. Sessions continue. After the date, new sessions answer `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`, the owned pool drains, and running sessions finish under their limits. Alice has moved `payments` to another base by then.

**A default clone.** The grant sets `allowGitClones`. Alice adds `github.com/acme/agent-skills` at `main`, under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, and the write warns that the ref floats and that repository access now decides who can use the runtime. Bob holds a link and read access, so his session binds the commit `main` names at that moment, and his plan records it. Carol has a link and no access, so her create answers `USER_REPOSITORY_ACCESS_DENIED` with `details.source: runtimeDefault` and `path: skills`, no repository name, and no pod claimed. Dave has no link and gets `LINKS_REQUIRED`, with the link under `details.userLinks`. A later push to `main` reaches new sessions with no write at Lenny, so `acme`'s `security-admin` sets `floatingCloneRefs` in `forbid`, and the runtime is suspended until Alice pins a commit.

**A warm, writable clone.** The grant sets `allowGitClones` and `maxWarmCloneBytes: 10Gi`. Alice adds a default clone of `github.com/acme/monorepo` at `main`, with `write: clientOptIn`, at `src`, and a `gitRepository` asset `monorepo` for the same URL with `prestage: true`. Each warm pod fills a store before it reports ready and stages a tree in each idle slot. Bob's client sends `writeDefaultClones: true` without knowing the repository's name, and his session binds the commit `main` names, adopts the staged tree by renaming it to `src`, and checks out the few commits that moved since the fill, fetching only what the store lacks. Bob holds read access and no write access, so his session runs read-only and the response lists `src` under `readOnlyDefaultClones`. Carol holds both and pushes her branch. A week later Alice removes the asset, the next claim refuses any pod still holding that store, the idle pods drain, and the default clone fetches its repository at materialization from then on.

**A repository the plan names.** Alice adds a `gitRepository` asset `monorepo-cache` for `github.com/acme/monorepo` at `main`, without `prestage`, to `acme--chat-payments`, which has no default clone of it, under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: off` and no `environments` list. The base `chat` carries a public `gitRepository` entry for `https://gitlab.com/initech/toolkit`, so each warm pod of the owned pool fills both stores. Bob's client clones `acme/monorepo` at his branch `bob/fix`, and his session fetches only the commits the store lacks, because a store serves any source of its URL. Carol's session names environment `research`, which denies `github.com`, so her read check on the entry fails. Her pod serves one session, so the adapter deletes the store before it materializes anything, her session proceeds, and the response lists `withheldWarmEntries` with `name: monorepo-cache` and `reason: environment`. Her pod drains when her session ends. The initech entry later fails to fill after its host moves, so the owned pool backs off, Alice's `GET` reports `blockedBy: platformEntry` with its URL, the operator gets the alert, and the failed pods do not count in `acme`'s T.

**A reservation.** The operator sets `reservations.enabled` and `totalPods: { small: 100 }`, with `placement.reservedOverlay` on the template naming a node pool the operator labels and taints for reserved pods. A platform admin gives `acme` `maxOwnedPods: 20` and `maxReservedPods: { small: 10 }`. Alice sets `reservedPods: 10` and `warmCount: 2` on `acme--chat-payments`, which takes T to 12, so 10 warm pods start on the overlay's nodes. A burst on other pools fills `lenny-agent-quota` and its own nodes, and `acme`'s pods are unaffected. With all 10 in use, two more warm pods start under the general quota if it has room, and further claims can take the tenant to 20 pods. An administrator of `globex` with no allowance who sets `reservedPods` gets `422 DERIVATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`.

**A stolen `tenant-admin`.** A grant with no default flag refuses the attacker's default file, command, clone, and shared asset, because an `inline` or `artifact` asset needs `allowAssets` and a `gitRepository` asset needs `allowGitClones`, each with DR-3 landed. Under a grant with `allowWorkspaceFiles`, the attacker writes a `requirements.txt` that a base command installs, or a hook the runtime loads, and under `allowAssets` a file under `/lenny/assets` that the runtime loads, and that code runs at the slot UID in each new session. It can read a `file` token and use an `inject` token. The audit event carries the file's path and digest, and an [SO-3](security-oversight-design.md#so-3-per-tenant-siem-destination) SIEM destination receives it at once. `derivedRuntimes.forbid` with `workspaceFiles`, `setupCommands`, and `gitClones` refuses the write. Otherwise an operator sets the flag false, which makes the runtime `incompatible` for new sessions, or a `security-admin` adds the forbid. The composed plan on each session row records which sessions ran it.

## Residual risks

- **A tenant admin's reach.** A `tenant-admin` can write defaults that run in every session on a derived runtime, including in an environment it does not belong to, because [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) already leaves it able to add itself as a member. Environments give no boundary against a tenant admin. No control bounds what a default file does once a base command or the runtime reads it, and commands within an allowlist can still do what the allowed tools do.
- **No per-session consent.** A user selecting a runtime receives its defaults. The plan on `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` shows them after the fact.
- **Shared capacity.** Budgets bound what a tenant holds, and only DR-10 sets a floor. Tenant-owned pods with no reservation share nodes and the namespace `ResourceQuota` with every other pool, and a quota reached by any pods stops pods of all pools, which is true of session load today. A `maxOwnedPodsTotal` set below the quota limits this for owned pools alone. The bound is a pod count, so a tenant using `large` holds more than one using `small`.
- **Pod-count overshoot.** The creation rule reads a cached list and the controller reconciles pools concurrently, so pods can pass `maxOwnedPods` by a small number until the next reconcile.
- **A busy reservation at its bound.** Pods that are draining count toward `maxOwnedPods` at creation, so a pool whose short sessions keep pods draining can fall below its floor when the tenant is at the bound. An operator gives headroom by setting `maxOwnedPods` above the reservation by what its drains need. `TenantReservationUnmet` reports the shortfall. Real drain durations were not measured.
- **Reads a user did not ask for.** Under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, a default clone reads a repository the session user can read, on the authority of that user's link, which a tenant admin chose and the user did not name. The user's choice of runtime is the only consent, and the plan shows it after the fact. A default command or base command can then send that content wherever the session's egress allows.
- **Pushers run code.** Whoever can push to a default clone's floating ref runs code in every new session on the runtime, as DR-9 states, until a forbid or a pinned commit applies.
- **Push access with no client statement.** Under `write: always`, every session on the runtime can push within the clone's ref namespace, including a session whose client does not know the repository exists and an agent under prompt injection. The grant flag, the forbid, and the namespace bound this, and branch protection on the host remains the tenant's control.
- **A warm store outlives a host-side revocation.** A repository whose host access ends stays readable on idle pods until the pool's `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds`, at most 24 hours by default, for a session whose bind checks still pass, because a commit the store holds needs no fetch. On a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required`, each user's own check still runs. A running session keeps its store when a clone is removed.
- **A new entry can change a resume.** A tenant admin adding a `gitRepository` asset withholds that store from a resumed session whose user cannot read the repository, because the session's next pod holds the store. On a pod with several slots the resume fails instead.
- **Deleted stores leave blocks on the node.** Withholding a store deletes its directory entries and leaves its data blocks on the node's disk until they are overwritten. A user without access could recover the repository only through a kernel or filesystem bug that exposes stale data, and only within the tenant, because the store sits on the tenant's owned pool or is a public operator entry. Every pod's ephemeral data already leaves stale blocks of the same class on its node, and a full kernel escape already exposes the node. The owner accepted this on 2026-11-08 (decision 34).
- **A `gitRepository` asset narrows some sessions.** A session whose user or environment fails the checks of an entry its plan does not name runs without the store, and its pod drains when the session ends, so such users cost the pool a pod each. The tenant admin's per-entry counts report how often this happens.
- **An operator's entry on a tenant's pods.** A base's public entry fills on every owned pool of the base, on the tenant's egress identity and within the template's `warmCloneBytes`. A failing operator entry leaves the owned pool with no warm pods until the operator fixes it or the backoff passes, and the tenant can only report it. Under decision 38 such pods still count toward the creation bound and the fill limit.
- **Disk pressure.** A warm pod within its storage request ranks after pods with no request for kubelet eviction. Every agent pod requests the sum of its disk-backed volumes' `sizeLimit`s ([PL-5](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-5-volumes-size-limits-and-eviction), PLR-12), so an agent pod within its volumes' limits does not go first. The ranking was read from upstream, and that a zero request counts as exceeded is from memory. `sizeLimit` counts bytes, so a tree within `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries` can still use many inodes on a node, and that eviction signal is also from memory.
- **A resume under `reusedPods`.** A session that began before the forbid can resume on a suspended runtime's pod, joining or taking a pod another user of the tenant held, because `resumeOnPod` skips the tenant view. The session already ran under that policy, and it stays within the tenant. A `PUT` setting `oneSessionPerPod: true` ends this, and refusing the resume would strand the session instead.
- **Writable clones on reused pods.** A tenant whose derived runtime reuses pods cannot give a default clone a `write` value until it sets `oneSessionPerPod: true`, and a client declares a repository to push to it. A pool that has held such a clone cannot widen later, so a tenant creates a new runtime for that.
- **A removed repository on reused pods.** As "Who reaches a store" states, a store stays readable to new sessions on a running or held pod until the pod retires.
- **A default clone can lock users out.** A repository removed from the allowlist or a revoked permission refuses new sessions on the runtime for the users affected.
- **Reserved capacity with shared nodes.** Without `placement.reservedOverlay`, a replacement for a reserved pod competes for free node capacity, so a full cluster delays it, and the alert reports it.
- **A listed overlay row.** A stolen `tenant-admin` with `caBundle` listed can read what tools send to hosts `netagent` does not terminate, on pods created afterwards, including a `file` token that opens systems outside the tenant. Other tenants' pods and platform services do not trust that CA. [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md) states the limit of the LLM host reservation on a pool with `egressProfile: internet`: no LLM credential lease exists under [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md), so the exposure is the session's proxy token, which a process in the pod can send to any host on such a pool and whose use the session's credit budgets bound. A listed `egressProfile` row set to `internet` lets raw traffic leave from a shared address with no decision.
- **Warm pods after an overlay row drops.** No pod roll is triggered, so warm pods carrying a dropped `caBundle` or `clientProfile` serve new sessions until they are used up.
- **Deleting a base's `Runtime` resource.** The RuntimeReconciler's finalizer soft-deletes the row and bypasses notice and the dependents check. An operator who deletes the resource directly strands tenant-derived runtimes at once, as `retired`.
- **A stored plan outlives a stop.** A grant flag set false, a forbid, or a suspension stops new sessions. §7.3 step 3e rebuilds a session with no checkpoint from its stored plan and runs the plan's setup commands, so an attacker's default runs again in that session. An emergency retirement with `force: true` ends such sessions. Filtering on rebuild would need a per-command source on the stored plan, and that is additive. A writable entry a default clone added also stays in the stored plan until the session ends.
- **Trust in the base.** A tenant takes base changes with no approval step, apart from narrowing. A base's `workspaceDefaults` change reaches the tenant's new sessions at once, and a template widening reaches its owned pools in the same transaction. Posture and `runtime.base_changed` report it after the fact.
- **The retirement window.** A base can stay usable for its notice period. A compromised image needs the emergency path, which interrupts tenants.
- **Environment membership through labels.** A runtime enters a selector by inherited labels at create, and a label edit or a base label change alters membership at once. The `Warning` reports it to the writer. A platform's base label edit reaches tenants with only the base-changed event.
- **A compromised gateway replica.** Row-level security limits a tenant-context query. A compromised replica can set any context, write any row, and write any pool. The owner label and the tenant pin check still refuse another tenant at the pod.
- **Defaults hold no secrets.** A tenant admin can write a secret into a default file against the documentation, and every reader of a session's plan then sees it. A secret in a tenant asset is readable to every session on the runtime in the same way.
- **Tenant assets are code.** A runtime can load a file under `/lenny/assets` that the base does not ship, so a tenant `inline` or `artifact` asset can change what the runtime does in every session on it, as a default file can. The append rule keeps a tenant from replacing an operator's file and does not stop this. The operator's Helm `allowAssets` and the grant entry's `allowAssets`, both off by default, and the `workspaceFiles` forbid bound it.
- **Prompt injection inside a tenant.** An agent discovering a derived runtime reads its `agentInterface` text, which a tenant admin wrote.
- **Unproven.** No part of the design has run. The warm clone spike of 2026-11-05 ran against a stand-in for `netagent`, and DR-9's "Not verified" lists what it left unverified. The pod path through `podRuntimeRef`, the `lenny.dev/served` readers, and the merge as an intersection have not run.

## Defects found while verifying

These exist independent of this design. Each is filed in `BUILD-GAPS.md` under the F-id in the "Filed as" column, at the severity proposed here. DF-12 has two entries, because its two parts concern different specification sections. "Read" means the evidence comes from reading the code, and nothing was run.

| ID | Filed as | Severity | Defect | Evidence | Closed by |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| DF-1 | F-5.1.26 | Critical | A `tenant-admin` holding a grant on a shared runtime can write fields that reach other tenants, including `workspaceDefaults.setupCommands`, which run in other tenants' sessions and are exempt from `setupCommandPolicy`. | `runtimeManage` in `routes.go`, `requireResourceManage`, `applyRuntimeUpdate`, §14.1 **Runtime workspace defaults.**, §7.5 **Command policy:**, and the RuntimeReconciler's `applyCRDFields`. §10.2's **Runtime and pool scoping clarification** permits this write. F-5.2.67 covers pools alone. Read. | DR-1 |
| DF-2 | F-10.6.25 | High | `runtime_tenant_access` does not gate session creation, discovery, or delegation, and `pool_tenant_access` gates only a pool a request pins, so a tenant whose environment selector or `allow-all` admits a runtime uses it with no access row. §10.6's "Runtimes owned by other tenants are never exposed" has no data behind it, and §5.1 **First-Party Reference Runtimes** states "no default tenant access grants". | `tenantReachesRuntime` is called only from the internal metadata route. `requireEnvironmentAdmission`, `filterRuntimesByEnvironment`, and `filterByEnvironmentAccess` read no access row. `tenantaccessstore` has no importer under `mcpfabric`. The comment in `pkg/embedded/stack/runtimes.go` saying `tenantReachesRuntime` denies a session is false. Read. | DR-1 |
| DF-3 | F-5.1.27 | High | A pool on a derived runtime, or on a runtime registered only through the admin API, produces no pods. `createPod` reads a `Runtime` custom resource by name, nothing in the admin path writes one, and the CRD has no `baseRuntime`. This is against §5.1's admin API statement and **Derived Runtime Instantiation**. | `createPod`, `handleCreateRuntime`, `runtime_types.go`. The only writers of `Runtime` resources outside tests are the chart's `reference-runtimes.yaml`, the embedded stack, and the reconciler's patch. Read. | DR-2 closes the derived half through `podRuntimeRef`. The admin-registered standalone half stays open in this design, because no base resource exists to point at. [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md) moves the pod builder to the revision record, which both registration paths must write ([RV-3](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-3-pods)), and that design lists the admin path under "Validation owed". |
| DF-4 | F-7.5.17 | High | Setup commands, a client's included, run at the adapter's UID in the adapter's container on a sidecar pod. That container holds the projected service account token and a writable credential volume, and on a concurrent pod the volume holds every slot's file. §7.5 **Security model:** treats the pod as the boundary, §13.1's adapter-agent boundary is a UID check, and a process a setup command starts can outlive it, as §5.2 **What the scrub reaches.** concedes. The command also runs from the adapter's image, so the `pip install` in §5.1's example does not touch the runtime's environment. `DefaultSetupEnv` sets `USER=agent` and does not change the UID, and the comment on `RunSetup` saying "as the agent user" is false. The rating is a floor: what the projected token can do at the gateway was not traced, and a wider reach there raises it. | `RunSetup` in `staging.go`, `workspace.RunSetup` and `buildSetupCmd`, `sidecar.go` mounts, `sa_token.go`. Read. | DR-3, for the adapter's identity. The concurrent-pod credential read stays, as §13.1 states. |
| DF-5 | F-5.1.28 | Medium | §5.1's fallback to the base runtime's pool does not exist, so a derived runtime with no pool answers `ErrNoMatchingPool`. With DF-3, no derived runtime runs a session today. | `ResolvePool`. No use of `BaseRuntime` in `podsession` or the claim path. Read. | DR-2, by changing §5.1 |
| DF-6 | F-5.1.29 | Medium | A runtime's `sessionPolicy` sets no pod behaviour beyond `maxClientIdleSeconds`, though §5.1's example and merge table imply it does. The way [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) gives rests on this. | `poolPolicyMirror`, `toConfig`, `sessionidle.go`, `envelope.go`. Read. | DR-2 |
| DF-7 | F-5.1.30 | Medium | A derived runtime can widen `setupCommandPolicy` and replace `delegationPolicyRef`, because `validateDerivedRuntime` checks neither and `Merge` replaces both. §5.1 says restrict, §8.3 says **Effective policy = `base_policy ∩ derived_policy`**, and `docs/getting-started/concepts.md` says restrict-only. | `validateDerivedRuntime`, `validateDerivedRuntimeUpdate`, `Merge`. Read. | DR-4 for tenant-derived, DR-2 for the rest |
| DF-8 | F-15.1.75 | Medium | `DELETE /v1/admin/runtimes/{name}` checks no pool or session, against §15.1's **Deletion semantics** Runtime rule. A derived runtime on a soft-deleted base still resolves as active, because `Get` returns soft-deleted rows and `Merge` clones the derived row. | `handleDeleteRuntime`, `pgstore.Get`, `Merge`. Read. | DR-6 |
| DF-9 | F-10.7.22 | High | Any `tenant-admin` can drain any pool in the deployment and delete its `PodSet`, by naming it as an experiment variant. The controller reads every tenant's experiments and applies effects to any named pool: `paused` sets `ForceZeroMinWarm`, `concluded` sets `DrainAndDelete`, `InitialMinWarm` overwrites `MinWarm`, and `SumActiveVariantWeights` reduces the sole pool warming the base. Variant pools have no `pool_tenant_access` check, and `variant.Runtime` has no access-row check, at write or at routing. Two tenants naming one pool make `ResolveVariantRoles` return an error, which stops the adjustment for every tenant. | `ExperimentVariantSource.ListPoolConfigs`, `ResolveVariantRoles`, and `drainAndDeleteWarmPool` in `pkg/controller/poolscaling`. `requireTenantResourceAdmin` on the experiment routes, with `PATCH` accepting `concluded`. `checkVariantIsolation` skips what it cannot resolve. `ApplyExperimentRouting` sets `row.RuntimeRef` from `variant.Runtime`, which steers the claim. It also sets `row.PoolRef` from `variant.Pool`, and `claimForNewRow` overwrites that, so `variant.Pool` does not steer an exclusive claim. Read. | [RT-1](runtime-routing-design.md#rt-1-removal) of `runtime-routing-design.md`, which removes experiments and their pool effects |
| DF-10 | F-5.1.31 | Low | A runtime name can be 128 characters, and `createPod` copies it to a pod label, whose value Kubernetes limits to 63. | `namePattern`, `pod.Labels[state.LabelRuntime]`. Read. | DR-1 |
| DF-11 | F-5.2.68 | Low | §5.2 **Pool Taxonomy** describes cold pools with `minWarm` 0 and `maxWarm` above 0, and `PoolStoreSource` maps both from `warmCount`, so a pool at 0 has no pods. | `toConfig`. Read. | Not in this design. It sets the 1-pod minimum, and a fix relaxes that. |
| DF-12 | F-5.1.32 for the merge table, F-10.2.43 for the quota row | Low | §5.1's merge table omits `workspaceTier`, `sdkWarmBlockingPaths`, `archivePolicy`, `toolCapabilityOverrides`, and `description`. A derived payload's `sdkWarmBlockingPaths` is accepted and discarded. §10.2's matrix gives `tenant-admin` "Manage quotas", and the tenant update route that holds `max_concurrent_sessions` uses `requireAdmin`. | `RuntimePayload`, `Merge`, `routes.go`. Read. | DR-4 for the table. `workspaceTier` and `sdkWarmBlockingPaths` have since left the runtime, under [TSS-9](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-9-removal-of-workspacetier) and the removal of pre-launched runtimes, and `toolCapabilityOverrides` has left with capability inference under D10 of [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md), so the table needs rows for `archivePolicy` and `description` alone. The quota row is not in this design. |
| DF-13 | F-8.3.13 | Medium | The delegation policy deletion guard ignores the tenant. Policies are keyed on `(tenant_id, name)`, and `delegationPolicyRuntimeDependents` takes a name alone, so a platform runtime referencing `strict` blocks every tenant's delete of its own `strict`, and the answer names that runtime to a tenant with no access row on it. | `delegationPolicyRuntimeDependents`, `handleDeleteDelegationPolicy`, which a `tenant-admin` reaches and which runs the guard before reading the policy, and migration `0054`. Read. | DR-4 |
| DF-14 | F-17.6.27 | Medium | §17.6 states that a seeded runtime or pool can carry `tenantAccess`, and nothing implements it. `BootstrapRequest` has no such field, and neither bootstrap handler makes a grant call. A seed therefore holds no access rows. That is harmless while DF-2 stands, and it stops every session once DF-2 closes. | `BootstrapRequest`, `bootstrap.go`, `bootstrap_resources.go`, and `tests/testinfra/kind/install.sh`. `pkg/embedded/stack/runtimes.go` grants runtimes alone. Read. | DR-1 |
| DF-15 | F-13.4.24 | High | The adapter's materializer follows symbolic links in a restored tree. `extractSymlink` and `ValidateSymlinkTarget` check the target lexically, so a chained link passes both and resolves outside the workspace or into `/run/lenny`, and `writeInlineFile`, `writeUploadFile`, `makeDir`, and `overlayTree` write through it at the adapter's UID. An agent can plant the chain, `ArchiveTree` records it, and a derive restores it ahead of the plan's sources. | `extract.go`, `upload.go`, `materialize.go`, `tree.go`. `scrub/resolve_linux.go` shows the safe form. Read. | Its own proposal, or [EG-3](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split), the egress design's proposal for the pod git path. |
| DF-16 | F-8.7.16 | High | A delegated child's exports reach an operator's setup command at the adapter's identity on a target with no runtime supervisor, so a prompt-injected parent can run code with the service account token. | `composeChildWorkspaceDefaults` and `materializeExport` in `pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go`, `runsSupervisor` in `pkg/controller/agentpod/supervision.go`, and `podspec/embedded.go`, which mounts the projected token. Read. | DR-3 and the materialization design, which run every setup command and every materializer write at the slot UID. DR-8's export refusal, which earlier revisions named here, leaves under AR O8, because every pod runs the supervisor. |
| DF-17 | F-5.2.69 | Medium | A pool can set `maxConcurrentSessions` above 1 on any runtime, because no runtime declares whether it can serve concurrent sessions. The owner chose `capabilities.maxConcurrentSessions` on the runtime, defaulting to 1. | `RuntimeCapabilities`, `ValidateSessionPolicy`, and `ValidatePreConnectExecutionMode`. Read. | Not in this design. An owned pool that keeps its template's value stays within the runtime's value, because the template passed the check, and `oneSessionPerPod` is unaffected. The record keeps the names of its time: the pool field is now `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, and `capabilities.maxConcurrentSessions` is not in the capability catalog of RP-2 in [`runtime-protocol-design.md`](runtime-protocol-design.md#rp-2-capabilities-and-their-dependencies). |
| DF-18 | F-13.4.25 | High | The adapter-local file tools (`read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir`, and `delete_file`) check a path as text and then follow symbolic links, so an agent's link makes the adapter read the projected service account token or write outside the workspace at its own UID. | `pkg/adapter/localtools/localtools.go`, `pkg/adapter/attach.go`. Read. | Its own fix, beside DF-15 |
| DF-19 | F-17.6.28 | Low | §17.6 shows the seed's tenant as one `tenant` object, while the chart's `bootstrap.tenants`, `BootstrapRequest`, and `lenny-ctl bootstrap` all take a list, so a seed naming several tenants is a normal Helm value. | `charts/lenny/values.yaml`, `bootstrap-configmap.yaml`, `bootstrap.go`, `pkg/ctlcli/main.go`. Read. | DR-1 |

F-5.2.67 (High) stands for pools, and this design adds that the same gate covers `runtimeRef`, `isolationProfile`, `sessionPolicy`, warm count, and `drain`. DR-1 refuses all of them to `tenant-admin` on a shared pool. [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s tenant pool overlay remains the path for egress and git settings.

## Specification sections affected

- §4.2 **Multi-tenancy:** and the classification table: the tenant-scoped row, and the removal of `tenant-admin` writes on platform-global records.
- §4.6.1 and §4.6.3: the owner label at creation, `podRuntimeRef`, the warm target under a reservation, `spec.reserved`, the rendered bound, the warm list on `warmClones` for every pool with each entry's declarer and `prestage`, the tenant's `inline` and `artifact` assets on an owned pool's pod sets, `warmCloneBytes`, `warmStores`, `fillFailure`, `withheldStores`, the drain of a pod that withheld a store, the backoff for every declarer, and the `lenny.dev/owner-tenant` label on the `AgentPod`.
- §4.7 and §28: `FinalizeWorkspace` and Resume carry the store directories to withhold, and the adapter deletes them, and the helper removes any tree staged from them, before materialization, restore completion, setup commands, and the runtime start. §6.4: the warm-up checkout's staging directory `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/`, which [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md#specification-changes) stages.
- §5.1 Runtime: a `gitRepository` entry in `sharedAssets` is the only declaration of a warm store, is public only on a Runtime, takes a full commit as its ref, carries no `destPath` and no other path field, carries optional `prestage: true`, and reaches the warm list of every pool of the runtime, owned pools of derived runtimes included. §5.1 **Derived Runtime**: a tenant-derived runtime's `sharedAssets` appends `inline`, `artifact`, and `gitRepository` entries on its owned pool alone, refuses a path conflict with the base instead of replacing it, pins an `artifact` to a digest uploaded for that runtime, takes `digest` and `destPath` alone on an `artifact` entry, which it places whole, and records each `gitRepository` asset's declarer from the writer's role. `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` exists on every runtime, appends on every derived runtime, and has no `warm` field. §5.2: a pool's warm list, `warmCloneBytes`, and `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds`. The rewrite of draft 0111 stages the warm list, to the requirements under "Relationship to draft proposal 0111".
- §4.7.10 **Runtime supervisor.**: running setup commands at the slot UID, on the existing `CH-SUPERVISE`, which [`session-isolation-design.md`](session-isolation-design.md#specification-changes) and [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md#specification-changes) state for every slot.
- §5.1 **Inheritance Rules**, **Normative Merge Algorithm**, and **Derived Runtime Instantiation**: the missing rows, the live merge for tenant-derived runtimes, the fallback statement, and what a runtime's `sessionPolicy` does. **Base runtime mutability**: the tenant-derived exception. **`publishedMetadata` Field**: the visibility rule. Name grammar.
- §5.2 **Tenant pinning:**, **Pinned idle inventory.**, and **Pool Taxonomy**: owned pools, `oneSessionPerPod`, and `lenny.dev/served`.
- §7.1: the order of composition and checks, the check ahead of a create-time claim, and [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) owns **Derive restore semantics:**.
- §14, the `gitClone` and `inlineFile` rows and the schema: `runtimeDefault` as a read-only field, `gitClone.path` required and never `.`, `workingDirectory`, and `setupCommands[].cwd`. §5.1 `workspaceDefaults.workingDirectory`. §6.4 and §7.1: the runtime's `cwd` comes from session start. §15.4's runtime author guide and the §26 reference runtimes read it there. §28: session start carries it. §4.7 and §28: the materialization message carrying it.
- §7.4 and §13.4: every materializer write, move, and removal, which `lenny-materialize` makes at the slot UID, resolves beneath the workspace with no symbolic links, under the rule of [WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway) that [`workspace-materialization-design.md`](workspace-materialization-design.md#specification-changes) stages.
- §7.5 **Command policy:** and **Security model:**: the scope of the exemption, and where commands run.
- §8.3: the intersection stated in code terms.
- §10.2: the permission matrix, the new permission, and **Runtime and pool scoping clarification**.
- §10.5 **`RuntimeUpgrade` State Machine**: [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#specification-changes) replaces the pool-level upgrade with the runtime rollout of [RV-4](runtime-revisions-design.md#rv-4-rollout), under which an owned pool keeps a pod set per distinct pod hash among the active base revisions and is exempt from the tenant's bounds as decision D8 of that design states. This design adds the owned-pool exemption to the §4.6.2 and §11.2 text that states T.
- §10.6: runtime reach before selectors, and the `allow-all` text that names a tag no record carries.
- §11.2: `maxOwnedPods` as a platform-held bound.
- §5.1 **Normative Merge Algorithm**, the `runtimeOptionsSchema` row, and §14's paragraph on derived runtimes' schemas: [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#specification-changes) replaces the override and its property-name subset rule with separate validations against both schemas, and this design adds the write refusals of a tenant schema and its `incompatible` case.
- §11.7: the new events.
- §12.3, §12.8, and §12.9: the tables, erasure, and classification under the data classes Public, Internal, Confidential, and Secret of [TSS-8](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-8-data-classes), including tenant artifacts and the tenant-owned assets set as Confidential, deleted with the tenant and held under legal hold.
- §13.2.6: an owned pool's profile comes from its template.
- §7.1, §8.2, and §15.1: at the post-chain point of every create, derive, and delegated child, a final runtime row that is not `registered`, or a platform-derived runtime whose base row is not, answers as an unknown runtime, which replaces SD-P4's `target_unsupervised` in [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) under AR O8. §5.1 states what `registered` records.
- §12.5 and §4.7: the session row's `coveredStores` and `withheldStores`, which decide the eligible sources of the platform chunk sets that [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#specification-sections-affected) stages.
- §14.1 **Runtime workspace defaults.**: tenant-written commands get no exemption, rule 4 does not apply where the tenant view is wired, default clones compose as `gitClone` sources, and **Path collision rule** states that a plan path crossing a symbolic link fails the session. §14 gains `writeDefaultClones`, and §6.4 gains the warm list's volume, `lenny-git-stores` at `/lenny/git-stores`, for every pool, as [`agent-pod-layout-design.md`](agent-pod-layout-design.md#specification-changes) states the layout. §14 **Cache-served `gitClone` sources.** serves any `gitClone` source whose normalized URL matches a store on the pod, unless it sets `depth` or `submodules: true`. [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) owns the changes to §7.1 **Derive restore semantics:** and §15.1 **Session Replay Semantics**.
- §15.1: the routes, the artifact upload route with its per-runtime digests, its `maxAssetUploadBytes` refusal, and the deletion of unreferenced uploads, the codes with `TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL` marked as an internal guard, the deletion rules for a runtime and a tenant, `retire`, the removal of the `tenant-admin` clauses on `PUT` rows, a tenant-derived runtime's `sharedAssets`, `withheldWarmEntries` on the session response and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}`, the per-entry withheld counts, `blockedBy` values, the suspended reasons `environmentScoped` and `tenantDenied`, and the environment rule's refusals on a derived runtime and a pool write.
- §16: bounds, base-status, and reservation metrics, with label cardinality stated, `TenantReservationUnmet`, and `WarmCloneFillFailing` with its declarer label and the operator's alert for a platform-declared entry.
- §11.7: `warm_store.withheld`.
- §17.2: the label webhooks, the split quota, and the cluster-scoped `lenny-reserved` `PriorityClass` in the scheme of [PM-8](placement-design.md#priority-classes), selected by the reserved quota in the agent namespace. The reserved overlay that replaces `reservedPlacement` is [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md#specification-changes)'s change.
- §17.6 and §17.8.2: the Helm values, the default for an absent `tenantAccess` on the seed, item 5's `--force-update` for `tenantAccess`, and preflight's sizing with `maxOwnedPodsTotal` and `reservedPods`.
- §24: `lenny-ctl` commands.
- §5.1 **Capabilities are customizable per tenant**: the per-tenant capability override narrows `interaction` and `injection`. [`runtime-revisions-design.md`](runtime-revisions-design.md#specification-changes) stages the change, and this design adds that the override reads the tenant view.
- §5.2 **Pool Taxonomy** and the owned pool's copy: `placement.offering` with the tenant's choice among granted offerings, and `placement.region`, which [`placement-design.md`](placement-design.md#specification-changes) stages.

## Proposals and dependencies

| Label | Proposal | Contents |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
