| DR-1 | Shared-record boundary | Removes `tenant-admin` writes on platform-global runtimes and pools, including the permissions, reads access rows on session, discovery, and delegation paths, implements the seed's `tenantAccess`, reserves `--`, and limits names to 63 characters. Closes DF-1, DF-2, DF-10, DF-14, and DF-19, and the remainder of F-5.2.67 that [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split)'s overlay does not cover. |
| DR-2 | Pods for derived runtimes | `podRuntimeRef` on the CRD and pool row, every `Runtime` read in the AgentPod controller, and the §5.1 text on fallback and `sessionPolicy`. It also covers a pool on a platform derived runtime. Closes the derived half of DF-3, DF-5, and DF-6, and DF-7 for platform-derived runtimes. |
| DR-3 | Setup command identity | Runs setup commands at the slot UID through the slot's runtime supervisor, as session isolation states for every slot. Closes DF-4 for the adapter's identity. |
| DR-4 | Tenant runtime records | The table, tenant-view interface and the holders that change type, `ErrTenantScopedName`, grant, prefix, permission, routes, field rules, Helm values, erasure, and classification, and the `registered` check at the post-chain point under "Registered rows" (AR O8), which lands with or after proposal AR-D of [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md#proposal-split-and-order) removes SD-P4. Closes DF-13, the table half of DF-12, and DF-7 for tenant-derived runtimes. |
| DR-5 | Owned pools | The template copy and its propagation, `ownedOverlays`, the readers of `lenny.dev/served`, the bounds with the base-rollout exemption, the PoolScalingController clamp, tenant operations, and tenant suspension and deletion. |
| DR-12 | Repository folders | `gitClone.path` required and never `.`, the overlap refusals, `workingDirectory` with its start check and resume fallback, `setupCommands[].cwd`, and the runtime `cwd` from session start. |
| DR-9 | Default git clones | The field on every runtime, the write checks, the composition order on every path, the refusal details, the write scope, and the guardrail values. |
| DR-11 | Shared assets and warm stores | A tenant-derived runtime's `sharedAssets` with `TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL`, the artifact upload route with its quota and garbage collection, the asset Helm values, the grant's `allowAssets`, `gitRepository` assets with their declarer and the protections of `platform`-declared ones, `maxWarmCloneBytes`, the owned pool's share of the warm list and its carrier, `prestage: true` with adoption by repository, `coveredStores` and `withheldStores` on the claim and the session row, the withholding of a store, the fill in `lenny-store-fill`, the volume, the pre-session reach, the age limit, the backoff, the fill limits, the environment rule at write and at render, and the exclusion from T of pods a platform-declared entry failed. |
| DR-10 | Capacity reservation | The Helm values, allowance, `reservedPods`, warm target, split quota, the reserved `PriorityClass` and its quota scope, the use of the placement design's reserved overlay, budget figure, reporting, and alert. |
| DR-6 | Base lifecycle | The live merge against base and grant, `baseStatus`, the outbox, retirement, emergency, and the deletion rule. Closes DF-8. |
| DR-7 | Oversight and records | The guardrail, posture, and events. |
| DR-8 | Interactions | Environment warnings, the checks at the post-chain point on the final row, and delegation resolution. |

These land early, because a stored format, a wire contract, or a shipped enforcement point would otherwise need rework: the `--` reservation in DR-1, `podRuntimeRef` in DR-2, the required `gitClone.path` and `workingDirectory` in DR-12, and `lenny.dev/served` with `ownerTenantId` in [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split). DR-1, DR-2, DR-3, and DR-12 stand on their own and fix today's tree, whether or not the rest lands.

| Proposal | Depends on | Why |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| DR-1 | None | It should land first, because it closes a Critical defect |
| DR-2 | None | |
| DR-3 | None | |
| DR-4 | DR-1, [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split), and [TS-3](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-3-session-environment-context-and-cross-environment-sharing) | The name rule, the write path and gate model, and the fail-closed gate |
| DR-4 (tenant defaults step, files and commands) | DR-3 | Neither a tenant command nor a base command reading a tenant file can run at the adapter's UID |
| DR-5 | DR-2, DR-4, and [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) | The pod path, the records, and `ownerTenantId` with the label rule |
| DR-6 | DR-4 and DR-5 | The merge and pools it acts on |
| DR-9 | DR-3, DR-4, DR-5, the [egress design](egress-and-git-design.md)'s pod-side clone, the materialization design's helper with the per-slot disk watch, and the plan check function of [SD-1](session-derive-design.md#sd-1-one-route), the derive route | A clone is code, it needs an owned pool's git path, and F-14.1.37 means no `gitClone` works in a deployed image today. A clone under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` also needs [UG-2](user-git-identity-design.md#ug-2-repository-authorization). |
| DR-11 | DR-9, draft 0111 as the egress design rewrites it and to the requirements under "Relationship to draft proposal 0111", the materialization design's helper with the warm-up checkout, the pod layout's store volume, and the `lenny-store-fill` container of the egress design's decision 61 | The clone it warms, the warm list, store, slot repository, capture copy, and restore completion it reuses, the staged checkout, the fill, and the backstop under a checkout. DR-11 adds no field to a default clone, so it can land after DR-9 with no format change to the clone. The session row's `coveredStores` lands with DR-11 or with the platform chunk sets of [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#platform-chunk-sets), whichever is first. |
| DR-10 | DR-5 | The owned pool and its budget |
| DR-7 | DR-4, and [SO-1](security-oversight-design.md#so-1-security-roles) and [SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture), whichever lands second adding the case | The role and the record |
| DR-8 | DR-4, [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping), [TS-3](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-3-session-environment-context-and-cross-environment-sharing), and the first proposal of [`runtime-routing-design.md`](runtime-routing-design.md#proposal-split-and-order) | The checks on the final row, and the post-chain point where they run |

The way [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 15](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions) gives needs DR-1, DR-2, DR-4 with neither default flag, DR-5, and [TS-1](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#ts-1-environment-scoping). It does not need DR-3. A platform-created derived runtime needs DR-2 alone, so that way has an operator's version before the rest lands. No `EG-`, `TS-`, or `SO-` proposal depends on a DR proposal, apart from the requests above.

## Documentation

Each proposal carries the documentation for its own surface. Pages state behaviour with no section numbers, and they land after the specification text they describe.

| Page | Change |
| :-- | :-- |
| `operator-guide/tenant-derived-runtimes.md` (new) | Owns the grant, template pools, budgets, reservations and how to size them, retirement, emergency, capacity headroom, and the costs above. It states that removing a tenant from a seed's `tenantAccess` revokes nothing, even under `--force-update`, which logs a `WARN`, and that a revocation is an admin API call. |
| `operator-guide/multi-tenancy.md` | Corrects what `runtime_tenant_access` does, removes tenant writes on shared records, and links the new page. |
| `getting-started/concepts.md`, **Derived runtimes** | Corrects the restrict-only statements, and states that a derived runtime needs a pool. |
| `runtime-author-guide/runtime-configuration.md` | States where setup commands run after DR-3, and which fields a tenant can set. |
| `api/admin.md`, the error reference, and `reference/configuration.md` | The routes, codes, and Helm values. |
| `operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md` | The new commands. |
| `reference/glossary.md` | Entries for tenant-derived runtime, owned pool, template pool, and derivation grant. |
| "Tenant configuration" in the operator guide chapter of [`egress-and-git-design.md`, "New chapter in the operator guide"](egress-and-git-design.md#new-chapter-in-the-operator-guide) | How a tenant admin creates a derived runtime for an environment, that default files hold no secrets, how to choose a default clone's ref, how to warm a repository with a `gitRepository` asset and when to set `prestage: true`, that a `gitRepository` asset a platform admin writes is `platform`-declared, stays through the tenant's writes, and does not count against the tenant, that `inline` and `artifact` assets hold no secrets and need the grant's `allowAssets`, how to upload an artifact for one runtime, that an `artifact` file is placed whole, and that an upload no version of its runtime references is deleted after 7 days, how to read withheld counts, suspended entries, and `blockedBy`, and how to read a reservation's status. |
| `runtime-author-guide/runtime-configuration.md`, warm stores | That stores are read-only at `/lenny/git-stores`, that a runtime image puts nothing under `/lenny` or in `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/`, and that a repository wired to a store through alternates without `lenny.*` keys gets no store service. |
| `runtime-author-guide/runtime-configuration.md`, `sharedAssets` | A `gitRepository` entry is the only way to warm a repository, `prestage: true` stages a checkout in each idle slot that the first `gitClone` source of the repository in the defaults or the plan adopts at its own `path`, and the entry adds no source to a session's plan. `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` lists repositories every session gets and has no `warm` field. |
| `reference/error-catalog.md` and `api/rest.md` | A create, derive, or delegated child onto a runtime whose row is not `registered` answers as an unknown runtime, with no reason that names the row's state, and an operator reads the `Runtime` resource's `Registered` condition to find the cause. |

**What the pages list (decision D13 of [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#decisions), which the owner extended to every design on 2026-11-13).** The pages above state every item of this design that an operator, a tenant admin, a runtime author, or a client meets:

- **Settings and defaults.** Every row of "Configuration by scope" with its default: `runtimes.tenantDerivation.enabled` (false), `maxOwnedPodsTotal`, `baseRetirementNoticeSeconds` (2592000), `maxWorkspaceDefaultsBytes`, `maxDefaultGitClones` (8), `maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling` (32), `allowAssets` (false), `maxAssetsBytes` (256 MiB), `maxAssetEntryBytes` (64 MiB), `maxInlineAssetBytes` (1 MiB), `maxAssetUploadBytes` (1 GiB), `warmClones.maxBytesPerPod`, `warmClones.totalBytes`, `warmClones.defaultMaxAgeSeconds` (86400), `warmClones.minAgeSeconds` (3600), `warmClones.maxAgeSeconds` (86400), `warmClones.failureThreshold`, `warmClones.maxConcurrentFillsPerTenant`, `reservations.enabled` (false), `reservations.totalPods`, `reservations.reservedRequests`, `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries`, `controller.tuning.gitStoreFillTimeout`, and `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationCeilingSeconds` (600); the grant's fields with their defaults (every flag off, `allowAssets` included, `maxWarmCloneBytes` 0, `maxAssetUploadBytes` and `maxDefaultGitClones` at their chart values, `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds` at `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`, `maxReservedPods` empty, `ownedOverlays` empty), with the operator's per-tenant choice of the upload limit, the default clone cap up to the ceiling, and the warm-up checkout timeout bound; the pool's `warmCloneBytes` and `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds` (unset on a platform pool, which means no age limit); and each derived runtime field with the rule of "Fields".
- **Node and cluster requirements.** `lenny-agent-storage-quota` sized with every pod's ephemeral-storage request, the sum of its disk-backed volumes' `sizeLimit` values with every pool's `warmCloneBytes` among them plus its resource class's own request, 1 GiB in the shipped classes, and the reserved pods included; nodes named by a `placement.reservedOverlay` labelled and tainted by the operator; the reflink rebuild needing XFS or btrfs under runc and falling back to a checkout under gVisor and Kata; and `lenny-store-fill` as an init container of every pod that renders a warm list.
- **Limits.** Names of at most 63 characters under a prefix with no `--`; the charge T and `maxOwnedPods`; one store per normalized URL holding one commit; the age limit; the tree-size check against `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` and `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries`; and the one-way `oneSessionPerPod`.
- **Failure modes and their answers.** `403 DERIVATION_NOT_GRANTED`, `422 DERIVATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `409 DERIVED_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE` with each `details.reason`, `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`, `422 TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL` as an internal guard that no request reaches today, `403 PLACEMENT_OFFERING_NOT_GRANTED`, `403 REGION_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATED`, `429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming a removed field, such as `supportedProviders`, `credentialCapabilities`, `workspaceDefaults.gitClones[].warm`, `pool.warmCaches`, `warmRepositories`, or a `gitRepository` entry's `path`, or naming `ref` on a tenant's `artifact` entry, `429 STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` on an upload past `maxAssetUploadBytes`, the unknown-runtime answer for a row that is not `registered`, `USER_REPOSITORY_ACCESS_DENIED` and `LINKS_REQUIRED` with `details.source: runtimeDefault`, `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE`, `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` from `git_tree_limit`, `SESSION_CREATION_FAILED` with `working_directory_invalid`, a failed fill taking the pod to `failed` with the backoff and `WarmCloneFillFailing`, `TenantReservationUnmet`, `PoolScaleoutBlockedByQuota`, the `PrestageSkipped` event and `lenny_prestage_skipped_total` when a warm-up checkout skips an entry, the `Warning` on staged trees above half of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, the `withheldWarmEntries` reasons, the suspended reasons `environmentScoped` and `tenantDenied`, and the `blockedBy` values.
- **Runtime-facing rules.** The runtime starts in the `cwd` that `session_start` carries, under `/workspace`; on an owned pool `/lenny/assets` holds the tenant's assets beside the base's; a runtime image puts nothing under `/lenny` or in `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/`; stores are read-only at `/lenny/git-stores`; `workspace.working_directory_fallback` on a resume; a repository wired to a store without `lenny.*` keys gets no store service; and a snapshot leaves out a warm repository's base history, which restore fetches.
- **Checkpoint references.** A session's snapshots and output files reference platform chunk sets only of the stores in its stored `coveredStores`, of its runtime revision's assets, and on a tenant-derived runtime of the runtime's tenant-owned assets set, never of a withheld store.

## Tests

Each proposal turns this list into tests with `// spec:` annotations at the tiers `test-coverage.md` maps. No test exists yet.

### Tier 0: static

- The schemas of the new routes and the CRD fields validate. The session-path holders compile with the tenant-view interface and no plain `Store`.

### Tier 1: unit

- Every row of the field tables: accepted, refused, and narrow, for each `sessionPolicy` limit. `oneSessionPerPod: true` on a concurrent template and a recycling template gives one session with no recycling and no acknowledgment, and a template widening does not reach it. `false` copies the template. `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` at any value, an acknowledgment, and each `recycle` field are refused. A `PUT` omitting `oneSessionPerPod` leaves it set, and a `PUT` setting `false` on a row holding `true` is refused, with no session placed on an occupied or served pod. A row with `oneSessionPerPod: true` passes pool validation on a recycling template. An owned limit stays the lower of the two values across template writes in both directions. A row with `oneSessionPerPod: false` is non-compliant with `reusedPods` on a template that reuses no pods, and a template widening leaves a row with `oneSessionPerPod: true` unchanged. A clone write on such a row over a reusing template passes with the field omitted. A zero limit resolves to its default before the comparison.
- `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds`: a tenant value below the base's interval answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` and one at or above it is accepted, the floor reads the owned pool's value, then the base Runtime's, then the gateway default, and a later base raise above the tenant's value gives sessions the base's interval at the next resolve and leaves the runtime compatible.
- Name and prefix grammar, including `a--b--x` and a trailing `-`, prefix mismatch, 63-character limit, platform refusal of `--`, and `ErrTenantScopedName` from `Store.Get`.
- The merge against base and grant, for each row of "Base changes", including an emptied set. With two base revisions in traffic, a runtime that one of them breaks reads as `incompatible`, a grant condition that one of them fails refuses the entry, and the notice fires when the breaking revision is created. A base write that raises the setup bound past an owned pool's window is refused with the pools named and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` that fits.
- `AdmitTenantPin` and `markPinnedIdle` across owned and shared pools, served and unserved, recycling and not.
- T at the bound: a claim and a write that reach it pass, and those that pass it are refused, a lowering always passes, warming pods are counted once, `terminated` `AgentPod` objects are not counted, a tenant's pods on shared pools are not counted, and a `ClaimSlot` on an occupied pod leaves T unchanged. T holds across several pools, with and without reservations. The WarmPoolController creates no `AgentPod` at the owner's bound, after a lowering followed by a raise, after a `DELETE` with pods draining, and for a reservation's replacement under short sessions. The clamp on the PoolScalingController formula. During a base rollout, the extra pod set counts in neither T nor the creation rule, carries no `spec.reserved` mark, and the pool counts as before once the rollout ends.
- A tenant schema with a `file:` `$ref`, a remote `$ref`, `$schema`, or `$id`, a non-compiling schema, and an oversized schema are refused at write, and one with a local `#/...` `$ref` passes. A session's options pass only when the base revision's schema and the tenant's schema both accept them, including a value the base allows and the tenant's schema refuses, and a tenant schema that names a property outside the base's properties is accepted at write. A base revision that forbids a property the tenant's schema names leaves the runtime compatible, and one that forbids a property the tenant's schema requires makes it `incompatible`, with the notice naming the property and the revision.
- Default clones: each write check refuses, including an uncovered host with a full commit, a write scope, an `auth`-less repository under a pool prefix, `submodules`, `path: .`, a `.git` component in any case, overlapping paths, a path overlapping a base default clone, a URL with userinfo, and a `warm` field with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`. `ComposeInto` places the base's clones, then the tenant's, after files and ahead of client sources. On an operator runtime, a default clone passes the runtime write without a tenant check, a `write` other than `none` answers `422 INVALID_RUNTIME`, a floating ref and a pool with no git path each draw a `Warning`, the floating-ref clone appears in each entitled tenant's posture, operator clones do not count toward `maxDefaultGitClones`, and a session whose tenant's VCS pool does not cover the host is refused at bind as for a client's clone. A platform-derived runtime's clones append after its base's, and one whose `path` overlaps a base clone or base default file is refused with `422 INVALID_RUNTIME`.
- Tenant `inline` and `artifact` assets: with the Helm `allowAssets` false, with the grant's `allowAssets` false, or before DR-3 any entry is refused, and with all three in place an entry passes. A path with `..`, a `.` component, a leading `.lenny`, or a `.git` component, an absolute path, a path equal to, above, or below a base asset's path in any revision in the base's traffic, two overlapping tenant paths, an `inline` entry above `maxInlineAssetBytes`, `inline` entries above 4 MiB together, an `artifact` entry above `maxAssetEntryBytes`, a total above `maxAssetsBytes`, an `artifact` digest the tenant did not upload, and a digest uploaded for another runtime are each refused with `INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` and `details.field`, and an `artifact` entry carrying `ref` answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `details.field`. An `artifact` file that is an archive lands whole at its `destPath`, unextracted. An upload is chunked into the runtime's tenant-owned assets set and stored nowhere else, an upload past the grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes`, or past the chart value under a grant without one, answers `429 STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, an upload that no version of the runtime in use references is deleted after 7 days and one a pod set still holds is kept, and deleting the runtime deletes its uploads. `TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL` is an internal guard that no request reaches, so a unit test of the render, given an owned pool whose `ownerTenantId` is another tenant's, checks that it answers `422 TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL`. A later base asset at a tenant path, `allowAssets` set false, and a lowered `maxAssetsBytes` each make the runtime `incompatible`. `workspaceFiles` in `derivedRuntimes.forbid` refuses an `inline` or `artifact` asset.
- `plan.Compute` given a floor above `warmCount` creates up to the floor, `ForceZeroMinWarm` renders `spec.reservedPods` as 0, and a `scaleToZero` window leaves it. The warm target at `active` below, equal to, and above `reservedPods`, with `draining` and `failed` not counted, a `scaleToZero` window not lowering it, and `warm_stop_reasons` overriding it. T with a reservation: a claim within it passes at the bound even with pods draining, a `reservedPods` write that takes T past the bound is refused, and pods stay at `reservedPods + warmCount` after those claims.
- The warm list render: entries naming one normalized URL render one store, a tenant entry's ref wins over an operator entry's, the merged store is staged when either entry carries `prestage: true`, and each rendered entry carries its declarer and `prestage`. A base's public entry renders on a platform pool and on every owned pool of the base, and a tenant-derived runtime's `gitRepository` assets render on its owned pool alone. A Runtime entry carrying `auth` is refused, and a write naming a `gitRepository` entry's `path`, a pool write naming `warmRepositories`, and a derived-runtime write naming `pool.warmCaches` each answer `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field. A `gitRepository` asset a `platform-admin` writes through the derived-runtimes route renders with declarer `platform`, a tenant's `PUT` that omits it keeps it, a tenant's `PUT` that carries it unchanged as `GET` returned it is accepted, and a tenant's `PUT` that changes any of its fields answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`. A store that a `gitRepository` asset of the runtime shares with a base's public entry contributes its staged trees to the tenant-owned set of the URL whichever entry sets `prestage: true`, a `platform`-declared asset's store contributes to the tenant-owned set, and a store that only a base's public entry declares contributes to the platform-owned set.
- T with platform failures: a pod whose `fillFailure` names a platform-declared entry, a base's public entry or a `platform`-declared asset, counts in no term of T, alerts the operator, and reports `blockedBy: platformEntry`, a `platform`-declared asset whose host the tenant denies is suspended with `tenantDenied`, a pod a tenant entry failed counts as `unusable`, and a store a tenant entry shares with a platform entry records `tenant` as its declarer.
- The environment predicate at write: a `gitRepository` asset whose repository resolves at E = ∅ only to a scoped VCS pool is refused with `INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME`, whether a tenant admin or a platform admin writes it. A repository under no prefix passes, and a repository that a tenant-wide pool and a narrower scoped pool both cover passes. A Runtime write whose public entry fails the predicate for one tenant passes with a `Warning` naming the owned pools.
- The predicate at render: a VCS pool write that adds `environments` to the covering pool passes with a `Warning`, the entry leaves the rendered list and reads as suspended with `environmentScoped`, and it returns when the pool is unscoped. A platform entry whose host the owning tenant denies renders off that owned pool with `tenantDenied`.
- `gitRepository` assets at write: each check under "Tenant shared assets" refuses, including a duplicate `name`, two entries naming one URL, a URL with userinfo, an uncovered host, a host outside the ceiling, a ref beginning with `-`, a grant with `maxWarmCloneBytes` at 0, a count past the grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones` with the default clones, and an entry under `userAuthorization: required` on an owned pool that reuses pods. A `tenant`-declared entry is refused with `allowGitClones` off and before DR-3, and `gitClones` in `derivedRuntimes.forbid` refuses one. A `platform`-declared entry passes with `allowGitClones` off, does not count toward `maxDefaultGitClones`, and passes under `gitClones` in `derivedRuntimes.forbid`.
- Grant bounds: a grant entry with `maxDefaultGitClones: 10` admits a runtime with 10 default clones and assets together and refuses an eleventh; an entry that omits the field reads `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClones`; a grant write above `maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling` is refused, and a chart whose default exceeds the ceiling fails to render; a grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes` limits that tenant's uploads, and a grant without one reads the chart value. A tenant's `prestageTimeoutSeconds` of zero or above the grant entry's `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds` answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`; the rendered timeout is the runtime's value, else the base revision's, else the owned pool's, else `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`; and a lowered `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds` renders the smaller value on new pods.
- The owned pool's `placement.offering`: an offering the tenant may use whose `classes` include the template's node class is accepted, any other answers `403 PLACEMENT_OFFERING_NOT_GRANTED`, and a create on a template whose `placement.region` differs from the tenant's `dataResidencyRegion` answers `422 REGION_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATED`. A change to a tenant asset, including a `gitRepository` asset's `prestage`, changes the pod-set key, and a change to the base's public entry creates a base revision instead.
- The per-tenant capability override: a widening of `interaction` or `injection` is refused, a narrowing passes, and a narrowing that clears a capability clears its dependents.
- A base write that raises the isolation floor above a template pool's profile is refused and names the template pool without naming an owned pool, and passes after the template's profile is raised.

### Tier 2: component

- The PoolScalingController renders `podRuntimeRef` and the owner, the controller creates a pod from the base's `Runtime` resource, and a missing base fails closed. With revisions, a base rollout gives an owned pool a `PodSet` per active base revision, each with `podRuntimeRef` naming the base and `runtimeRevision` naming the revision, each pod set's pods are built from its revision with the derived fields merged over it, and each pod set renders its own revision's warm list.
- The webhook admits the owner label at creation and refuses every other tenant change.
- The chart renders the split with `lenny-agent-storage-quota` unscoped, and it applies on a cluster. `lenny-agent-quota` admits a pod with no priority class under its `NotIn` scope.
- The WarmPoolController marks `AgentPod` objects reserved up to `reservedPods` and no further, a marked `AgentPod`'s pod carries `lenny-reserved` and the template's `placement.reservedOverlay`, an overlay that names another class's taint or a `lenny.dev/tenant` key is refused at the template write, a replacement after a reserved pod ends is marked, unmarked idle pods drain first, and a podless unmarked `AgentPod` is replaced by a marked one while marks fall short. With `reservations.enabled` false, stored floors do not apply and no pod is marked.

### Tier 3: contract

- The route bodies refuse unknown fields, the error codes match §15.1, and `GET /v1/admin/derived-runtimes/{name}` reports `baseStatus`.
- `CH-SUPERVISE` carries a setup run with the DR-3 outcome reports.
- `runtimeDefault` survives the stored plan, the schema, the mapping to `WorkspaceSource`, and the adapter's decode, and a client's plan setting it is refused.

### Tier 4: integration

- Row-level security: a query with no context sees no row, tenant `globex`'s context sees none of `acme`'s, and `InAllTenants` sees both.
- A create writes the runtime and pool together, and a failed pool write leaves no runtime.
- Concurrent creates cannot exceed `maxRuntimes` or `maxOwnedPods`, a claim at the bound answers `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, and a grant write cannot push the sum past `maxOwnedPodsTotal`.
- A template write copies to owned pools and bumps each generation, a flag-lowering write succeeds and stops an owned overlay's extension, a base write succeeds with incompatible derived runtimes present, and retirement moves through its states.
- Each session surface refuses a `--` name it cannot resolve, and `setupCommandPolicy.shell: false` reaches the adapter for a tenant-derived runtime.
- A platform `PUT` and `warm-count` on an owned pool succeed. A delegation policy's deletion guard sees its own tenant's reference, and another tenant's reference neither blocks the delete nor appears in the answer.
- A grant entry's `retireAt` moves its runtimes through retirement, an emergency write takes effect at once, and `DELETE .../tenant-access/{tenantId}` answers `409` while the entry is live. With `enabled` false, a narrowing grant write passes and a raise is refused.
- A default clone passes through the auth binding check, the repository authorization check, and the commit pin on create, create-and-start, finalize, and a delegated child, and a create with no plan refuses before any claim.
- A `gitClone` with no `path` or with `path: .` answers `WORKSPACE_PLAN_INVALID`, two overlapping clones are refused, and a client clone meeting a default clone is refused with no URL in the details. A file inside a clone's folder follows the collision rule. A derive refuses before the claim.
- A session with `workingDirectory` starts its runtime there and runs its setup commands there, a command's `cwd` overrides it, and the runtime default applies when the plan omits it. A missing folder, a file, and a symbolic link each fail the start with `SESSION_CREATION_FAILED` and `working_directory_invalid`, before setup and again before start, and on a resume or a `from: latest` derive the same cases start in `/workspace` with `workspace.working_directory_fallback`. A client's `workingDirectory` does not move an operator's or tenant's default command. Composition writes each command's folder on it as `cwd`, an `operator` command that a derive onto another runtime relabels `client` keeps that `cwd`, and an `additionalSetupCommands` entry with no `cwd` runs in the plan's working directory. The session row's `cwd` and `expected_workspace_root` hold `/workspace` on every pod and after a derive.
- Each `write` value on create, finalize, and a delegated child: `clientOptIn` with and without the flag, `always` with no plan, a read-only parent with a child under `always`, and a user with read and no write. 
- A pool's `warmCloneMaxAgeSeconds` outside the Helm bounds is refused at write, an unset value drains at the default, a changed value applies at the next drain decision with no roll, and a stored value above a lowered bound drains at the bound.
- A warm pod reports ready only after its fill. A session binds with a store at the same commit, an older commit, and no store. A pod holding a store outside `coveredStores` is refused at an idle acquisition and on `ClaimSlot`'s second pass, and a pod created under an earlier list that holds no such store is claimed. Idle pods holding a store off the rendered list drain, and the age drain continues while fills fail. A `write` clone on an owned pool that reuses pods is refused, a clone under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` holds the policy too, a `gitRepository` asset on such a pool answers with a `Warning`, its store is fetched once and each session gets its own tree, adopted from the warm-up checkout or checked out at bind, a new session on a running pod can read a removed repository's store until the pod retires, a template raise does not reach a pool with `oneSessionPerPod` in either order against a concurrent `PUT`, and removing the clone leaves the field set. A restore onto a pod with no store completes with a shallow fetch, completes a repository whose pinned commit was amended when its history fits, and fails when it does not. A resume onto a pod holding a store added after bind claims for a user who passes the check. A Pending pod at the quota neither stops the age drain nor uses the fill limit, and a loop of writes with a bad URL stays in backoff. A fill past `warmCloneBytes` fails, repeated failures back off, and a grant past `totalBytes` is refused. A resume after a `gitRepository` asset was added runs the check and withholds the store from a user with no access, and on a pod with several slots it fails. A tree above `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` or `adapter.maxCloneTreeEntries` is refused before checkout by `lenny-materialize` with `git_tree_limit`, which reaches the client as `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, including from a commit the store holds, a tree of repeated subtrees and a tree of empty files stop the listing early, and two clones cannot sum past the limit. [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md) lists the derive cases.
- One store per URL: a tenant `gitRepository` asset and a base's public entry naming one repository fill one store on an owned pod, at the tenant entry's ref.
- A store serves any source of its URL: a default clone and a client source of the entry's URL, at another branch, each fetch only the commits the store lacks, a source with `depth` or `submodules: true` takes the ordinary path, and a session whose workspace does not name the URL gets no checkout of it.
- Tenant assets on the pod: an owned pool's pods hold the tenant's `inline` and `artifact` files under `/lenny/assets` beside the base's, read-only in every slot, the template pool's pods hold none, `lenny-assets`' `sizeLimit` counts both, an upload's digest pins the file, and a second upload changes no pod set until a write names its digest. A checkpoint of a session on the runtime references the tenant-owned assets set, and a session of another runtime of the same tenant does not.
- Withhold and drain: a session whose environment denies the host of a `gitRepository` asset its plan does not name, on a one-session pool, proceeds with the store deleted before materialization, the response and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` list the entry in `withheldWarmEntries` without its URL, the tenant admin's `GET` counts it, `warm_store.withheld` is written, the claim admits the pod through `withheldStores`, and the pod drains when the session ends. The same holds on `ClaimSlot`'s first pass and on `rebindReserved` on a recycling pool. A resume after a `gitRepository` asset was added withholds that store for a user who fails its checks, and a derive does the same.
- Concurrent refusal: on a pod with `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod` above 1, a failed check on an entry the plan does not name refuses the create, and a resume that would withhold fails.
- Warm-up checkout: an idle slot of an owned pool holds a staged tree of each entry with `prestage: true` under `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/`, with the index refreshed, at warm-up and again after a slot release. The first `gitClone` source of the repository in the composed workspace, a default clone or a client source, adopts it at its own `path`; a session whose resolved commit equals the staged one adopts it with no checkout, and one at a later commit checks out in place; the adopted tree's inode numbers and modification times are unchanged and `git status` reports no change without rereading file contents; a second source of the same URL clones from the store; a source with `depth` does not adopt; a target that already holds a default file follows the collision rule; the adopted tree's `origin` is the adopting source's URL; and a staged tree no source adopts never appears in a checkpoint, download, or export, and is visible to the session only under `.lenny-discard-<id>` until its `discard` ends. A `prestage` whose tree-size check stops an entry, or that times out, skips that entry, the slot still reports ready, a session that clones the repository builds it from the store, and `lenny_prestage_skipped_total` and a `PrestageSkipped` event record the skip. A runtime write whose entries' staged trees exceed half of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` answers with a `Warning`. Under the reflink rebuild on runc with XFS, a slot's files belong to the slot UID and share extents with the pristine tree, and a write in one slot changes no other slot's copy; on gVisor the helper falls back to a normal checkout. The adapter runs no git on a slot repository at any step.
- Concurrent pods: an assignment carrying a withhold list onto a pod that has a session is refused by the adapter and refuses the session.
- `coveredStores` on the session row: a create, a resume onto another pod, `ClaimSlot`'s first pass, and `rebindReserved` each write the sets their read checks produced, a resume replaces the earlier sets, a checkpoint and an export after each reference platform chunk sets only of stores in the stored `coveredStores`, and a withheld store's set is never referenced.
- `lenny-store-fill`: a pod fills its stores and pristine trees in the init container before the adapter starts, the container holds no service account token and no credential volume, its fetches reach `netagent` attributed to the platform fill, a failed fill records the store in `fillFailure` from the container's termination message, and the adapter deletes a withheld store through its read-write mount without running git.
- An operator entry with `prestage: true` on a platform pool is staged in each idle slot, a session whose plan clones that repository at any `path` adopts it there, and a session that does not clone it discards it. An operator entry without `prestage` stages nothing.
- On a concurrent pod where `netagent` attributes connections to slots, a session whose environment denies the host of a `gitRepository` asset its plan does not name is refused with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE` naming the entry without its URL.
- Operator failures: a failing base entry backs off an owned pool's pod creation after the threshold, the `AgentPod` records the store and `platform` in `fillFailure`, the tenant admin's `GET` reports `blockedBy: platformEntry` with its URL, the operator's alert fires, and the tenant's T does not rise. A failing base entry on a shared platform pool backs off the same way.
- A grant write cannot push allowances past `totalPods`, counting live marked pods above an allowance. A lowered allowance or `maxOwnedPods` lowers `reservedPods`.
- A `DELETE` with live sessions changes nothing. Suspension drains owned pools, and resuming restores them. Tenant deletion and erasure remove the table, grant, and pools, and a consumed prefix stays refused.

### Tier 5: end to end on Kind

- A seeded install with no `tenantAccess` starts a session on its seeded runtime, and the embedded stack starts one on its CRD-only pool.
- The scenario of [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 15](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions): a concurrent base, an owned one-session pool, and a session with E's full policy.
- With the general quota full, a reserved pod starts and a general pod is refused at quota admission. With nodes full and no dedicated pool, a reserved pod stays pending and no other pod is evicted.
- Warm pods stay in inventory on an owned non-recycling pool, a served pod drains, and a recycling owned pool reuses a pod for its owner within the reserved hold and drains it afterwards. A non-empty `warm_stop_reasons` drains served idle pods.

### Tier 6: end to end on cloud

- Materialization on a gVisor pod opens paths under `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` and refuses a link.

### Tier 7: load

- Many concurrent creates and session starts across tenants, with the lock on the grant row under race detection.

### Tier 8: chaos

- A failed read of the tenant table fails a session create closed. A failed scan does not fail a platform base write, and its outbox row retries until `runtime.base_changed` goes out.

### Tier 9: security

- DF-1: a `tenant-admin` holding a grant, and a custom role naming either permission, gets `403` on each field of the runtime and pool `PUT`, and on `drain`. A custom role write naming either permission is refused.
- A `tenant-admin`'s route naming another tenant's runtime or owned pool as a target gets the unknown answer, and a route moving a session onto an owned pool of another tenant is refused at the post-chain point.
- DF-2: a tenant with no grant cannot create on, discover, or delegate to a platform runtime.
- Each surface answers identically for another tenant's name and for an absent one: admin reads, `GET /v1/runtimes`, metadata, `/v1/models`, session create, delegate, route write, and `pool` pin.
- DF-4, on a one-session pod: a setup command's process runs at the slot UID, and cannot read the service account token or write the credential volume.
- A default command outside the base's allowlist, or under a policy with `shell: true`, is refused at write and at bind. A tenant file on a path a base file holds is refused.
- `allowSetupCommands` or `allowWorkspaceFiles` set false makes a runtime holding that default refuse new sessions.
- A tenant-set variable outside the chart's allowlist, such as `NODE_OPTIONS` or `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, is refused on an owned pool, whatever the copied flag says.
- A create, a derive, and a delegated child, with and without exports, whose final runtime row is not `registered` are refused with the unknown-runtime answer, including when a runtime route or a `PreRoute` interceptor moves the session there after the export step, and the child's exports are undone. A platform-derived runtime whose base row is not `registered` is refused the same way. The same requests onto a `registered` runtime pass, and a resume onto an unregistered runtime's row is not refused. A runtime write naming `type` or `executionMode` answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field.
- A `--` name from another tenant creates no admission rate-limit entry for its pool, and `verbatimClientFiles: forbid` refuses a default file.
- Revoking access after a route's write refuses the sessions and delegated children it moves, at the post-chain point, for a runtime with no `--`.
- A flag-lowering write emits `runtime.base_changed` to a tenant with an overlay and no grant. A tenant `PUT` does not clear `warm_stop_reasons`.
- A pod of an owned pool cannot be claimed for another tenant, even when the gateway's first layer is skipped.
- A restored symbolic link chain, and a single link an agent wrote in the live tree, fail materialization for an `inlineFile`, `uploadFile`, clone, and move, and nothing is written outside the slot.
- A user with no read access on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` is refused on a default clone, the answer omits the repository name, no `ls-refs` runs, and no App token is minted. A `.git/config`, `commondir`, or hook already in a clone's target, including from a restored snapshot, runs nothing in the helper's git and does not survive the move, and the adapter runs no git on the slot repository. A default-clone refusal on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: off` for a repository made private carries no URL. A `floatingCloneRefs` forbid suspends a runtime with a branch ref. `verbatimClientFiles: forbid` refuses a default clone. A route target brings its clones under the same checks.
- A hostile repository in a warm fill is parsed only in `lenny-store-fill`, which holds no service account token and no credential, and the adapter's container runs no git. The hostile-repository stress suite of the egress design's decision 61 runs on runc, gVisor, and Kata: setuid and setgid files, symbolic-link and hard-link farms, deep and wide trees, mode-`000` directories, name collisions, very large files, and the deletion of a withheld store while a slot reads it leave no slot with write access to a store, the store group, or a set-ID bit. An unbound owned pod reads its warm list and nothing else, and gets no write token. An agent cannot write a store from its slot container. A commit the store holds is refused at bind for a user who lacks access, with no pod claimed. `write: always` with a pattern outside the namespace is refused at write, as is `always` under `prAuthoring: user`, and a push to the default branch is refused by `netagent`. A planted `lenny.gitStoreUrl` causes no fetch of that URL. `writableDefaultClones` and `alwaysWritableClones` suspend the runtimes they match. A kept tree's planted `.git/config`, hook, and alternates run nothing in the helper's `capture-copy` or `restore-complete`, and nothing at the adapter's UID.
- A withheld store is gone before any setup command, restore completion, or runtime start: a setup command listing `/lenny/git-stores` finds no directory for it, the slot container cannot open it, a tree staged from it under `/workspace/.lenny-prestage/` is gone, and a restored repository whose `lenny.gitStoreUrl` names its URL gets no alternates and no fetch. No runtime process exists in the slot before assignment, so none holds a descriptor under `/lenny/git-stores`.
- An agent-written `lenny.gitStoreUrl` outside the URLs the gateway passed for the bind causes no fetch, and a repository wired to a store through alternates with no `lenny.*` keys cannot read a withheld store.
- A tenant with no allowance cannot set `reservedPods`, and no tenant write changes a Helm total or `placement.reservedOverlay`.
- A tenant asset never reaches another tenant: no pod of a shared pool or of another tenant's owned pool mounts it, another tenant's session cannot reference its chunk set, an upload is stored under the owning tenant's key, tenant deletion erases the uploads and the set, and a legal hold keeps them. A tenant `inline` path such as `../x`, `.lenny-prestage`, or `.git/hooks/x` is refused, and nothing a tenant asset writes lands outside `/lenny/assets`.
- A forbid in the tenant security record ([SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)) refuses a write, suspends existing runtimes, and a removal waits out the delay.

### Tier 10: conformance

- Reference runtimes run setup commands from the runtime image after DR-3.

### Tier 11: documentation

- The page's examples validate against the schemas and the chart, and each error code resolves.

## Decisions

The owner has settled every decision below, and the design above states each one. The owner settled decision 4: tenants can define default files, setup commands, and git clones, so its alternative of no tenant defaults is gone, and the owner chose the grant flags in the words "separate flags, off by default". The owner settled decision 17 and decision 20 as recommended, the latter in the words "go with dedicated pools". The owner settled decision 16 as recommended, in the words "go with your recommendation", and settled decisions 22 and 27 on 2026-11-04. The owner settled decisions 1, 3, 6, 7, and 8 as recommended on 2026-11-04, each in the word "ok". The owner settled decisions 9 to 12 as recommended the same day, in the words "go with your recommendations", and decisions 5 and 28, in the words "yes" and "makes sense, yes". The owner settled decision 2 as recommended the same day, after considering an additional grant covering all tenants, in the words "no all-tenant grant. Since grants can be configured via API, it's not a big deal to have grants that are specific to tenants". The owner settled decisions 29 and 30 as recommended on 2026-11-08, in the words "go with your recommendations". The owner settled decisions 31 to 37 on 2026-11-08, after an assessment of the operator's `gitRepository` entry against the warm clone and a check of its open points, and settled decision 38 as recommended and decision 39 later that day. The owner settled decision 40 on 2026-11-09, during the review that compared every design with the others, and decisions 42 and 43 on 2026-11-14.

| # | Decision | Recommendation | Cost of the recommendation | Alternative |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| 1 | Where tenant records live | **Settled.** A separate tenant-scoped table with a platform-assigned name prefix | Every session-path read changes, a customer's names carry a prefix, and the prefix appears in operator logs and metric labels | A nullable owner on `runtime_definitions`, which is less code and relies on application filtering, or a composite key, which allows any name and needs a global identifier beside it for pods and CRDs |
| 2 | Who turns it on | **Settled.** A per-tenant platform grant listing bases, templates, and budgets | An operator acts for each tenant and each base | A deployment-wide switch over every runtime a tenant holds an access row on, which is less work and gives no per-tenant limit |
| 3 | Raising `maxConcurrentSessions` | **Settled.** Refused. Decision 28 also removes lowering to a middle value. | A tenant wanting higher density asks the operator to raise the template | A ceiling in the grant above the template's value, which moves the acknowledgment to the operator per tenant and adds cost per pod for a tenant wanting it |
| 4 | Tenant defaults | **Settled.** Files, commands, and clones each under their own grant flag, off by default, on a supervised base, after DR-3. Commands also need `allowlist` and `shell: false`. | Where a flag is on, a stolen `tenant-admin` runs code at the agent UID in every new session on that runtime, reading `file` tokens and using `inject` tokens. DR-3 is new work. | No tenant defaults in the first version, which serves R2 and removes this cost, and leaves a tenant needing files or tools to ask an operator. Files without commands is no middle ground, because a file is code. |
| 5 | Pool for each runtime | **Settled.** Always an owned pool, with no fallback | One warm pod at least per runtime, while DF-11 stands | A fallback to a non-recycling, one-session base pool, which costs no warm pods and does not serve the case of [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` decision 15](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md#decisions) |
| 6 | Bounding capacity | **Settled.** `maxOwnedPods` per tenant, checked at write and claim, with the sum of grants at or below a deployment total | The bound is a pod count, the count at claim can overshoot, an operator sizes each grant up front, and the namespace `ResourceQuota` stays shared apart from DR-10's split | A dedicated agent namespace for owned pools, with its own quota. That isolates capacity and needs multi-namespace work in `PoolStoreSource`, `ResolvePool`, and the chart |
| 7 | Template values | **Settled.** Copied, and propagated in the platform write's transaction | A platform write touches every owned row, and a widening reaches tenants at once | Read-through at each use, which needs a new merge in each reader |
| 8 | Base retirement | **Settled.** A notice period of 30 days by default, with an emergency path | A platform waits on retirement, and the figure is a judgment | No notice, which strands tenants, or tenants blocking as today, which R6 refuses |
| 9 | A delay on tenant commands at a tenant with a security record | **Settled.** None on create, with a forbid and an immediate event | A stolen `tenant-admin` at a tenant with no forbid has its command run until someone acts | [SO-2](security-oversight-design.md#so-2-tenant-security-record-and-posture)'s weakening delay on each command change, or an approved-digest list like `tokenTrust.allowedRecords`, the guardrail that lists the token trust record digests a tenant may use. Each is additive and slows every legitimate change |
| 10 | Service-mode bases | **Moot** since 2026-11-13, when [`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md) removed service mode (AR D1 and O2). As settled earlier: **Settled.** Refused in the first version | A tenant using a service-mode runtime has no derivation, and an environment needing per-session policy still needs an operator | Allowed with a narrow field set. It gives little, because service mode materializes no workspace, and it depends on a per-pool Service whose creator was not established |
| 11 | Public metadata and cards | **Settled.** Refused, and generated cards carry `tenant`. Since revision 76, Lenny generates no card under [`rest-only-client-api-design.md`](rest-only-client-api-design.md), and a tenant-derived runtime stays out of R8's public runtime index because it carries no `public` entry | A tenant's agent is not discoverable across organizations | A grant flag with a content-type allowlist, which publishes customer content on the operator's origin |
| 12 | `tenant-admin` writes on shared records | **Settled.** Removed entirely | §10.2's stated ability goes, including a tenant's scaling of a shared pool | Per-tenant overlays on runtime fields. Derived runtimes meet that need, and [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md)'s overlay covers egress and git settings |
| 14 | Overlay rows only an owned pool has | **Settled.** Read as `none` unless the grant entry's `ownedOverlays` lists them, with a named variable list, the exposure of an LLM credential lease on a pool with `egressProfile: internet` stated as a residual, and a posture row and event for an added CA | An operator lists each row per base, a tenant needing its own CA or `gitAccess` asks, and a listed `clientProfile` sets variables only from the named list and only on a pool with `egressProfile: internet` that runs no `netagent`, and it sets file templates on any pool as shared-pool overlays do, subject to the `verbatimClientFiles` constraint of [`security-oversight-design.md`](security-oversight-design.md). The owner amended this wording on 2026-11-09, in the word "ok", so that it matches DR-5 | Follow the copied flags as [EG-9](egress-and-git-design.md#proposal-split) states, which opens rows the operator set the flag without considering |
| 15 | An absent `tenantAccess` on a seeded runtime or pool | **Settled.** A `WARN` names a runtime or pool left with no access, and `--force-update` only adds listed tenants. Grants `default` when the seed names no tenant, the seed's tenant when it names exactly one, and none when it names several | A seed naming several tenants lists each tenant's access, or its sessions are refused | Every seeded tenant, which is less to write and over-grants, or none, which breaks §4.2's statement that a single-tenant deployer need not think about tenants |
| 16 | A default clone's write scope | **Settled.** `write: none`, `clientOptIn`, or `always`, default `none`. `clientOptIn` uses a session flag that names no repository. `always` needs `allowGitCloneWrites` and a ref namespace, and a `security-admin` can forbid either value. A user who can read and not write gets a read-only session. `always` is refused under `prAuthoring: user`. | A session can hold push access to a repository its client did not name, and under `always` with no client statement at all | Drop `always`, which leaves transparent clients with no push access |
| 17 | Floating refs on default clones | **Settled.** Allowed, with a `Warning`, a posture entry, and a `floatingCloneRefs` forbid | Whoever can push to the ref runs code in every new session until someone forbids it | Require a full commit, which removes that risk and makes each update a tenant admin's write |
| 18 | Default clones on a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` | **Settled.** Allowed, under the check on the session user's link, refusing a session whose user cannot read | A runtime can be unusable for some users, and a tenant admin chooses which repository a user's access is spent on | Refuse such clones at write, which keeps every read under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` the user's own statement and loses private default clones there. A skip for users without access changes what the runtime contains per user, so it is not recommended |
| 19 | A default clone's location | **Settled.** Every repository, a client's included, in a named subfolder of `current/`, with `workingDirectory` choosing where the session starts (DR-12) | A tenant cannot place a default repository at the workspace root | Allow `path: .`, which needs the [egress design](egress-and-git-design.md)'s statement on a non-empty target and makes a client's clone at `.` collide |
| 20 | What a reservation holds | **Settled.** Pods that exist, a split quota, and an optional dedicated node pool, with no preemption or raised priority, in the default agent namespace | Without dedicated nodes a replacement pod can wait. The reserved share stays idle when unused, and a dedicated pool costs idle nodes | Preemption, which guarantees a place and can end other tenants' running sessions. Or low-priority placeholder pods that reserved pods preempt, which holds nodes without a dedicated pool and needs every agent pod to carry `preemptionPolicy: Never`, plus a controller or fixed count sizing the placeholders |
| 21 | Who sets the floor | **Settled.** The operator sets a total, a platform admin sets each tenant's allowance per base and class, and a tenant admin divides it among its runtimes | Three settings to keep in step | A platform admin sets each pool's floor directly, which is simpler and puts an operator's action on every change |
| 22 | A default clone at a derive | **Settled.** A derive has two modes, `from: latest` and `from: start`, and composes nothing, under [`session-derive-design.md`](session-derive-design.md). | An earlier agent's changes under a default clone stay under `from: latest`, and a warm clone's commit since removed from the host fails it | Replace the path at the source's commit, which the owner did not want |
| 23 | What a warm clone holds | **Settled.** Objects alone, with the checkout at materialization, as 0111 states | Checkout time stays per session for a large tree | A pre-checked-out tree on each pod, which 0111 lists as a non-goal and which would need a move into each slot and more disk per pod |
| 24 | Sizing a warm store | **Settled.** `maxWarmCloneBytes` in the grant, per pod, added to one container's storage request, with the sum across grants bounded by `warmClones.totalBytes` | The figure is set up front, every pod on the pool requests it whether or not the repository is that large, and the sum is a worst case that overstates use | Unsized, as 0111 has it, which lets a tenant's repository fill a node |
| 25 | Refreshing a warm store | **Settled.** Idle pods drain past an age limit that each owned pool sets within Helm bounds, 24 hours by default and at most, which an operator can raise, whether or not refills succeed | Churn on idle pods, including a reservation's, refill traffic each day, and a host outage longer than the limit empties the warm pods | No age limit, which leaves an idle pod holding a removed or revoked repository indefinitely, or an in-place fetch, which ends 0111's immutability |
| 26 | A warm fill that keeps failing | **Settled.** The pod stays out of ready, and the pool backs off | A bad clone leaves the runtime with no warm pods until the tenant fixes it | Report ready with no store and fetch cold, which keeps the runtime usable and hides the fault behind slow sessions, as 0111 records |
| 27 | Warm clones on reused pods | **Settled.** Allowed, with the risk accepted. `write` and clones under `userAuthorization: required` stay on pods that serve one session. | A repository removed from the runtime stays readable on running pods until they retire | Refuse such pods on the claim paths, which can hit the budget until old pods empty |
| 28 | What a tenant sets on concurrency and recycling | **Settled.** One field, `oneSessionPerPod`, default `false`, which keeps the template's policy. `true` gives one session with no recycling. | A tenant cannot choose a middle value or tune recycling, such as shortening `maxPodUptimeSeconds` on a template that recycles, and asks for another template or sets the field. The field is one-way, so going back takes a new runtime, and it replaces the former `reuseLocked`, a separate field of earlier revisions that kept an owned pool from taking a template's wider reuse policy. | A value lowered field by field, which the owner saw no case for, or no tenant setting, which brings an operator's step back for an environment on a concurrent base |
| 29 | A setup command's folder on a derive | **Settled.** The gateway writes each command's folder on the stored command as an explicit `cwd` when the plan is composed, under DR-12's rule, and a derive copies it, so the relabel under [`session-derive-design.md`, "Checks"](session-derive-design.md#checks) does not move a command. `origin` decides only which policy checks a command. An `additionalSetupCommands` entry with no `cwd` takes the plan's working directory | Every stored command carries a `cwd`, and a target's own `workingDirectory` moves no copied command | Derive the folder from `origin` at each run, under which a relabel to `client` moves an operator's command to the plan's working directory, and "its own runtime" has no single reading on a derive |
| 30 | Which response field lists a read-only default clone on a derive | **Settled.** The derive response carries `readOnlyDefaultClones` for an entry a default clone added, with its `path` and a reason and without the repository's name, and `readOnlyRepositories` for every other entry, under [`session-derive-design.md`, "Writes"](session-derive-design.md#writes) | A client reads two fields to learn which writes a derive lost | List every entry in `readOnlyRepositories`, which names a repository the client never named |
| 31 | Warm stores across declarers | **Settled.** One warm list per pool with one rule set, option (b) of the assessment. Every pre-filled repository store on a pod comes from it, each entry records its URL, its ref as a branch, a tag, or a full commit, its declarer, and whether it adds a checkout, and DR-11's rules govern every entry: the sized volume and matching storage request, the age limit, the drain when an entry leaves the list, claim refusal through `coveredStores`, backoff keyed by store, the per-tenant fill limit, a full commit, and the adapter's tree bound. `PodSet.spec.warmClones` carries the list for every pool. Decisions 23 to 27 apply to operator stores, and the age limit stays unset by default on platform pools | An operator chooses a byte figure for each pool that renders entries, and operator stores gain churn wherever an age limit is set. The rewrite of 0111 takes on DR-11's store rules | Keep the operator's entry and the warm clone with separate rules, which leaves operator stores unsized, refills them without limit on failure, fills a shared URL twice on an owned pod, and charges an operator's failure to the tenant. Or express the operator entry as a base's default clone, which forces a checkout on every session of every tenant on the base |
| 32 | Who declares a warm-list entry | **Settled.** Changed by decision 42 on 2026-11-14: a store comes only from a `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets`, so the warm default clone, the cache-only entry, and the private entry on an owned pool's row below are gone. As settled: A public `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime's `sharedAssets` reaches the warm list of every pool of that runtime, owned pools included, and operator entries on a Runtime are public only. A private operator entry is written only by a `platform-admin` on one owned pool's row. A tenant admin declares a warm default clone, which adds a checkout, and a cache-only entry in `pool.warmCaches`, which adds none, under the warm clone's grant bounds and write checks. Entries naming one normalized URL share one store, filled once, and a tenant entry's ref wins | 0111's private entries on a Runtime, its Runtime-wide ownership rule 2, and this design's grant condition on private base entries go. Every session must pass a cache-only entry's read checks or have its store withheld | Private entries on a Runtime under 0111's ownership rules, which DR-1 makes partly moot. Or no tenant cache-only entry, which leaves a client cloning its own branch of a tenant's large repository with a full fetch |
| 33 | Which sources a store serves | **Settled.** Decision 42 keeps this rule and adds that the first such source of the composed workspace adopts a staged tree. As settled: Any `gitClone` source in the plan, whoever wrote it, whose normalized URL matches a store on the pod, unless it sets `depth` or `submodules: true`. The restriction to sources marked `runtimeDefault` goes | None found. Its restore reason predates derive's copy of the stored plan, and serving a client's source reads nothing a session could not already read | Serve only default clones, which leaves cache-only entries with nothing to serve |
| 34 | A failed read check on an entry the plan does not name | **Settled.** Decision 42 keeps this rule, with a tenant `gitRepository` asset whose repository the plan does not name in place of the cache-only entry and the warm default clone the plan omits. As settled: At create the gateway runs the read checks on every tenant entry of the pool's warm list, and `coveredStores` counts every tenant entry whose checks pass plus operator public entries. A warm default clone whose check fails refuses the session at create. A cache-only entry whose check fails is withheld: the adapter deletes its store before materialization, restore completion, setup commands, and the runtime start, the list reaches every assignment path, the session proceeds and reports `withheldWarmEntries` without the URL, and the pod drains when the session ends. On resume and derive a warm default clone the stored plan omits takes the same rule. SDK-warm pools refuse. On a pod with concurrent sessions a failure refuses, and the design keeps that case unreachable. The owner accepted the residual of stale blocks on the node | A pod drains after each session that withheld a store. A deleted store's blocks stay on the node's disk until overwritten | Refuse the session, which makes a runtime unusable for every user without access to a repository the session never asked for, and for every user during a provider outage |
| 35 | A failing operator entry on an owned pool | **Settled.** One backoff keyed by store directory for every declarer. Each rendered entry carries its declarer, and the `AgentPod` records the first store that failed and its declarer. `unusable` in T excludes a pod whose recorded failure is on a platform-declared entry. The tenant admin sees `blockedBy`, and the operator gets an alert. A platform entry whose host the tenant denies is left off that owned pool's rendered list | An operator's broken entry leaves an owned pool with no warm pods until it is fixed or the backoff passes, and the tenant can only report it | Charge such pods to the tenant, who cannot fix the cause, or keep 0111's unlimited replacement for operator entries |
| 36 | Warm-list entries under an environment-scoped VCS pool | **Settled.** Decision 42 keeps this rule for every `gitRepository` entry. As settled: An entry of either declarer, checkout or cache-only, is refused at write unless its repository lies under no VCS pool prefix, or credential selection at E = ∅ resolves it to an unscoped VCS pool whose allowlist names it. A later VCS pool change that breaks this passes with a `Warning`. The PoolScalingController applies the same predicate at render, the entry leaves the rendered list as suspended with `environmentScoped`, the drain and `coveredStores` reach idle pods and claims, and the entry returns when the pool is unscoped | A tenant cannot warm a repository that only a scoped pool covers. A Runtime's public entry is checked per owned pool at render, so its write passes with a `Warning` | Refuse the VCS pool write while an entry depends on the pool, as an environment deletion does, which blocks a security narrowing |
| 37 | What the rewrite of draft 0111 must do | **Settled.** The list under "Relationship to draft proposal 0111": define the warm list and its rule set, drop private entries on a Runtime and rule 2's ownership key, drop `destPath`, confirm and drop the restore lineage if derive's plan copy leaves it no case, state restore completion's answer to a `lenny.gitStoreUrl` outside the passed set, and state that a repository wired to a store without `lenny.*` keys gets no store service | The rewrite carries the store rules this design stated for warm clones | Keep the comparison table and let the rewrite choose, which leaves two rule sets |
| 38 | Pods a platform-declared entry failed, against the creation bound and the fill limit | **Settled** as recommended, in the owner's words "yes (go with your recommendation)": they count toward the tenant's creation bound under `maxOwnedPods` and toward `maxConcurrentFillsPerTenant`, because those limits protect the cluster from runaway creation whoever's entry is broken, and on an owned pool the fill runs on the tenant's egress identity | An operator's broken entry can hold the tenant at its creation bound, within the backoff | Leave such pods out of both limits, which lets an operator's fault create pods past the tenant's bound |
| 39 | Choices made while applying decisions 31 to 37 | **Settled**, in the owner's word "ok". Decision 42 retires the names `pool.warmCaches`, `warmRepositories`, and the `blockedBy` value `warmCache`, and the ordering of a runtime's tenant entries. As settled: A platform admin's private entry on an owned pool's row is refused at write under the environment rule, and a public `gitRepository` entry on a Runtime passes with a `Warning` and is suspended at render on each owned pool where it fails the rule. Warm-list hosts join the pre-session host formulas within the ceiling. Among tenant entries for one URL, the first in order sets the ref, with default clones first, and a merged store's declarer is `tenant`. Cache-only entries count toward `maxDefaultGitClones`. The render leaves a platform entry under a VCS pool with `userAuthorization: required` off an owned pool that reuses pods. A Runtime entry carrying `auth` is refused, and a private platform entry on an owned pool is read-checked like a tenant entry. The names are `pool.warmCaches`, `warmRepositories`, `fillFailure`, `withheldStores`, `withheldWarmEntries`, the `blockedBy` values `warmCache` and `platformEntry`, and the suspended reasons `environmentScoped` and `tenantDenied`. | A tenant's VCS pool settings can suspend an operator's public entry on that tenant's owned pool | Refuse a platform write that any tenant's scoping would break, which lets one tenant block a platform-wide write |
| 40 | A platform write that raises the setup bound past a pool's window | **Settled** on 2026-11-09 as the owner's own rule, review question Q3. A base write that raises the setup bound is refused when any pool, platform or tenant-owned, no longer fits its window. The refusal names the pools and the smallest `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` that fits. DP9 gains this exception, the egress design's clamp on a template write becomes the same refusal, and a tenant's own raise stays refused | A tenant-owned pool can block a platform write to its base, and the refusal names that pool to the `platform-admin` | Let the write succeed and clamp each owned pool's window, which keeps DP9 without exceptions and leaves an owned pool with a window shorter than its template's |
| 41 | The code for a failed read check on a concurrent pod with slot attribution | **Settled** on 2026-11-13 as recommended, in the owner's word "ok" to item 13 of the consolidated list. Found in the cross-design check of 2026-11-13. Under "Withheld stores", a session on a concurrent pod whose environment denies a warm-list entry's host is refused with `ENVIRONMENT_POLICY_NOT_ENFORCEABLE` naming the entry. [`tenant-scoping-identity-design.md`](tenant-scoping-identity-design.md) and the egress design's "Facts the pages state" scoped that code to a pool without slot attribution, and both now list this case under it, because the cause is the same, a pod that cannot apply the session's environment to a resource its other sessions share. | The code's documented scope widens to a case where `netagent` does attribute connections | A code of its own for a shared store that the session's environment denies, which leaves the existing code's scope as it is |
| 42 | Shared assets and warm stores (question 17 of the consolidated list) | **Settled** on 2026-11-14 as the owner's own model. The owner wrote: "sharedAssets should define any git stores to warm up. workspaceDefaults shouldn't also do that through `warm: true`"; "`include: onRequest` doesn't make sense. If the session plan asks for a repo ... and the user/session is allowed to use it, it is prepared in the workspace. git clones should only appear under workspaceDefaults if we want them there by default"; and "in sharedAssets, we should indicate whether we want a repo to be pre-staged". On the staged tree's folder the owner then asked: "does prestage need a path? Can't the path be determined at the time of copying (based on workspaceDefaults and the session plan)?", and settled `prestage: true` with no path. The model: a `gitRepository` entry of `sharedAssets`, on an operator's Runtime or on a tenant-derived runtime, is the only way to declare a warm store, with optional `prestage: true`; `workspaceDefaults.gitClones` lists only repositories every session gets by default, has no `warm` field, and is allowed on operator runtimes; any `gitClone` source of a session's workspace, from the defaults or the plan, uses a store of its URL, and the first such source adopts a staged tree by rename to its own `path`, with a ref difference checked out in place, while the withheld-store rules stay as they are; `pool.warmCaches` and the platform admin's `warmRepositories` leave, and a tenant `gitRepository` asset takes the checks of warm default clones and cache-only entries; the egress design's decision 63 `path` becomes `prestage: true`. A tenant-derived runtime's `inline` and `artifact` assets, under the owner's acceptance of the proposal of 2026-11-13 ("looks generally good"), render on its owned pool alone (`TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL`), append to the base's without replacing a path, take relative paths with no `..` or reserved name, sit within `maxAssetEntryBytes` and `maxAssetsBytes` under the operator's `allowAssets`, pin an uploaded artifact by digest under the tenant's key, are Confidential, are erased with the tenant and kept under legal hold, form a tenant-owned chunk set billed to the tenant, and must hold no secrets. | A tenant warms a repository with an entry that is separate from the default clone that checks it out. A tenant file under `/lenny/assets` is code to the runtime. The owner changed these: a tenant's `PUT` may include a `platform`-declared asset unchanged, and only a change or a removal is refused (item 1); the warm-up checkout timeout is set per runtime, in the owner's words "the tenant can choose repos, so if a repo can add warm-up time, I think the tenant should have a way to change the setting per runtime", as the runtime field `prestageTimeoutSeconds` within the grant entry's `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`, whose default is `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`, 900 seconds, and operator runtimes and pools may set it too (item 4); the upload limit is also a grant field per tenant, `maxAssetUploadBytes`, with the chart value as its default (item 5); and the default clone limit is the grant entry's `maxDefaultGitClones`, the cap the operator sets per tenant, 8 by default, under the deployment ceiling `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling`, 32 by default, in the owner's words "If a tenant wants a derived runtime with 10 repos, they should be able to do that (maybe with a hard cap defined by the operator)" (item 10). | The operator sets more values per grant, a tenant's longer timeout delays its own owned pool's pods from reporting ready, and a tenant with a high cap composes more clones into each session's plan within the same byte bounds | Keep the deployment-wide values for every tenant |
