**Owner decisions of 2026-11-12 that other designs own.** The consistency pass of 2026-11-12 applies these to this design, and the table above keeps each decision's words as written:

- Every git step on a slot repository runs in `lenny-materialize` as the slot UID with a pinned static git, and the adapter runs no git on slot repositories (SI O11, settled; [WM-10](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-10-git)). Archive extraction moves from the gateway into the helper ([WM-9](workspace-materialization-design.md#wm-9-archive-extraction-leaves-the-gateway)). Rules 2, 3, 5, and 6 of draft 0111 and the checkout bound therefore run in the helper, and "Not verified" under DR-9 becomes an integrity rule at the slot UID.
- The warm-up checkout and the reflink rebuild are approved, and the materialization design owns them. "Warm stores" states how they meet the warm list, and a withheld store also loses any tree staged from it.
- The embedded deployment model is removed ([PL-2](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-2-removal-of-the-embedded-deployment-model)), and the runtime-visible layout is fixed ([PL-3](agent-pod-layout-design.md#pl-3-runtime-visible-filesystem-layout)): `/workspace` replaces the session's `current/`, and the stores sit on `lenny-git-stores` at `/lenny/git-stores`, which `lenny-store-fill` fills under the egress design's decision 61 of 2026-11-13 and the adapter mounts at `/adapter/git-stores` only to delete a withheld store. The `incompatible` row for an embedded base and the `deploymentModel` conditions leave, and DR-8's export refusal reduces to a target with no supervisor.
- Pre-launched runtimes are removed, and every runtime launches at bind. The owned pool's `sdkWarm` setting, the SDK-warm refusal of decision 34, the fill's `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds`, and the `preConnect` and blocking-path parts of the per-tenant override leave.
- `workspaceTier` leaves for the tenant security settings ([TSS-9](tenant-security-settings-design.md#tss-9-removal-of-workspacetier)), and integration levels leave for capabilities ([RP D3](runtime-protocol-design.md#decisions)), so neither appears in "Fields".
- `reservedPlacement` becomes the placement design's `placement.reservedOverlay` ([PM-3](placement-design.md#reserved-overlay)), `lenny-agents-kata` is dropped ([PM-9](placement-design.md#pm-9-namespaces)), the `lenny-reserved` class joins the priority-class scheme of [PM-8](placement-design.md#priority-classes), and an owned pool's template copy gains `placement.offering` and `placement.region`. Decision 20's dedicated node pool is now the reserved overlay on nodes within the pool's class, and its "default agent namespace" holds every profile.
- `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` is renamed `maxConcurrentSessionsPerPod`, `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` is renamed `acknowledgeSharedPod`, and `recycle.allowCrossTenantReuse` is removed. Decision 3's field is the renamed one.

**Owner decisions of 2026-11-13 that other designs own.** The cross-design review of 2026-11-13 applies these to this design, and the table above keeps each decision's words as written:

- **`type: mcp` runtimes and service mode are removed** ([`agent-runtimes-only-design.md`](agent-runtimes-only-design.md)). The owner settled its open decisions on 2026-11-13 in the words "O5 (b), yes to all the others". Under O1 and O2 the `type` and `executionMode` fields leave, so the grant conditions on `executionMode: session` and `type: agent`, the `executionMode` rows of "Fields" and "Template copy", the "Base changes" sentences on both fields, the scope bullet on service-mode runtimes, and the `executionMode` term leave, and decision 10 is moot. Under O3 a write naming a removed field answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming it. Under O8 (b) the export refusal of DR-8 on an unsupervised target and SD-P4's `target_unsupervised` leave, and DR-4 owns the check at the post-chain point that the final runtime row of every create, derive, and delegated child is `registered` ("Registered rows"). DF-16 stays closed by DR-3 and the materialization design.
- **Platform chunk sets** (decision D1 of [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#decisions), settled on 2026-11-13 as the hybrid, "1. ok 2. ok"). The gateway stores `coveredStores` and `withheldStores` on the session row at each claim, and computes a session's eligible platform sources from them at every checkpoint, export, and resume. A withheld store's set is never eligible ("Stored with the session").
- **`lenny-store-fill`** (decision 61 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), settled on 2026-11-13: "if the separate container works from a permissions standpoint, then yes"). An init container under its own UID, with no credentials and only the store volume read-write, fills the stores and writes the pristine trees, and the adapter keeps its read-write mount only to delete withheld stores ("Fill").
- **Per-slot scopes** (decision 60 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), settled on 2026-11-13 as option (a)). A pod with several slots evaluates each slot's session with its own environment where `netagent` attributes connections to slots, and keeps the union scope and E = ∅ where it cannot. "Pods with no per-session policy", "One session per pod", "Environments" under DR-9 and DR-8, the concurrent case of "Withheld stores", and the first scenario state it. The concurrent refusal of decision 34, which that decision called unreachable, becomes reachable through an environment's deny where attribution holds, and it refuses the session.
- **Warm-up checkout on platform pools: (a).** The operator's `gitRepository` entries gain an optional target `path` and are staged like default clones ("Warm list" and "Warm-up checkout and reflink rebuild"). Decision 42 replaces the `path` with `prestage: true`.
- **LLM upstreams and the removal of direct delivery mode** ([`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md#decisions), D1, in the owner's words "Thin LLM proxy: sounds good"). `llmFormats` replaces `supportedProviders` and `credentialCapabilities` in "Fields" and "Base changes", `deliveryMode` and `allowDirectModeStandardIsolation` leave the template copy, the owned pool gains `llmUpstreams`, and `validatePoolProxyDialect` leaves the tenant-view handlers. Settled decision 14 changes in one respect: the residual it states is the session's proxy token on a pool with `egressProfile: internet`, bounded by the session's credit budgets, because no LLM credential lease exists.
- **Documentation completeness** (decision D13 of [`incremental-checkpoint-design.md`](incremental-checkpoint-design.md#decisions), which the owner extended to every design on 2026-11-13: "we must document all the relevant details of this design (and all others)"). "Documentation" lists every setting, default, node requirement, limit, failure mode, and runtime-facing rule.
- **Answers to the consolidated list of open items, 2026-11-13.** The owner's answers settle decision 41 as recommended (item 13, "ok") and these rules from other designs, which this design applies:
  - `lenny-store-fill` writes the stores and pristine trees with a dedicated store group in place of the adapter's former GID, that group is the primary group of both the fill and the adapter, no slot container holds it, every agent-pod container runs with `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, and the mode pass clears setuid and setgid bits (decision 61 of [`egress-and-git-design.md`](egress-and-git-design.md#decisions), in the owner's words "ok but add an owed item to stress test this thoroughly (with actual tests)"). "Fill" and the Tier 9 test state it, with the hostile-repository stress suite on runc, gVisor, and Kata.
  - Each resource class carries its own `requests.ephemeral-storage`, 1 GiB in the shipped classes and set per class, which the pod's request adds to the sum of its disk-backed volumes' `sizeLimit` values (BR-Q1 settled as option (a), recorded as [`billing-resource-attribution-design.md` decision 18](billing-resource-attribution-design.md#decisions)). "Size", "Share of the quota", and "Node and cluster requirements" state it.
  - A write that names a removed field is refused with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, the rule of AR O3, which the owner extended on 2026-11-13 to every removed field, so a derived-runtime or owned-pool write naming `supportedProviders`, `credentialCapabilities`, or another field that LU-4 of [`llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md`](llm-upstreams-and-cost-budgets-design.md#lu-4-removal) or AR removes is refused by name, and "Documentation" lists the code.

## Review history

This section records the reviews of earlier revisions and what each revision changed. The current text above supersedes any statement here that differs from it.

### Revision notes

These notes were carried in the status line of earlier revisions.

- Revisions 2 to 6 apply independent reviews, each under its own "Review of revision" section. A stop check on revision 5 found no Critical, High, or Major issue in this document, and no review has read revisions 6, 7, or 30.
- Revision 7 records the owner's choice of this design.
- Revision 8 adds default git clones (DR-9) and capacity reservation (DR-10), records the owner's yes on default setup commands, and applies the requests under "Changes requested of other design documents".
- Revisions 9 to 13 apply the review of revision 8 and the stop checks on revisions 9 to 12, under "Review of DR-9 and DR-10".
- Revision 14 adds warm clones and the write scope of default clones to DR-9, records how this design relates to draft proposal 0111, and states the recommended rule for derive and replay, which the owner has not settled.
- Revision 15 applies the review of revision 14, under "Review of DR-9 and DR-10".
- Revision 16 applies the stop check on revision 15 and simplifies warm clones.
- Revision 17 applies the stop check on revision 16 and removes the warm list generation.
- Revision 18 applies the stop check on revision 17.
- Revision 19 applies the owner's decisions on warm clones on reused pods and on derive, and moves replay to `session-replay-design.md`.
- Revision 20 applies the review of revision 19 and of `session-replay-design.md` revision 1.
- Revisions 21 to 26 apply the stop checks on revisions 20 to 25. A stop check on revision 26 found no Critical, High, or Major issue in DR-9, and revision 27 applies its Minors.
- Revision 28 follows the owner's merge of derive and replay into one route, in `session-derive-design.md`, which replaces `session-replay-design.md`, and shortens DR-9's "Derive".
- Revision 31 records the owner's settling of decisions 1 to 8 and adds decision 28, which replaces the tenant's concurrency and recycling fields with `oneSessionPerPod`. It also names DF-17.
- Revision 32 applies the review of revision 31, which makes `oneSessionPerPod` one-way and folds `reuseLocked` into it.
- Revision 36 replaces the `recycle` and `concurrency` forbid values with `reusedPods`, which requires `oneSessionPerPod: true`. That replaces the rules of revisions 32 to 35. A check on revision 36 found no route from a `true` row to a reusing policy and one Major, a resume on a suspended runtime, which the document now states as a residual.
- Revision 38 records the owner's settling of decisions 9 to 12.
- Revision 39 corrects the reason for `ownedOverlays`, because the egress design's allowlist already excludes the variables it named.
- Revision 40 records the owner's settling of decision 13.
- Revision 41 records the owner's settling of decisions 14 and 15 with their additions.
- Revision 42 records the owner's settling of decisions 18, 21, and 23.
- Revision 43 records the warm clone spike under "Not verified".
- Revision 44 adds DR-12, which puts every repository in a subfolder of `current/` and adds `workingDirectory`, after the owner's settling of decision 19.
- Revision 45 states that an empty or `.` `workingDirectory` names `current/`.
- Revision 46 records the owner's settling of decisions 24 to 26 and makes the warm store age limit a pool setting.
- Revision 47 applies the review of revisions 39 to 46 under "Review of revisions 39 to 46".
- Revision 48 lets a platform admin set the warm store age limit on any pool.
- Revision 49 files DF-19 on the seed's tenant list.
- Revision 50 makes the emergency retirement's ticket optional, after `security-oversight-design.md` decision 24.
- Revision 51 adds the seed revocation steps to the operator guide.
- Revision 52 records that billing attributes no idle owned pod to a tenant.
- Revision 53 points the idle billing bullet under "Not verified" to `billing-resource-attribution-design.md`, which adds `pool.idle_usage`.
- Revision 54 changes only pointers after the user credential documents split.
- Revision 55 followed a pass for a reader new to the project. It added "Terms", "Terms from other designs and the platform", "Labels from other designs", and "High-level design", moved DR-12 after DR-9, moved the revision notes and review records into "Review history", linked each citation of another design to the file and the heading it names, named the setting behind each configuration value, and distinguished this document's decision numbers from those of other designs. After a reader review, it glossed the platform and draft 0111 terms the text relied on, and qualified "route", "grant", "pin", "store", "T", and "reservation" where a use carried another sense. It also corrected two statements that later revisions had made stale: the status line's statement that no spike was run, which revision 43's warm clone spike contradicts, and the opening of "Decisions", which called the decisions open although every row is settled. After a validation pass, it reworded the status line and the **Unproven** residual risk to record the warm clone spike, filled the DF-16 row's evidence and closing proposal and put the defects table in order, merged the two §17.2 bullets, pointed DR-12 at DR-9's "Not verified", and pointed DR-10 **Reporting** at `pool.idle_usage` and `reserved_pod_minutes`, and changed no rule.

- Revision 58, dated 2026-11-08, applies decisions 31 to 37, which the owner settled that day, records decision 38 as open with its recommendation, and changes these rules. One backoff covers every declarer, the Sandbox records the failing store and its declarer, and T excludes pods a platform-declared entry failed. - Revision 73, dated 2026-11-14, applies the independent review of revision 72's write-up of question 17, and changes these rules. Each item marked "for owner review" is a new choice made in resolving a finding rather than a statement of a settled decision, and each item marked "confirmed" or "answered" is one the owner settled on 2026-11-14 as decision 43, which revision 74 applies, or as decision 44, which revision 75 records:
  - A `gitRepository` asset records its declarer from the role of its writer, so an asset a `platform-admin` writes on a tenant-derived runtime is `platform`-declared, and decision 35's protections apply to it as they applied to the retired `warmRepositories`: it counts in no term of the tenant's charge T, its failure alerts the operator and reads `blockedBy: platformEntry`, a host the tenant denies suspends it with `tenantDenied`, it does not count toward `maxDefaultGitClones`, and the tenant's `derivedRuntimes.forbid: gitClones` does not refuse it. It needs neither `allowGitClones` nor DR-3, which bound the tenant's own input, and its store's staged trees still feed the tenant-owned chunk set (below). A tenant's `PUT` keeps it as stored, and a body that names it answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`; `GET` lists it with `declarer: platform`. Revision 72 recorded such an asset as `tenant`-declared, which dropped those protections. "Terms", "Fields", "Bounds", "Tenant shared assets" with its new **Platform-declared assets.**, "Warm list", "Reach before binding", "Failure", "Who reaches a store", DR-7's posture and records, "Configuration by scope", the documentation, and the Tier 1 tests follow. Confirmed, with the change that a tenant's `PUT` may carry such an asset unchanged.
  - A `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` asset needs the grant's `allowGitClones` and DR-3 landed, as the cache-only entry it replaces did, in addition to a `maxWarmCloneBytes` above zero, because its staged tree lands in each idle slot and a session adopts it. Revision 72 required `maxWarmCloneBytes` alone. "Tenant defaults", R9, DP6, "Fields", DR-6, and the tests follow. Confirmed.
  - A store that a base's public entry and a `gitRepository` asset of the runtime share feeds the tenant-owned chunk set of its URL with every staged tree, whichever entry carries `prestage: true` ("Warm list", in line with IC-4 of `incremental-checkpoint-design.md`). Among entries of one URL, the runtime's own entry's ref wins over the base's.
  - A `platform`-declared asset's store feeds the tenant-owned set too, because it serves the owner alone, fills on the tenant's identity, and holds a repository private to the tenant, which the tenant key covers; only a store that a base's public entry alone declares feeds a platform-owned set. Decision 35's protections, which concern the tenant's pod budget and the operator's alerting, are unchanged by it. Confirmed.
  - A warm-up checkout that fails its tree-size check or reaches `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds` skips that entry in that slot, the slot still reports ready, a session that clones the repository builds it from the store, and the skip is counted in `lenny_prestage_skipped_total` and reported as a `PrestageSkipped` event. A runtime write answers with a `Warning` when the measured staged trees of its entries exceed half of `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` ("Checkout" under "Warm stores", WM-15). Confirmed, with the timeout made settable per runtime.
  - The warm-up checkout test states adoption by the first source of the repository, the adopted tree's `origin`, and that an unadopted tree never appears in a checkpoint, download, or export and is visible only under `.lenny-discard-<id>` until its `discard` ends.
  - An uploaded artifact's digest is scoped to the derived runtime the upload route names, an upload that no version of that runtime in use references is deleted after a grace period of 7 days by reference-based garbage collection, uploads are deleted with the derived runtime and with the tenant, and the Helm value `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetUploadBytes`, 1 GiB by default, caps a tenant's stored upload bytes, with `429 STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` past it. `admin.derived_runtime.artifact_deleted` records each deletion. Was for owner review; confirmed (decision 44). The owner answered the upload limit by making it a grant field per tenant with the chart value as its default (decision 43) and no deployment ceiling (decision 44).
  - An upload is stored once: it is chunked into the packs of the runtime's tenant-owned assets set at upload, and those packs are its only copy, so the separate `runtime_asset` ledger row of revision 72 leaves and `billing-resource-attribution-design.md` bills the packs once. `inline` content lives in the derived runtime's row and is not billed apart from it. Was for owner review; confirmed (decision 44).
  - An `inline` entry is capped by `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxInlineAssetBytes`, 1 MiB by default and at most 4 MiB, and a runtime's `inline` entries together by 4 MiB; larger content goes through an `artifact` entry. Was for owner review; confirmed (decision 44).
  - A tenant's `artifact` entry carries `digest` and `destPath` alone, `ref` answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `details.field`, and the file is placed whole and never extracted. The example names a file. Was for owner review; confirmed (decision 44).
  - The grant entry gains `allowAssets`, off by default, beside the deployment switch `runtimes.tenantDerivation.allowAssets`, and a tenant's `inline` and `artifact` assets need both and DR-3 landed. DP6, R9, "Tenant defaults", the upload route, DR-6, the stolen `tenant-admin` scenario, the residual risk, and "Configuration by scope" follow. Confirmed.
  - An operator runtime's default clone at a floating ref draws a write `Warning`, and each tenant's posture lists such clones; a pool of the runtime with no git path draws a write `Warning`; DR-6 gains a row for a base default clone added at, above, or below a tenant default file's `path`; and a platform-derived runtime's `gitClones` append to its base's, with a conflicting `path` refused with `422 INVALID_RUNTIME`. Was for owner review; confirmed (decision 44).
  - A write naming `workspaceDefaults.gitClones[].warm`, `pool.warmCaches`, `warmRepositories`, or a `gitRepository` entry's `path` answers `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, which "Warm list" states and the documentation's failure modes list, under D27 of `runtime-revisions-design.md`.
  - `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClones` defaults to 8 and bounds a tenant's default clones and `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets; an operator runtime's default clones and `platform`-declared assets do not count toward it. Answered: the grant entry carries the cap per tenant, with this value as its default, under a deployment ceiling.
  - Wording, which changes no rule: the 0111 rewrite list names "Warm stores"; "Compared with a session plan" gains a shared-assets row; "Fields" names `/lenny/git-stores` where it said "filled there"; DR-6's base-asset row reads "at, above, or below" and any revision in the base's traffic, as the asset checks at write do; `TENANT_ASSETS_REQUIRE_OWNED_POOL` is marked as an internal guard in "API", the tests, and the documentation's failure modes; and the dated requests to `workspace-materialization-design.md` under "Changes requested of other design documents" carry a note that decision 42 superseded them.
- Revision 74, dated 2026-11-14, applies the owner's answers of that day to the confirmation list of revision 73's choices, recorded as decision 43, and changes these rules:
  - A tenant's `PUT` may carry a `platform`-declared `gitRepository` asset unchanged, as `GET` returns it, and is accepted; a body that changes one answers `422 INVALID_DERIVED_RUNTIME` with `details.field`, and a body that leaves one out keeps it, in place of revision 73's refusal of any body that named it. The owner's answer: a tenant `PUT` may include platform-declared entries unchanged, and a change or a removal is refused. **Platform-declared assets.**, "Fields", and the Tier 1 test follow.
  - A tenant-derived runtime may set `prestageTimeoutSeconds`, the timeout of a warm-up checkout run on its owned pool, positive and at most the grant entry's new `maxPrestageTimeoutSeconds`, which defaults to `adapter.prestageTimeoutSeconds`, 900 seconds. The owner's words: "the tenant can choose repos, so if a repo can add warm-up time, I think the tenant should have a way to change the setting per runtime". The field is versioned and pod-affecting, and a pod's timeout is the runtime revision's value, else the pool's, else the chart's, which `workspace-materialization-design.md` owns. A lowered bound renders the smaller value on new pods. "Who turns it on", "Fields", "Template copy", "Checkout", DR-6, "Configuration by scope", the documentation, and the Tier 1 tests follow.
  - The grant gains `maxAssetUploadBytes`, the tenant's upload limit, which defaults to `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxAssetUploadBytes`, 1 GiB, under the owner's answer that the upload quota is also a grant field per tenant. "Terms", "Who turns it on", the upload route, "Uploads", DR-6, "Configuration by scope", the documentation, and the Tier 1 tests follow.
  - Each grant entry gains `maxDefaultGitClones`, the cap the operator sets per tenant on a runtime's default clones and `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` assets, which defaults to `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClones`, 8, and which the new chart value `runtimes.tenantDerivation.maxDefaultGitClonesCeiling`, 32 by default, bounds. The owner's words: "If a tenant wants a derived runtime with 10 repos, they should be able to do that (maybe with a hard cap defined by the operator)". "Checks at write" states that `maxWarmCloneBytes` bounds the stores' cost whatever their number. "Terms", "Who turns it on", DR-6, "Configuration by scope", the documentation, and the Tier 1 tests follow.
  - The owner confirmed as written that a `platform`-declared asset's store feeds the tenant-owned chunk set, that a `tenant`-declared `gitRepository` asset needs `allowGitClones` and DR-3, the skip of a warm-up checkout with its metric, event, and write `Warning`, and the grant flag `allowAssets`. Revision 73's notes mark each as confirmed.
  - "Changes requested of other design documents" gains the requests of decision 43 to the materialization, runtime revisions, and pod layout designs, which the same pass applied. The status line's reads-against list names the current revision of each cited design.
- Revision 75, dated 2026-11-14, records the owner's later answers of that day to the remaining items of the confirmation list, in the words "Ok on the rest", as decision 44, and changes no rule. The owner confirmed the items of revision 73 that were for owner review: the upload lifecycle with its 7-day garbage collection, its deletion with the runtime and the tenant, and the per-tenant upload limit; the single stored copy of an upload; the `inline` caps; the `artifact` entry's `digest` and `destPath` with no extraction; and the rules for an operator runtime's default clones, the floating-ref `Warning` among them. The owner confirmed revision 71's store group as the primary group of the adapter, revision 74's DR-6 rows for a lowered cap, and that a grant's `maxAssetUploadBytes` has no deployment ceiling, which "Who turns it on" and "Configuration by scope" now state. Revision 73's notes and the store-group note of revision 71 mark each as confirmed. The status line's reads-against list names the current revision of each cited design.
- Revision 76, dated 2026-11-14, applies the instruction of [`rest-only-client-api-design.md`](rest-only-client-api-design.md) revision 3 that reaches this design. That design records the owner's decisions of 2026-11-13 on items 23 to 26 of the consolidated list of open decisions, under which every client protocol adapter leaves Lenny ("ok, go with (b)"), and the owner's answers of 2026-11-14 to its O1 to O13. The revision changes this rule. The `agentInterface` row of "Fields" no longer has the gateway generate and cache an agent card from the merged value: discovery publishes the merged value under runtime revisions decision D12 as revision 19 of that design restates it, and the A2A adapter builds the card. The override and its prompt-injection note stay. The row and decision 11 state that a tenant-derived runtime stays out of R8's public runtime index, because decision 11 refuses it a `public` entry. The instruction says nothing about visibility; this is the reading of decision 11 under R8. "Labels from other designs" gains RO-9 and R8, and the status line reads against `rest-only-client-api-design.md` revision 3.
- Revision 77, dated 2026-11-15, follows [`connector-removal-design.md`](connector-removal-design.md) revision 3, the final consistency pass of the connector removal under the owner's decision "Yes, let's drop connectors.", and changes this rule. To "Remove `capabilityInferenceMode`, `toolCapabilityOverrides`, and `tenantRbacConfig.mcpAnnotationMappings`?", the owner answered "yes" on 2026-11-15 (D10 of that design). The row of "Fields" for `capabilityInferenceMode` and `toolCapabilityOverrides` becomes a refusal with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` naming the field, under D27 of `runtime-revisions-design.md`, the introduction to "Fields" drops their description, and DF-12's remaining merge-table rows drop `toolCapabilityOverrides`. Wording, which changes no rule: the introduction to "Fields" describes `agentInterface` as the source of the A2A adapter's agent cards, as revision 76 states, in place of a generated card.
- Revision 78, dated 2026-11-16, applies the kind and field names of [`pod-lifecycle-controller-design.md`](pod-lifecycle-controller-design.md) revision 3, which the owner accepted on 2026-11-16 as its O3: `PodSet`, `AgentPod`, and the pool table `pools` replace the retired names, the `SandboxTemplate.spec` and `SandboxWarmPool.spec` rows of the stored formats merge into one `PodSet.spec` row, the AgentPod controller and `agentPodLabels` replace the Sandbox controller and `sandboxLabels`, and the controller package is `pkg/controller/agentpod`. No rule changes. The status line's reads-against list names the revision this pass gives each design it edits.

### Review of revision 1

An independent review read revision 1. Each finding was checked against the text and the tree. No finding was rejected outright, and the "Result" column states where the fix differs from the one proposed.

| Finding | Result | Check and outcome |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| H1, experiments drain shared pools | Holds. Fix changed. | `ListPoolConfigs`, `ResolveVariantRoles`, and the routes read as stated. "Delete" means the `SandboxWarmPool`, because the template and the row remain. An owned-only rule would end experiments on every deployment with no grant, against R8, because `tenant-admin` cannot create a pool. The rule is a pool's single tenant, with `tenant-admin` writes limited to owned pools, and it moves to DR-1 because the defect exists today. |
| H2, a missed site does not fail closed | Holds. | Applied, and the lint is replaced by the type change. `messages.go` was not traced for its not-found branch, and the type change covers it either way. |
| H3, default files are code | Holds. Fix widened. | `allowWorkspaceFiles` and refusal of a base path applied. The proposed refusal on a supervisor-less base with setup commands becomes refusal on any supervisor-less base, because an embedded runtime reading a tenant file shares the adapter's identity. This is one rule in place of a conditional. |
| M1, an overlay blocks a template write | Holds. | Applied for owned pools. An overlay on the template itself keeps EG-9's refusal, which is recorded as a residual. |
| M2, "no new surface" is false | Holds. | `ownedOverlays` applied, and the deny rule requested. |
| M3, custom roles keep DF-1 open | Holds. Fix narrowed. | Both permissions leave `RolePermissions(RoleTenantAdmin)`, which is the ceiling. The second list is `AllPermissions()`, which `platform-admin` needs, so it stays. |
| M4, R5 is not met | Holds. Fix smaller. | `maxOwnedPods` replaces `maxWarmPods` and the `max_concurrent_sessions` prerequisite. With the sum of grants at or below the total, only grant writes take the global lock, and pool writes need none. R5 is reworded. |
| M5, grant changes have no effect | Holds. | Applied through the rows in "Base changes". |
| M6, an allowlist under a shell does not bound | Holds. | Applied. |
| Minor 1, `podRuntimeRef` scope | Holds. | DF-3's closure narrowed, and the grant write checks the `Runtime` resource. |
| Minor 2, `resolveSDKWarm` | Holds. | Applied. |
| Minor 3, admin readers | Holds. | `validatePoolUpdate` reads the runtime unconditionally, so `PUT` and `warm-count` answer `400`. Applied, and `runtimeRestartable` added. |
| Minor 4, the DF-4 test and claim | Holds. | §13.1 states the concurrent-pod read. Test scoped, and DR-3's claim limited. |
| Minor 5, prefix grammar | Holds. Fix widened. | A trailing `-` also collides: prefix `a-` with suffix `x`, and prefix `a` with suffix `-x`, both give `a---x`. |
| Minor 6, erasure frees a prefix | Holds. | `consumed_name_prefixes` added. |
| Minor 7, template retirement and `DELETE` order | Holds. | A template is held by its grant entries and retires through a grant write, which needs no new mechanism. `DELETE` checks first, and a tenant `drain` route is added. |
| Minor 8, a failed scan loses the event | Holds. | Outbox row added. |
| Minor 9, tenant suspension and deletion | Holds. | Rules added, reusing `ForceZeroMinWarm`. |
| Minor 10, the owner on `SandboxTemplate` | Holds. | Added. |
| DF-4 rating, DR-3's limit, and the false comments | Hold. | `setup.go` and `pkg/embedded/stack/runtimes.go` say what was reported. Recorded in DF-2 and DF-4. |

| :-- | :-- | :-- |

The guard takes the policy's tenant and reads the tenant view, which is granted platform rows plus that tenant's rows. Reading all platform rows, as proposed, would still let an ungranted platform runtime block a delete and disclose its name, so that is filed as DF-13. |

| Minor 3, `checkVariantIsolation` | Holds. | Applied. |
| Minor 4, `PATCH` | Holds. | Applied. It also corrects DR-1's claim that `PATCH` names a pool. |
| Minor 5, DF-9's routing evidence | Holds. | Corrected, and `variant.Runtime` is bounded at write. DF-9's rating stands, because it rests on the controller's effects. With `variant.Runtime` alone, a `tenant-admin` keeps a working experiment before DR-5, which lowers decision 13's cost. |

Fix smaller. | `retireAt` on the grant entry carries it, and the emergency form uses the same `PUT`. `DELETE .../tenant-access/{tenantId}` answers `409` while an entry is live, in place of a new route form and a date column on the access table. |

| Minor 11, `type` and `deploymentModel` | Holds. Fix smaller. | `applyCRDFields` mirrors no `deploymentModel`. The RuntimeReconciler mirrors it, so a resolve needs no read of the `Runtime` resource. |
| Minor 12, commands re-run on rebuild | Holds. | §7.3 step 3e says so. Recorded as a residual, because filtering needs a per-command source on the stored plan. The false sentence is removed. |
| Minor 13, rate-limit order, `bootstrapMinWarm`, and the recycling test | Holds. | `requireAdmissionRateLimit` runs first in `create.go`, so the check moves ahead of it. `bootstrapMinWarm` is refused, and the recycling test and its rule state the reserved hold. |

An independent review read revision 8 and found no path across tenants. Each finding below was checked against the text and the tree. None was rejected, and the "Result" column states where the fix differs from the one proposed. | Finding | Result | Check and outcome |

| High, a stale target defeats the floor | Holds. | `defaultSyncInterval` is 30 seconds, and `plan.Compute` counts a warming pod and drains only idle ones. The target moves to the WarmPoolController, with the two rules under "Which pods are reserved". |
| High, the quota runs out early | Holds. Fix changed. | `applyResources` stamps every container. The reserved quota limits `pods` alone, so a sizing error cannot refuse a reserved pod. |
| High, a raised priority changes eviction | Holds. | The class value is 0. |
| High, `.git` in a clone's target | Holds. | `validatePath` has no such check. Refused for defaults, and the egress design now requires a new directory with hooks and repository configuration ignored. |
| Major, `auth`-less clones | Holds. | `checkGitCloneAuthBindings` skips them. Checks now key on the pool prefix. |
| Major, refusals name the repository | Holds. | `writeRefResolveError` returns `url`. A separate writer applies, and the pinned-commit failure is stated. |
| Major, switching off breaks pods | Holds. | The controller reads the same value, so stored floors stop applying. |
| Major, the floor ignores other paths | Holds. | The order and the `active` definition are stated. |
| Major, lowering `maxOwnedPods` | Holds. | Allowances are bounded across the grant and lowered first. |
| Major, the marking race and overrun | Holds. | "No race" is withdrawn, and grant writes count overrun. |
| Major, placement and namespaces | Holds. Fix narrowed. | Reservations stay in the one namespace the controller manages and leave Kata out. The field is renamed. |
| Minors, wording, derive and replay, unsupported pods, and the other documents | Hold. | Applied. |

A stop check then read revision 15. It found no Critical issue, one High, and six Majors, and no path across tenants. Its statements about Kubernetes and git came from memory. Each was checked against the tree, and most came from warm clones on concurrent pods and from one generation value both refusing and rolling pods, so revision 16 removes those two causes where the review proposed patches.

| High, the generation refusal misses occupied and reserved pods | Holds. Fix changed. | `slotclaimer.go` Pass 1 and both rebinds skip `AdmitTenantPin`. `warm` now needs a one-session pool, so Pass 1 has no free slot, and a reserved pod was checked at create. |
| Major, a stored writable entry survives a template raise | Holds. | An owned pool with a warm or writable clone no longer takes a raise, which also removes the read-as-`none` rule. |
| Major, a derived session resumed before its first checkpoint skips the table | Holds. Exists today. | This is F-7.3.36, already filed. DR-9 now depends on its first suggested route, and the entry names this. |
| Major, new sessions stop on a concurrent pool at budget | Holds. Removed. | Concurrent pools no longer hold stores. |
| Major, a generation move leaves no claimable pod | Holds. Fix changed. | The claim compares `coveredStores` with what the pod holds, so only a removal refuses, and the generation only rolls idle pods. |
| Major, gaps in the derive table | Holds. | The table is keyed on `path`, a kept tree stays kept through a chain of derives, an overlap refuses, and a dropped repository travels as a marker source, which also gives it read tokens under `required`. |
| Major, the size listing fails on a hostile tree | Holds. | The listing streams with a per-entry charge, an entry cap, a time limit, and a total across the plan. |
| Minors: the budget on Helm lowering, carriers, base stores, and the resume error writer | Hold. | Applied. A grant check runs only on a raise, "Stored formats" lists every field, a base with a private store takes no warm entry, and a resume records its reason. |

A stop check then read revision 16. It found no Critical issue, one High, and four Majors, no path across tenants, and no route to the adapter's identity. It read no upstream Kubernetes or git source. Each was checked against the tree, and revision 17 applies them.

| Finding | Result | Check and outcome |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| High, a recycled pod skips `coveredStores` | Holds. Introduced by revision 16's fix, which misread `reserved`. | `claimer.go` runs `rebindReserved` before any admission check on a pool where `ReusesPods` is true. `warm` and `write` now need `ReusesPods` false. |
| Major, the hold at concurrency 1 can be evaded | Holds. Fix changed. | `ClaimSlot`'s first pass takes any pod with a live claim. The reviewer proposed computing the value at resolve, which would add a merge to the readers "Template copy" keeps from one, so a recompute under the row's lock replaces it, and `Sandbox.spec.reusesPods` stops the first pass. |
| Major, the age bound fails when access is lost | Holds. | The guard on the warm target goes, so a failing refill does not stop the drain. |
| Major, the derive table reads the wrong plan | Holds. | A source with no checkpoint of its own passes its entries through, and `snapshotIsOwn` also serves F-7.3.36. |
| Major, losing the store can make a restore fail | Holds. | 0111 accepts this as an edge case, and a tenant's edit makes it routine. Restore fetches full history under the fetch cap. |
| Minors: a delegated child's `coveredStores`, drain against roll, and roll order | Hold. Fix wider. | `coveredStores` comes from the plan. The list generation and the rolling replacement go entirely, which removes the second and third, and with no refill on a list change, the change interval goes too. |

A stop check then read revision 17. It found no Critical or High issue, four Majors, no path across tenants, and no route to the adapter's identity. It read no upstream source. Each was checked against the tree, and revision 18 applies them.

| Major, full-history restore collides with the workspace limit | Holds. Introduced by revision 17's fix. | §4.4 sets the slot limit, and 0111's rule 6 fails above it. Restore fetches shallow first and fetches history only for a missing object, within the remaining bytes. |
| Major, `coveredStores` from the plan breaks resume | Holds. Introduced by revision 17's fix. | The set now includes the clones a resume checked, and a create with no plan reads the routed runtime. |
| Major, a stuck `warming` Sandbox stalls the drain and the fill limit | Holds. | DR-10 states that `createPod` retries a refused pod. Both limits count pods running a fill, and the drain is not serial. |

The owner then merged derive and replay, so revision 28 replaces the derive text those rounds reviewed, along with `keepRestored`, `restoredDefaults`, `restoredSnapshotIsOwn`, and `session-replay-design.md`, and no review had read it. ### Review of revision 31

An independent review read revision 31 in full. It found no Critical or High issue, two Majors, and no path across tenants. Each was checked against the tree, and revision 32 applies them.

| Finding | Result | Check and outcome |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Major, `true` to `false` is unguarded | Holds. Inherited from the field-by-field rule. | `PUT` takes the same body, `poolPolicyMirror` reads the stored policy, and `ClaimSlot`'s first pass takes any live same-tenant claim. A session on a pod could then gain a neighbour with the union writable set. The field is one-way, an absent field is unchanged, and `reuseLocked` folds into it. |
| Major, a template widening escapes a forbid | Holds. | Suspension fires only on a violating write or a tightening. The copy transaction sets the field on a matching row. The reviewer also offered suspension, which would stop a tenant's runtime for a platform write. `recycle` and `concurrency` now state what they match. |
| Minors: a `true` row's `recycle` block, the copy source of cleanup fields, the scope of a template widening over limits, the `warm` `Warning`, a silently ineffective `false`, and the carriers | Hold. | Applied. A refused `PUT` closes the ineffective `false`. |

### Review of revision 32

A stop check read revision 32's changes. It found no Critical or High issue, three Majors, all in revision 32's forbid rule, and no path across tenants. Each was checked against the tree, and revision 33 applies them.

| Finding | Result | Check and outcome |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Major, the forbid read fails open | Holds. Introduced by revision 32. | `poolstore`'s update carries no tenant, and row security returns no rows and no error. The read runs under `__all__` with its audit event, and `InAllTenants` gains a variant on an open transaction. |
| Major, the lock order inverts | Holds. Introduced by revision 32. | SO-2's writer holds the record and then reaches rows, and its check reads under a shared lock. The copy locks rows and reads with no lock on the record. A tightening applies in its write's transaction, so "activation" goes. |
| Major, suspended rows and "either order" | Holds. Introduced by revision 32. | When the copy commits first, the tightening suspends the row. The field is set only on a transition into a match on an unsuspended row, so `all` and a later unrelated write set nothing. |
| Minors: `ReusesPods` as the `recycle` match, a clone write on a `true` row, and a zero limit | Hold. | Applied. |

### Review of revision 33

A narrow stop check read revision 33's forbid rule. It found no Critical or High issue and two Majors. Each was checked against the design, and revision 34 applies them.

| Finding | Result | Check and outcome |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Major, a tightening can miss a row the copy widens | Holds. Introduced by revision 33. | SO-2 marks non-compliant objects and locks no compliant row, so a copy between its read and its commit leaves a recycling row with no mark. A tightening now locks the tenant's owned rows in name order before evaluating them. |
| Major, clearing a mark can release a row another value matches | Holds. | SO-2 clears the mark a removed rule set. A removal now re-evaluates against the remaining values, which `security-oversight-design.md` revision 24 states. |
| Minor, `suspended_by` has row security | Holds. | The `__all__` read covers it. |

### Review of revision 34

A final check on revision 34's forbid rule found no Critical or High issue and two Majors: a tenant's compliant `PUT` could clear a mark while a template copy read it, and a platform `PUT` on an owned pool had no forbid rule at all. Both held. Three rounds had each found new interleavings, because revisions 32 to 34 made every writer enforce the forbid. Revision 35 stops patching that rule. `recycle` and `concurrency` now apply at resolve, reading `incompatible`, as a grant or base change already does, and a platform `PUT` refuses the pool's concurrency, recycling, and cleanup fields on an owned pool. This removes the cross-tenant read, the lock rules, the marks for these values, and the automatic setting of the field. The cost is that a platform widening stops the runtime of a tenant holding such a forbid until it acts, which revision 32 had rejected.

### Review of revision 35

A narrow check on revision 35 found no Critical or High issue and two Majors. A platform `PUT` could set `executionMode: service` on an owned pool, and a resume skips the tenant view and can be placed on a pod another user held. Both held against `validatePoolUpdate` and `resumeOnPod`. The second showed that a check at resolve has to reach every placement path. Revision 36 removes the need for either rule: `reusedPods` requires `oneSessionPerPod: true`, which no template write can change, so compliance reads one stored field and no writer or placement path interacts with it. A non-compliant row is suspended by SO-2's existing mechanism and its pool drains. The platform `PUT` refuses every field taken from the base or the template. The cost is that a tenant under `reusedPods` sets the field on a runtime whose template happens not to reuse pods today.

| M2, default commands run in the client's folder | Holds. | A command's `cwd` defaults by its `origin`. |
| M2b, SDK-warm | Holds. `ConfigureWorkspace` carries `cwd`, and the blocking paths match `current/` alone. | The check runs before `ConfigureWorkspace`, and the patterns also match the working directory. |
| M3, a tenant extends how long revoked content stays readable | Holds. The stated reason was wrong. | The Helm maximum defaults to 86400, so a tenant can only shorten, and the reason is corrected. |

| m2, resume | Holds. | The row and `expected_workspace_root` keep the slot root, the fallback covers a `from: latest` derive, and a directory-keyed SDK resume is stated. |
